3
FOREWORD
Paris, January 1, 2026
The Defence Industry Directorate (DID), initiated as part of the French defence procurement 
agency (DGA) ‘Impulsion’ project, has now been in operation for over a year.   
Within the DGA, the DID guarantees the continuity, quality, performance and resilience of French 
defence companies. It plays an essential role in preserving France’s strategic autonomy and 
promoting competitiveness in the defence industry.
It supports French defence companies, whether large groups, mid-sized companies or SMEs, in 
their development and industrial strategies, and helps them to self-protect, innovate and invest to 
meet the needs of the armed forces. It also supports them in their export efforts, advising them 
and facilitating their dealings with the DGA’s International Cooperation and Export Directorate 
(DICE).
The DID ensures that France has the sovereign industrial and technological capabilities needed 
to respond to the geopolitical situation and achieve the desired level of strategic autonomy, both 
now and in the long term. It encourages partnerships between the French State, local authorities, 
companies, research laboratories and the armed forces. It also ensures that these principles are 
applied at the European level, through partnerships, negotiations on EU industrial policies and 
dialogue with our European counterparts.
This new 2026 edition of the International Defence Companies notebook is published in light of 
these issues.
This publication provides key data from publicly available sources, for a selection of international 
companies chosen each year according to their main activities in the defence sector.
In this regard, Anduril, Czechoslovak Group (CSG), Delair, FN Browning Group, Hemeria, 
Sabena Technics and UkrOboronProm have been included for the first time in this 2026 edition 
while Diehl Defence, FNSS, Israel Aerospace Industries (IAI), Kawasaki Heavy Industries (KHI) 
andt Rafael have been re-introduced.
The notebook should in no way be considered as reflecting the opinions or positions of the DGA, 
nor of Xerfi Specific, which contributed to its preparation.
A digital version of the notebook, both in French and English, is available on the armament 
website at www.armement.defense.gouv.fr.
I wish you a pleasant reading.
Ingénieur en chef des mines 
Benoit Laroche de Roussane
Director, Defence Industry Directorate, DGA

SCOPE
The 2026 edition of the Notebook on International Defence Companies covers a panel of 70 companies. 
A specific section, “Key Data”, presents the following consolidated financial indicators for each company,
provided such information is available (“N/A” meaning that data is not available):
•	
Revenue (in euros)
•	
Revenue variation (as a percentage change; calculated from data in local currency)
•	
Defence revenue (as a % of total revenue)
•	
Export revenue (as a % of total revenue)
•	
Operating profit/loss (in euros)
•	
Operating margin (as a %)
•	
Net income (in euros) or net income attributable to shareholders
•	
Order book at the end of the fiscal year (in euros) 
•	
Headcount
To ensure consistency, consolidated financial statements were the preferred source of information. 
Emphasis was placed on the most recent, restated financial indicators. Furthermore, some data 
contained in the previous edition have been revised, corrected or modified for this edition in order to take 
into account more recent currency exchange rates (see below).
Currency conversions were calculated using the annual average exchange rate (source : European 
Central Bank) during each company’s fiscal year. However, order book values were converted using 
the exchange rates at the closing date of each company’s fiscal year. Average annual exchange rates 
used for conversions are presented in the table below. It should be noted that all financial indicators 
were converted using the average exchange rate for their corresponding year. To avoid a bias when 
calculating annual changes of monetary indicators, variations (as a percentage change) were calculated 
in the original currency used in each company’s financial statement.
SOURCES
The defence industry ranking is based on data from the Defense News magazine 2024 top 100 list.
For publicly listed groups or those that publish annual statements of consolidated accounts, the following 
sources were used:
	•
Annual reports and reference documents
	•
Consolidated balance sheets and profit & loss statements
	•
Financial and investor presentations
For unlisted groups or those that do not publish annual statements of consolidated accounts, the 
following sources were used:
	•
Press releases, company presentations
	•
Data bases: Factiva, Diane
	•
Business press and specialist publications
List of companies that do not publish, or only partially publish, financial statements:

OWNERSHIP
The “Ownership structure” section draws data from the folllowing sources:
	•
Company publications (websites, reference documents, investor presentations)
	•
Brokerage firms and major trading platforms (Bolsa de Madrid, Borsa Italiana, Deutsche Börse, 
Euronext, LSE, NASDAQ, NYSE, Tokyo Stock Exchange, etc).
CHARTS 
Charts represent the sales breakdown by business segment (i.e. branches or divisions as presented by 
the company) and geographical region. 
MAIN PROGRAMMES, COOPERATIONS AND EXPORT PARTNERSHIPS
The “Main programmes, cooperations and export partnerships” section presents the main programmes 
highlighted by companies in their public media releases (excluding upstream study programmes). 
Partnerships for each programme are also listed, without further details. These partnerships can 
take several forms: co-contractorship, co-development or co-production agreements, subcontracting 
agreements (for tier-1 equipment suppliers considered as “strategic partners”), marketing agreements, 
joint ventures (JV), or partnerships with foreign companies as part of offset agreements.
Due to the very concise format of the notebook, this section cannot claim to be exhaustive.

12
STRATEGIC HIGHLIGHTS
AIRBUS
Business trends
	
 Airbus revenues went up by 5.8% in 2024 to reach €69.2 billion, mainly due to the increase in commercial 
aircraft deliveries (766 in 2024, compared to 735 in 2023). The sales of the group’s two defence segments also 
grew (+5.1% for Airbus Defence and Space and +8.2% for Airbus Helicopters).
	
 In December 2024, the group announced plans to cut 2,043 jobs within its Airbus Defence and Space division 
by mid-2026, including 540 jobs in France at Élancourt (116 positions) and Toulouse (424 positions), 689 in 
Germany, 477 in the United Kingdom and 303 in Spain. More than half of these job cuts relate to space activities.
Home markets (France, Germany, Spain, United Kingdom)
	
 Airbus’ offer of the A321 MPA was selected by the French Ministry of Armed Forces in November 2024 for the 
French Patmar programme, intented to procure a maritime patrol aircraft meant to replace the 22 Atlantique 2 
(Bréguet) aircraft in service from 2035 onwards. The A321 MPA is based on the A321XLR passenger aircraft.
	
 The Spanish government signed a €4 billion contract with the NATO Eurofighter and Tornado Management 
Agency (NETMA) in December 2024 for the acquisition of 25 Typhoon fighter aircraft (Halcón II programme). 
These aircraft will be assembled and tested at the group’s Spanish site in Getafe.
	
 In January 2025, Airbus delivered to the French defence procurement agency (DGA) the first two of eight 
H225M Caracal heavy transport helicopters ordered in 2021. These helicopters are equipped with the Euroflir 
410 optronic system, Safran’s Sigma inertial navigation system and Thales’ TRA6034 communication system.
	
 In February 2025, the French defence procurement agency (DGA) awarded a contract worth up to 
€480 million over 10 years to Airbus and Naval Group to develop and integrate the French Navy’s RIFAN 3 
(Réseau IP de la Force Navale) communication network. More than 80 ships will be equipped with this future 
network between 2028 and 2032.
	
 The first two NH90 Standard 3 multirole helicopters, which feature a modernised secure communications 
system, were delivered to the Spanish Navy in May 2025. A total of 23 NH90 Standard 3 helicopters are to be 
delivered to the Spanish Armed Forces until 2028, including 7 to the Navy, 10 to the Army and 6 to the Air Force.
	
 In June 2025, the DGA awarded Airbus and Neverhack an eight-year framework contract for cyber defence 
training aimed at all cybersecurity experts in the French Ministry of Armed Forces.
	
 The French Ministry of Armed Forces ordered a first batch of six VSR700 shipborne helicopter drones from 
Airbus and Naval Group in June 2025. The drones are to be deployed on four FREMM frigates and two FDI 
frigates of the French Navy.
Export markets
	
 In December 2024, the group completed the modification of the Swedish Armed Forces’ fourth and final NH90 
multirole helicopter, converting a standard transport helicopter (Hkp 14E) into an anti-submarine warfare 
helicopter equipped with sonar adapted to the waters of the Baltic Sea (Hkp 14F).
	
 The NATO Support and Procurement Agency (NSPA) ordered two A330 MRTT refuelling and transport aircraft 
in June 2025. The aircraft are to be delivered in 2028 and 2029 and will bring the NSPA’s fleet to 12 units. 
These aircraft are available to eight European countries, including Denmark and Sweden.
	
 In August 2025, Airbus delivered the 16th and final C295 tactical transport aircraft produced in Seville (Spain) 
to the Indian Air Force. Another 40 aircraft are to be be assembled in India by Tata Advanced Systems Ltd 
(TASL) by August 2031.
Technology and Innovation
	
 The group’s self-financed R&D expenditure amounted to €3.3 billion in 2024, representing 4.7% of revenues.

15
STRATEGIC HIGHLIGHTS
ANDURIL
Business trends
	
 Anduril posted revenues of approximately $1 billion (€924 million) in 2024, compared to approximately 
$420 million (€389 million) in 2023. Growth was driven by numerous contracts signed in previous years, in 
particular for the installation of Sentry surveillance towers at U.S. borders to automatically detect illegal entries 
into the country and for the supply of anti-drone systems to the U.S. Armed Forces, as well as by the progress of 
the Ghost Shark programme (large autonomous submarines for the Royal Australian Navy). Acquisitions made 
during the previous financial year also supported growth.
	
 In January 2025, the group acquired the compact radar and C2 systems business of the American company 
Numerica Corporation. Anduril thus added to its portfolio the Spark and Spyglass radars, designed for short-
range air defence and vehicle protection missions, and the Mimir C2 software.
	
 In January 2025, the group also initiated a $1 billion (€942 million) investment in the construction of a 465,000 m² 
factory in Columbus, Ohio, called Arsenal-1, to produce autonomous systems and weapons on a large scale 
for the United States and allied countries. The factory is expected to be operational in July 2026 and could 
employ up to 4,000 people by 2035.
	
 Anduril raised $2.5 billion (€2.3 billion) in a Series G round in June 2025, doubling its valuation to $30.5 billion 
(€28.2 billion). The group could go public in 2026.
	
 At the Paris Air Show in Le Bourget in June 2025, Anduril announced a strategic partnership to produce 
Barracuda cruise missiles and Fury UCAVs at Rheinmetall’s factories.
Home market
	
 The group is mainly active in its domestic market, with numerous ongoing contracts with U.S. agencies such as 
Customs and Border Protection (CBP), the Defence Innovation Unit (DIU) and Special Operations Command 
(SOCOM).
	
 In February 2025, Microsoft transferred management of the Soldier Borne Mission Command (SBMC, formerly 
IVAS) programme to Anduril. The programme aims to equip U.S. Army soldiers with helmets incorporating 
augmented reality and virtual reality to improve situational awareness and facilitate the operation of UAVs. 
Anduril will partner with several subcontractors such as L3Harris Technologies (ENVG-B night vision systems), 
Meta (virtual reality solutions) and Microsoft (Azure hyperscale cloud solution). This programme has a budget 
of $21.9 billion (€20.2 billion).
	
 In March 2025, Anduril signed a 10-year, $642 million (€593 million) contract with the U.S. Marine Corps to 
supply, install and support anti-drone systems designed to protect facilities around the world.
Export markets
	
 Saab selected Anduril in June 2025 to develop and produce a new propulsion system for the Ground-
Launched Small Diameter Bomb (GLSDB), produced by Saab in partnership with Boeing.
	
 The Royal Australian Navy awarded Anduril a contract worth A$ 1.7 billion (€1 billion) in September 2025 to 
produce Ghost Shark large autonomous underwater vehicles, based on the Dive-LD.
Technology and Innovation
	
 In October 2025, Anduril conducted several flight tests of its Barracuda-100M manoeuvrable missile as 
part of the U.S. Army’s High-Speed Maneuverable Missile (HSMM) programme. This missile will be able to 
autonomously search for, identy and strike targets it was previously shown.

18
ARIANEGROUP
Business trends
	
 ArianeGroup’s revenues recovered in 2024 to reach €2.5 billion (+7.7% year-on-year). The year was marked 
by the successful maiden flight of Ariane 6 in July 2024, which contributed to the group’s return to profit. 
Arianespace also carried out two launches on Vega/Vega C during the year. Defence activities related to 
oceanic deterrence (M51 missile) also performed well.
	
 At the end of 2024, Arianespace had a backlog of around 30 launches for Ariane 6, including commercial 
flights (18 of which to launch over 600 satellites in Amazon’s Kuiper constellation) and institutional flights 
(notably for the launch of Galileo satellites and the European PLATO telescope, scheduled for the end of 
2026). From 2029, Arianespace is also set to participate in the deployment of the European IRIS² constellation, 
comprising nearly 300 satellites.
	
 Ariane 6 began commercial flights in 2025 and successfully completed four launches during the year (in 
March, August, November and December), launching the French military satellite CSO-3, the Metop-SGA1 
meteorological satellite, the Sentinel-1D satellite from the Copernicus programme and two satellites from the 
Galileo programme, respectively. The next launch is scheduled for early 2026 for Amazon using the four-
booster version of the launcher (Ariane 64). The aim is to reach a rate of around ten missions per year from 2027 
onwards (roughly double that of Ariane 5).
	
 Vega C successfully completed two flights in April and July 2025. Following an agreement signed in July 2024 
with Avio, Arianespace has responsibility for the launcher’s next two flights.
	
 The MaiaSpace subsidiary is developing its reusable mini-launcher Maia, which is scheduled to enter 
commercial operation in 2026. In June 2025, the company announced the construction of a 10,000 m² factory 
in Vernon (in northern France), near the ArianeGroup site, where 160 jobs will be created.
Home market
	
 In March 2025, MaiaSpace signed its first commercial contract with French start-up Exotrail, a specialist in 
space logistics, for several launches starting in 2027.
	
 In June 2025, the French Space Agency (CNES) awarded ArianeGroup a contract for preliminary work on a 
new-generation engine using liquid oxygen and methane, twice as powerful as the Vulcain 2.1 engine with a 
thrust of between 200 and 300 tonnes.
	
 In August 2025, the French defence procurement agency (DGA) awarded ArianeGroup the contract to 
develop and produce the fourth version of the M51 strategic ballistic missile. The M51.4 missile will offer 
increased performance in terms of range, accuracy and penetration capabilities. The group is also preparing 
to bring the M51.3 version of the missile into service.
	
 In September 2025, Arianespace signed a contract with Airbus Defence and Space to launch the two future 
German secure military communications satellites SATCOMBw 3 aboard two Ariane 6 flights.
Technology and Innovation
	
 At the Paris Air Show in June 2025, ArianeGroup presented a concept for a hypersonic deep strike weapon. 
The ballistic missile could aim at a target more than 1,000 km away and fly at around 20,000 km/h (Mach 16). 
Drawing on expertise gained from other programmes such as the M51 missile, the group plans to develop this 
new weapon within a few years.
	
 A test of the reusable Prometheus cryogenic engine, involving several successive starts, was carried out in 
June 2025 on the test bench in Vernon. Testing of the reusable first stage prototype, Themis, also began in 
September 2025 at a launch pad at the Esrange Space Centre (Sweden). An initial low-altitude flight and 
recovery test will then be conducted as part of the European SALTO programme.
	
 ArianeGroup signed a cooperation agreement with the German Aerospace Centre (DLR) in September 2025. 
Research will focus on space technologies for defence, including advanced materials and technologies and 
space surveillance systems.
STRATEGIC HIGHLIGHTS

21
Business trends
	
 Atos generated revenues of €9.6 billion in 2024, down 10.4% year-on-year (-5.4% on a like-for-like basis and 
at constant exchange rates). The Eviden division (-9.5%) was weighed down by the Digital segment (contract 
terminations) and the Big Data & Security segment (decline in cybersecurity activity). Only the Advanced 
Computing segment recorded growth, with project deliveries in Denmark and Germany. The Tech Foundations 
division (-11.3%) suffered from customer withdrawals and a decline in demand for services. However, after 
several years of losses, the group’s net income returned to positive territory in 2024.
	
 Atos completed its financial restructuring programme at the end of 2024, including €1.6 billion in new financing 
and the sale of its Worldgrid subsidiary for €270 million. The group now has no debt maturities before the end 
of 2029.
	
 In May 2025, Atos presented a new strategic and transformation plan called Genesis, aimed at reviving 
revenue growth (with revenues expected to reach between €9 billion and €10 billion by 2028), and improving 
profitability with an operating margin of around 10%.
	
 In June 2025, Atos accepted a €410 million offer from the French State Holdings Agency (APE) to acquire 
its Advanced Computing segment (Eviden division), which includes supercomputers, quantum computing 
activities, advanced cybersecurity solutions and certain critical skills in embedded AI. This acquisition aims to 
prevent foreign control over these sensitive technologies, which are essential for nuclear deterrence, military 
simulations and strategic research. However, contrary to initial plans, the Mission-Critical Systems (MCS) 
activities were not sold and remain within Atos’ scope. The transaction is expected to be finalised in 2026.
Home market
	
 In addition to supercomputers and advanced computing, Atos’ defence activities include the provision 
of command systems (SCORPION programme), secure communications (Auxylium, Rafale F4) and C4I 
infrastructure for the Armed Forces through its MCS segment. The group also offers cybersecurity and 
sovereign encryption solutions.
	
 In December 2024, the French Space Agency (CNES) tasked Atos with upgrading its LVCUGEN flight 
software for satellites and spacecraft and to maintain it for five years.
	
 In March 2025, Atos partnered with Esri France to offer solutions combining digital twins and geolocated data 
for various sectors, including defence and security. The aim is to provide immersive simulation and planning 
tools with high added value, particularly for operational training, crisis management and cybersecurity.
Export markets
	
 In November 2024, Atos signed a €165 million contract extension with Eurocontrol, a pan-European civil and 
military organisation, to continue providing critical infrastructure monitoring and cybersecurity services for 
airspace and aviation management in Europe.
	
 Through Eviden, Atos signed a €50 million contract in Serbia in April 2025 to develop the country’s artificial 
intelligence capabilities. The group will provide various AI services and capabilities, including a centre of 
excellence and a high-performance computing platform.
	
 In September 2025, Atos secured a contract worth up to €326 million over four years to provide cybersecurity 
services to various European Union bodies, agencies and institutions. The group will partner with Leonardo 
on this programme.
Technology and Innovation
	
 As part of its new strategic plan, Atos plans to invest €500 million in R&D over the next four years. R&D work 
will focus on emerging technologies and next-generation solutions (generative AI, agentic AI, cybersecurity, 
quantum computing, etc.).
STRATEGIC HIGHLIGHTS

24
AUSTAL
STRATEGIC HIGHLIGHTS
Business trends
	
 Austal posted revenues of A$ 1,823 million (€1,086 million) during fiscal year 2025 (ended June 30th), up 
24.1% year-on-year in local currency. Growth was driven by shipbuilding activities, whereas revenues from 
maintenance and support services grew at a slower pace. Austal delivered six naval vessels during the fiscal 
year: three Guardian-class and two Evolved Cape-class patrol boats by Austal Australia, and one EPF vessel 
by Austal USA.
	
 In the United States, construction work is ongoing at the shipyard in Mobile, Alabama to expand capacity, 
particularly to produce submarine equipment. The creation of more than 2,000 jobs is expected.
	
 Already holding a 10% stake in Austal, South Korean group Hanwha intends to increase its stake to 19.9%. 
The transaction will be subject to approval by the Australian and U.S. authorities.
Home market
	
 In Australia, the group is a long-standing supplier of patrol boats (Cape and Guardian programmes) to the 
Australian Border Force and the Royal Australian Navy. In December 2024, the Australian government ordered 
two additional Evolved Cape-class patrol boats for A$ 137 million (€83 million), bringing the programme to 
12 units. The ninth Evolved Cape-class ship was delivered in September 2025 and two Guardian-class patrol 
boats are under construction.
	
 The agreement committed to at the end of 2023 with the Australian government, known as the SSA (Strategic 
Shipbuilding Agreement), was officially signed in August 2025. It secures the group’s participation in future 
federal naval defence programmes, which include the supply of 26 landing craft, of which 18 LC-Ms (Landing 
Craft-Medium) and 8 LC-Hs (Landing Craft-Heavy), and 6 naval drones (Large Optionally Crewed Vessels). 
Australia also selected Mitsubishi Heavy Industries (over tkMS) for its programme to acquire 11 multi-purpose 
frigates (Mogami-class), 8 of which will be built locally by Austal.
Export markets
	
 Austal generated 78% of its revenues from exports during fiscal year 2025. The United States is by far the 
group’s largest market, as a major supplier to the U.S. Navy and U.S. Coast Guard. Austal USA delivered the 
last Independence-class LCS frigate to the U.S. Navy in July 2025 and will continue to provide maintenance 
and support services for the 19 ships in the fleet.
	
 The U.S. Navy’s EPF transport ship programme is also nearing completion, with the last unit currently under 
construction. It will be followed by the EMS (Expeditionary Medical Ship) programme, comprising three units 
with enhanced medical capabilities. Their construction is scheduled to begin during the 2026 fiscal year.
	
 Austal USA launched the first Navajo-class T-ATS ship in June 2025. Faced with losses on this programme, the 
first one requiring the production of ships made entirely of steel, the group reached an agreement in October 
2025 with the U.S. Navy to supply only three ships (already under construction) out of the five originally planned.
	
 Austal USA launched the first LCU 1700-class craft in August 2025. The programme could include up to 
12 units, three of which are under construction.
	
 In the summer of 2025, the U.S. Coast Guard exercised two options for the Heritage-class Offshore Patrol 
Cutter (OPC) programme, worth a total of $587 million (€502 million) for the construction of the second vessel 
and initial work on the production of the next four units (numbered 3 to 6). The programme could include up to 
11 patrol boats, for a potential total of $3.3 billion (€3.1 billion).
	
 Austal USA could also produce up to seven T-AGOS Ocean Surveillance ships for the U.S. Navy. Delivery of 
the first unit is scheduled for 2031.
Technology and Innovation
	
 Austal’s main areas of innovation focus on naval drones, additive manufacturing (including through the 
U.S. Navy’s centre of excellence in Danville, Virginia) and the digitisation of production processes.

27
Business trends
	
 AVIC, which includes Chengdu, Shenyang, Guizhou, Hongdu, Xi’an and Shaanxi aircraft manufacturers, 
as well as Harbin and Changhe helicopter manufacturers, recorded revenues of $81.3 billion (€75.1 billion) 
in 2024, down 2.6% year-on-year in US dollars. Over the year, the group delivered nearly 100 Chengdu 
fifth-generation J-20 fighter aircraft to the Chinese Air Force, bringing the number of these aircraft in service 
to more than 250.
	
 AVIC has been an Airbus partner since 1985 on several programmes, including the assembly of the A320 
passenger aircraft in China and the local production under licence of the AC352 (H175) civil helicopter and the 
Z-9 (AS365 Dauphin) military helicopter. Moreover, some of AVIC’s military aircraft (J-16, J-15 and JL-10) are 
derived from platforms designed by Russian group United Aircraft Corporation (UAC).
	
 The group has also set up several joint ventures, both with foreign players such as Textron (Cessna-AVIC 
Aircraft, sales and maintenance of business jets) and with Chinese players through the COMAC joint venture 
to produce the short-haul C909 and medium-haul C919 aircraft, powered by CFM International’s LEAP-1C 
engine (GE Aerospace/Safran), and to develop the C929 and C939 long-haul aircraft.
Home market
	
 AVIC generates a large part of its turnover on its domestic market. As the leading company in the Chinese 
aeronautics sector, the group is directly or indirectly involved in most of the country’s military aircraft 
programmes.	
	
 Two modernised versions of the Shenyang J-15 carrier-based fighter aircraft entered service with the 
Chinese Navy in October 2024: the single-seat J-15T, equipped with a catapult launch capability (CATOBAR), 
and the two-seat J-15D, dedicated to electronic warfare.
	
 Xi’an is developing the future H-20 stealth strategic bomber. Flight tests reportedly began in late 2024 and the 
aircraft is expected to enter service in the 2030s.
	
 The group aims to deliver 120 Chengdu J-20 fighter aircraft to the Chinese Air Force in 2025 and 2026.
Export markets
	
 The group generates about 20% of its turnover form exports. Africa, the Middle East and Eastern European 
countries are AVIC’s main target markets.
	
 AVIC  delivered several aircraft to export customers in 2024: an unspecified number of Chengdu J-10C fighter 
aircraft to the Pakistan Air Force and six Guizhou FTC-2000G trainer/light attack aircraft to the Myanmar Air 
Force in August, and two Shaanxi Y-9 transport aircraft to Namibia in December.
	
 In June 2025, Pakistan Aeronautical Complex signed a $4.6 billion (€4.3 billion) contract to supply 40 JF-17 
Block 3 fighter aircraft to Azerbaijan. Chengdu is an aerostructures supplier for the aircraft, representing 
approximately 42% of the airframe, with final assembly being carried out in Pakistan.
	
 The Changhe Z-10ME attack helicopter entered service with the Pakistan Army in August 2025. This helicopter 
was ordered in 2023, following the United States’ ban on Turkey selling Pakistan the T129 ATAK helicopter 
(Turkish Aerospace), powered by an American turboshaft engine.
Technology and Innovation
	
 Two of the group’s (potentially) sixth-generation fighter aircraft underwent flight testing in December 2024: the 
Chengdu J-36, a multirole trijet (air superiority, attack fighter, interception and C2) with double delta wings, and 
the Shenyang J-50, a twin-jet with lambda wings.
	
 In December 2024, Xi’an also conducted flight tests on a detection aircraft called the KJ-3000, whose airframe 
is based on the Y-20B strategic transport aircraft.
STRATEGIC HIGHLIGHTS
AVIATION INDUSTRY CORPORATION OF CHINA (AVIC)

30
AVIO
Business trends
	
 Avio revenues increased 30.4% in 2024 to €442 million. Growth was driven by the Vega and Ariane launchers 
divisions, thanks to the successful maiden flight of Ariane 6, the return to flight of Vega C and the development 
of Vega E, and the Defence propulsion division. Revenues from technology development projects funded by 
the Next Gen EU plan almost doubled.
	
 The group’s order book increased by more than a quarter to reach €1.72 billion at the end of 2024, thanks to 
orders worth €807 million received during the year.
	
 Vega C successfully completed two flights in April and July 2025. Arianespace will manage the next two flights 
of Vega C, after which responsibility for launch operations and commercialisation rights for the launcher will 
be transferred to Avio. In August 2025, the group obtained a 10-year administrative licence from France to 
manage operations related to Vega launchers at the Guiana Space Centre in Kourou.
	
 In September 2025, Avio presented a new 10-year strategic plan, forecasting average revenue growth 
of around 10% per year, thanks in particular to the construction of a solid propellant engine factory in the 
United States. Scheduled to be operational by the end of 2028, the site will be financed by a €400 million capital 
increase. In August 2025, the group also strengthened its partnership with the U.S. Army for the production of 
solid propellant rocket engines for tactical missiles.
Home market
	
 Ariane 6 successfully completed its first three commercial flights in March, August and November 2025, and 
a fourth launch is scheduled for December 2025. Avio has already signed several contracts for the production 
of P120C engines and liquid oxygen turbopumps for the Vulcain 2.1 and Vinci engines for the first 14 units of 
the launcher.
	
 In December 2024, the group signed two contracts worth a total of around €350 million with the European 
Space Agency (ESA) to increase the number of Vega C flights up to six per year and to accelerate the 
development of Vega E, whose maiden flight is scheduled for late 2027 or early 2028.
	
 In August 2025, as part of the European Flight Ticket Initiative, ESA tasked Avio with launching three satellites 
and systems with a Vega C launcher for the German Aerospace Centre (DLR), the French company 
GRASP Earth and the Spanish company PERSEI Space.
	
 In September 2025, Avio signed a €40 million two-year contract with ESA for the development of a reusable 
upper stage demonstrator and associated ground segment. The group will draw on its expertise in liquid 
propulsion systems and the Space Rider space shuttle programme.
Export markets
	
 Avio signed several contracts with MBDA to supply rocket motors for CAMM-ER missiles (€150 million in 
December 2024) and ASTER 30 missiles (€60 million in July 2025).
	
 In September 2025, the Avio USA subsidiary signed a contract worth up to $26 million (€22 million) with 
Raytheon (part of RTX) to provide engineering services to support the production of the Mk 104 propulsion 
system for Standard Missiles.
	
 In September 2025, Avio also reached an agreement with American launch services operator SpaceLaunch to 
launch an Earth observation satellite using Vega C in 2027 on behalf of a non-European institutional customer.
Technology and Innovation
	
 The group’s self-financed R&D expenditure amounted to €4.9 million in 2024, representing 1.1% of revenues.
	
 The hot-fire test of the P160C, the future engine for the Ariane 6, Vega C and Vega E launchers with improved 
capabilities compared to the P120C, was successfully completed in April 2025. Development of the MR10 
engine, which will power the third stage of the Vega E, and its successor, the MR60 engine, is also underway.

33
BAE SYSTEMS
Business trends
	
 BAE Systems revenues reached £26.3 billion (€31.1 billion) in 2024, 96% of which in defence. Year-on-year 
growth of 14% in local currency was driven by the integration of equipment manufacturer Ball Aerospace from 
February 2024, a large number of contracts for armoured vehicles signed in recent years by the Swedish 
subsidiary BAE Systems Hägglunds, and progress on the construction of submarines and frigates for the 
Royal Navy. The group is the leading supplier of defence equipment to the UK, Saudi Arabia and Australia, as 
well as one of the 10 top contractors to the U.S. Department of Defense.
	
 In June 2025, BAE Systems, Leonardo and Japan Aircraft Industrial Enhancement Co. (JAIEC, including 
Mitsubishi Heavy Industries), each holding a 33.33% stake, created the Edgewing joint venture. It will be 
responsible for designing and developing the future sixth-generation GCAP (Global Combat Air Programme) 
fighter aircraft, known as Tempest in the United Kingdom. The joint venture is based in the United Kingdom and 
its first Chief Executive Officer is Italian.
	
 The group inaugurated two production units in the United Kingdom in June 2025: a 170-metre-long military 
shipbuilding hall in Govan (Glasgow) and an 8,700 m² artillery gun factory in Sheffield.
Home market
	
 BAE Systems generated 27% of its turnover in the UK in 2024.	
	
 In June 2025, the UK Ministry of Defence awarded the group a £205 million (€242 million) contract for the 
production and integration of the new ECRS Mk2 radar (developed in partnership with Leonardo UK) on the 
Royal Air Force’s Typhoon fighter aircraft. 
 
	
 Construction of Astute-class SSN submarines and Dreadnought-class SSBN submarines is ongoing (two and 
three units respectively, scheduled to enter service by early 2029 and in the 2030s).
Export markets
	
 In 2024, 73% of the group’s revenue came from exports, with the United States accounting for nearly half of 
sales, thanks in particular to the F-35 programme.	
	
	
 In December 2024, Qatar confirmed its intention to acquire 12 additional Eurofighter Typhoon fighter aircraft 
for an estimated £2.8 billion (€3.3 billion). These aircraft will be assembled at BAE Systems’ Warton site in the 
UK, where production of an initial order for 24 units is nearing completion with two aircraft remaining to be 
produced. Turkey could also acquire 40 aircraft.
	
 BAE Systems Hägglunds signed two contracts worth a combined $2.5 billion (€2.3 billion) in December 2024 
to supply CV9035 Mk IIIC tracked armoured infantry fighting vehicles to Denmark (115 units) and Sweden 
(50 units).
	
 The U.S. Air Force awarded the group a $1.2 billion (€1.1 billion) contract in January 2025 to continue 
maintenance of 450 LGM-30G Minuteman III intercontinental ballistic missiles until July 2027.
	
 In May 2025, the U.S. Space Force awarded a $1.2 billion (€1.1 billion) contract to BAE Systems for the 
production of 10 missile warning and tracking satellites in medium orbit (Epoch 2 constellation), to be delivered 
from 2029 onwards.
	
 BAE Systems secured two contracts worth a combined $369 million (€341 million) in March and August 
2025 from the U.S. Marine Corps to produce 61 ACV-30 8x8 amphibious armoured vehicles equipped with 
Kongsberg’s PROTECTOR RT20 remote weapon station, armed with a 30mm cannon.
	
 In August 2025, the Ministry of Defence of Norway selected BAE Systems’ Type 26 offer for its programme to 
acquire five to six anti-submarine warfare frigates.
Technology and Innovation
	
 Self-funded R&D expenditure reached £357 million (€421 million) in 2024, representing 1.4% of revenues.

36
BAYKAR TECHNOLOGY
Business trends
	
 Baykar posted revenues of around $2.03 billion (€1.88 billion) in 2024, up 2.5% year-on-year in US dollars. 
Growth was driven by export sales, which grew 4% over the year to $1.83 billion (€1.69 billion) in 2024, 
representing 90% of total revenue. According to the group, drones were delivered to around ten countries 
during the year, including Albania, Poland, Romania, Kenya, Mali, Morocco, Azerbaijan, the United Arab 
Emirates, Maldives and Pakistan.
	
 The group is investing $96 million (€89 million) in the construction of a factory in Ukraine (near Kiev), which will 
employ around 500 people and will be dedicated to the production of Bayraktar TB2 and Bayraktar Akıncı 
UCAVs (around 120 units per year). In August 2025, this future factory was hit by Russian missiles, damaging 
it and delaying its entry into service.
	
 In June 2025, Baykar and Leonardo created a 50-50 joint venture specialising in the design, development, 
production and maintenance of aerial drones for the European market. Named LBA Systems, its headquarters 
will be based in Italy and production sites will be shared between the two countries. Leonardo will provide 
software, electronic systems and artificial intelligence solutions.
	
 In June 2025, Baykar also completed the acquisition of Piaggio Aerospace, an Italian civil and military aircraft 
manufacturer. The group is expected to retain most of Piaggio’s sites in Italy (six in total prior to the acquisition), 
particularly those involved in the production of the P.180 Avanti EVO business jet and engine maintenance. 
Baykar is also considering producing its Bayraktar TB2 and Bayraktar Akıncı UCAVs at Piaggio Aerospace’s 
sites, which could facilitate their sales on the European market.
Home market
	
 The group is one of Turkey’s leading UAV manufacturers, and several of its models are in service with the 
Turkish Armed Forces (Bayraktar Mini UAV, Bayraktar TB2 and Bayraktar Akıncı).
	
 Baykar is still flight testing its future Bayraktar Kalkan VTOL UAV, configured for reconnaissance and 
surveillance missions. During its 54th flight in January 2025, the drone was able to transmit information at a 
distance of over 100 km.
	
 In August 2025, the group began production of the Bayraktar Kızılelma carrier-based UCAV, which is expected 
to enter service in 2026.
Export markets
	
 In 2024, 90% of the group’s revenue came from exports. Baykar’s greatest success on foreign markets is the 
Bayraktar TB2, which has been acquired or ordered by 35 countries (mainly in Eastern Europe, Africa, the 
Middle East and Central Asia).
	
 In February 2025, Morocco received its first batch of Bayraktar Akıncı UCAVs, probably ordered in autumn 
2024. The country already operates the Bayraktar TB2 UCAV.
	
 The Bayraktar TB2 UCAV entered service in two additional countries in 2025: Kuwait in January and Croatia 
in August.
Technology and Innovation
	
 In March 2025, Baykar conducted a test firing of Roketsan’s UAV-122 supersonic missile using the Bayraktar 
TB3 UCAV on a target designated by laser from a Bayraktar TB2 UCAV, thus validating the coordination 
between the two aircraft for target designation and engagement.
	
 In June 2025, the group successfully conducted a destruction test of an aerial target using its Kemankes 1 
small cruise missile fired from a Bayraktar TB3 UCAV. The missile has a range of one hour and can strike a 
target located more than 100 km away, which will be recognised using an optronic guidance system based 
on artificial intelligence.

39
STRATEGIC HIGHLIGHTS
BOEING
Business trends
	
 Boeing revenues reached $66.5 billion (€61.5 billion) in 2024, down 14.5% year-on-year in local currency. The 
Commercial Airplanes division is still facing difficulties: its revenues fell 32.6% year-on-year and only 348 aicraft 
were delivered in 2024 compared to 528 in 2023. The group still recorded losses during the year (operating 
loss of $10.7 billion, or €9.9 billion), mainly due to the Commercial Airplanes and Defense, Space & Security 
divisions, with programmes suffering from major delays (KC-46A Pegasus, VC-25B or MQ-25 Stingray).
	
 Boeing operates in the military sector through its Defense, Space & Security division, which generated sales 
of $23.9 billion (€22.1 billion) in 2024. Deliveries of combat aircraft decreased over the year (11 F/A-18E/F 
Super Hornet and 14 F-15E Strike Eagle / F-15EX Eagle II, compared with 22 and 9 respectively in 2023), as 
did deliveries of new CH-47 Chinook helicopters (4 in 2024, compared with 11 in 2023), new AH-64 Apache 
helicopters (16 in 2024, compared to 20 in 2023), KC-46A Pegasus tanker aircraft (10 in 2024, compared to 
13 in 2023) and P-8A Poseidon maritime patrol aircraft (4 in 2024, compared to 11 in 2023). The group also 
handed over six MH-139A Grey Wolf helicopters to the U.S. Army (compared to two in 2023).
	
 In July 2024, Boeing reached an agreement to acquire its subcontractor Spirit AeroSystems (which posted 
revenues of $6.3 billion, or €5.8 billion, in 2024), for $4.7 billion (€4.3 billion). With Spirit AeroSystems being 
responsible for producing defective parts for civil aircraft (notably the 737 MAX), Boeing seeks to bring part of 
its aircraft production back in-house to better detect any manufacturing defects. The group intends to take over 
three factories in the United States and two in the United Kingdom by the end of 2025.
Home market
	
 The group generated 54% of its turnover in the United States in 2024. In defence, the U.S. remains by far 
Boeing’s largest market (78% of sales).
	
 In January 2025, the U.S. Air Force announced the award in 2026 of a contract for the production of the first 
batch of T-7A Red Hawk advanced trainer aircraft (produced by Boeing and Saab). The aircraft should reach 
initial operational capability (IOC) in late 2027 or early 2028.
	
 In March 2025, the U.S. Air Force selected Boeing, over Lockheed Martin, to develop the future sixth-generation 
F-47 air superiority aircraft (NGAD programme), which will be accompanied by UCAVs. This aircraft is intended 
to replace the F-22 Raptor fighter aircraft (Lockheed Martin).
	
 In July 2025, Boeing secured a $2.8 billion (€2.6 billion) contract with the U.S. Space Force to develop and 
produce two communication, command and control satellites (with an option for two more), enabling the 
President of the United States to use nuclear weapons in all circumstances.
Export markets
	
 The group generated 46% of its revenues from exports in 2024. About half of exports sales were generated by 
the Commercial Airplanes division.
	
 Boeing was awarded two contracts worth a combined $580 million (€536 million) in December 2024 by the 
U.S. Air Force to integrate new electronic systems on 68 F-15J Eagle fighter aircraft belonging to the Japan Self-
Defense Forces (Japan Super Interceptor programme). The group will supply its new AN/ALQ-250 EPAWSS 
protection system (warning radar, countermeasures).
	
 In June 2025, Boeing and the Royal Australian Air Force demonstrated the destruction of an aerial target by two 
MQ-28 Ghost Bat «loyal wingman» UCAVs controlled from an E-7A Wedgetail detection aircraft.
Technology and Innovation
	
 The group’s total R&D expenditure reached $3.8 billion (€3.5 billion) in 2024, representing 5.7% of revenues.

42
STRATEGIC HIGHLIGHTS
CZECHOSLOVAK GROUP (CSG)
Business trends
	
 CSG revenues reached €4,009 million in 2024, up 131.2% year-on-year. Growth was driven by the Defence 
(medium and large calibre ammunition, armoured vehicles and artillery systems) and Mobility (heavy off-road 
vehicles, chassis for weapon systems, etc.) divisions. Both divisions benefited from sustained demand due 
to significant ammunition requirements in Europe and the supply of armoured vehicles to Ukraine. Revenue 
in Ukraine increased 4.3-fold between 2023 and 2024, while revenue generated in the European Union 
(excluding the Czech Republic and Slovakia) nearly doubled year-on-year.
	
 In November 2024, CSG completed the acquisition of The Kinetic Group from Vista Outdoor for $2.2 billion 
(€2 billion). These activities include, in particular, the manufacture of small-calibre ammunition for military 
and civilian markets. The Kinetic Group employs 3,800 people, mainly in the United States, and generated 
$1.5 billion (€1.4 billion) in revenues in 2024.
	
 In June 2025, the MSM Group subsidiary partnered with Hellenic Defence Systems (HDS) to create a 51%-
49% owned joint venture in Greece, called Hellenic Ammunition, which will be dedicated primarily to the 
production of TNT and 155mm artillery ammunition from 2027 onwards. The joint venture will use the HDS 
facilities in Laurion, Greece. CSG will invest €50 million in this project and will also receive a €33 million grant 
from the European Union under the ASAP programme.
	
 In July 2025, MSM Group acquired the ammunition manufacturer ZVI, based in Vsetín (Czech Republic). 
The company produces medium-calibre ammunition (20mm and 30mm).
Home markets (Czech Republic, Slovakia)
	
 CSG is historically a major player in its domestic markets. However, the share of revenue generated in the 
Czech Republic and Slovakia has been declining since 2021 due to external growth operations carried out 
internationally in recent years.
	
 In July 2025, the Ministry of Defence of the Czech Republic ordered 24 Titus 6x6 multirole armoured vehicles 
from the Eldis Pardubice subsidiary for CZK 4.07 billion (€162 million), including 22 in command post version 
and two in training vehicle version. These vehicles are assembled locally by various CSG subsidiaries under 
licence from KNDS France.
	
 Eldis Pardubice signed a CZK 258 million (€10 million) contract with the Czech Air Navigation Service in 
September 2025 for the supply of two RL-2000 surveillance radars (with two MSSR-1 secondary radars). 
These radars are due to be delivered by 2029 and will allow coverage of the entire Czech airspace.
Export markets
	
 The group generated 83% of its revenues from exports in 2024, a share up by 16 percentage points since 2021.
	
 MSM Group was awarded two contracts in June and December 2024 by Junghans Defence (a joint venture 
between Diehl Defence and Thales) to supply hundreds of thousands of artillery rockets in 2025-2026.
	
 In February 2025, the Excalibur Army subsidiary completed the upgrade of 90 T-72M/M1 main battle tanks 
from the Czech Army’s stocks to the T-72EA standard (which includes new optronic systems, a more powerful 
engine, better ballistic protection, etc.). These tanks are intended for the Ukrainian Army.
	
 In June 2025, CSG was selected by the Indonesian Air Force to participate in its Trisula air defence system 
acquisition programme. These systems are mounted on Tatra Force 8x8 truck chassis, are equipped with 
radars supplied by Retia (two of the group’s subsidiaries) and launch Roketsan’s HISAR-O+ and SIPER 
interceptor missiles.
	
 In August 2025, the MSM North America subsidiary received a $636 million (€588 million) order to build a 
155mm artillery shell loading plant in Middletown, Iowa, near the Iowa Army Ammunition Plant. Due to be 
operational by September 2029, this facility will employ 70 people and will be able to fill 36,000 shells per 
month.

45
STRATEGIC HIGHLIGHTS
DAMEN
Business trends
	
 Damen revenues fell by 2.2% in 2024, to € 3,023 million. The group delivered 117 ships during the year, 
compared with 129 in 2023. The year was marked by several deliveries of naval vessels, including the third and 
fourth Yarmook-class corvettes, named Hunain (F-273) and Yamama (F-274), to the Pakistan Navy.
	
 Damen recorded a net profit of €58 million in 2024, after reaching losses of €466 million between 2018 and 
2020. The group is facing difficulties in naval defence following the loss of several contracts, for example to 
supply 12 minehunters to the Belgian and Dutch Navies (awarded to Naval Group and Exail) and 4 Tamandaré-
class frigates (awarded to tkMS and Embraer) in 2019, or to build 3 frigates for the Greek navy in 2022 (won by 
Naval Group). Damen is also facing delays on the F126 frigate programme for the German Navy.
Home market
	
 The group is a key supplier to the Royal Netherlands Navy for its surface ship programmes. Since June 2023, it 
has been in charge of supplying four anti-submarine warfare frigates to the Belgian and Dutch navies (two units 
each) to be delivered between 2028 and 2030, replacing the Karel Doorman-class frigates currently in service 
and already built by the group (Anti-Submarine Warfare Frigate programme). Three additional ships could be 
ordered (two for the Netherlands and one for Belgium).
	
 In March 2025, Damen delivered the Den Helder replenishment oiler to the Royal Netherlands Navy. The ship 
was built at the group’s shipyard in Galati (Romania) and was fitted out in Vlissingen (Netherlands). It entered 
service in October 2025.
	
 In June 2025, Damen and Fugro delivered to the Royal Netherlands Navy a FCS 5009 Fast Crew Supplier, 
named Galatea, which was modified for surveillance missions and deployment of UAVs and UUVs. The ship, 
which is owned by the two groups, will be leased by the Navy for a period of 2 years (+2 years optional).
Export markets
	
 Damen generated 80% of its revenues from exports in 2024. Its extensive network of shipyards abroad 
(Poland, Romania, South Africa, United Arab Emirates, Turkey, China, Vietnam, etc.) allows the group to win 
international tenders in both the civil and military sectors.
	
 Since 2020, Damen has been prime contractor for the German Navy’s F126 Niedersachsen-class multi-
mission frigate programme, in partnership with Thales Nederland and NVL Group, and at a cost of more than 
€8 billion for 6 ships. Damen is designing the ships, which will be built in Germany by two NVL Group shipyards, 
Blohm+Voss and Peene-Werft, and German Naval Yards Kiel. The first frigate is under construction and is 
expected to be delivered from 2028 onwards.
	
 Damen delivered two ships in May 2025: the third and final Warrior-class patrol boat, named Adam Kok 
(P1573), to the South African Navy, and the ASD Tug 3010 ICE icebreaker tug ordered by the Lithuanian Navy 
in August 2024.
	
 The Royal Navy ordered 24 maritime service ships from the British group Serco (prime contractor) and Damen 
(construction contractor) in September 2025 as part of the Vessel Replacement Project (VRP). The acquisition 
programme includes ASD tugs, RSD tugs, pilot boats, barges and crane vessels, to be delivered in 2027-2028.
Technology and Innovation
	
 In February 2025, the group introduced two new logistics support ships based on the Den Helder replenishment 
oiler (180 metres long with a capacity of 7,600 m³ of diesel fuel): the LSS 11000 (140 metres and 4,850 m³) and 
the LSS 9000 (127 metres and 3,300 m³).

[Page 49 / 226 | chars: 3848]
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

48
Business trends
	
 Dassault Aviation adjusted revenues reached €6,230 million in 2024, up 29.8% year-on-year. Revenues in the 
Defence division grew by 33.1% year-on-year, thanks to higher deliveries of Rafale fighter aircraft (21 units in 
2024, compared with 13 in 2023). Deliveries of Falcon business jets also increased (31 units in 2024, compared 
with 26 in 2023).
	
 The group’s order book stood at €43.2 billion at the end of 2024, up 11.2% year-on-year. It mainly consisted of 
defence orders (88.4% in value) and included 220 new Rafale fighter aircraft, of which 164 are to be delivered 
overseas and 56 to France (compared with 141 and 70 respectively at the end of 2023). In France, the order 
book also included the future Albatros (7 units) and Archange (3 units) mission aircraft, the Ravel (Rafale), 
Balzac (Mirage 2000) and Océan (ATL2) MRO contracts, the upgrading of the Rafale F3R to the F4.1 standard 
and phase 1B of the FCAS demonstrator (in partnership with Airbus and Indra Sistemas).
	
 The group inaugurated a production site in Cergy (in the Paris region) in September 2025. This new facility 
is intended to take over most of the activities of the former Argenteuil factory, located nearby (assembly of 
front fuselage parts and skins for the Falcon and Rafale fighter aircraft, fitting out of the Rafale fuselage and 
production of metal piping).
	
 For 2025, Dassault Aviation expects a new increase in revenues, mainly driven by the delivery of 25 Rafale and 
40 Falcon aircraft.
Home market
	
 Dassault Aviation generated 32% of its turnover in France in 2024, a share wich remained stable year-on-year 
due to the large number of Rafale deliveries during the year (14 units, compared to 11 in 2023).
	
 The future Falcon Archange intelligence aircraft made its maiden flight in July 2025. It is scheduled to enter 
service in 2027.
	
 The French defence procurement agency (DGA) ordered five Falcon Albatros maritime and patrol 
reconnaissance aircraft from Dassault Aviation in October 2025, bringing the number of aircraft ordered since 
2020 to 12. The first delivery is scheduled for 2026.
Export markets
	
 In January 2025, Dassault Aviation completed deliveries of Rafale aircraft to Greece (24 aircraft ordered in 
2020 and 2022, including 12 new and 12 second-hand units).
	
 In January 2025, the group also presented its first Rafale F4 for the United Arab Emirates (80 units ordered). 
The aircraft will undergo testing at Dassault Aviation’s Flight Test Centre in Istres (in southeastern France). 
The first deliveries are scheduled for the end of 2026.
	
 In April 2025, India formalised its order for 26 Rafale F4 Marine aircraft for an estimated €6.5 billion. 
The contract also provides for the integration of the Astra Mk1 air-to-air missile (Bharat Dynamics), as well as 
the establishment in India of fuselage production facilities (in partnership with Tata Advanced Systems Ltd) 
and MRO centres for engines, sensors and weapons. MRO activities will be carried out by a future subsidiary, 
named Dassault Aviation MRO India (DAMROI) and based in Noida. Deliveries are expected to take place 
between 2028 and 2030.
	
 In April 2025, Dassault Aviation began deliveries to Egypt of the 31 Rafale aircraft ordered in 2021. A contract 
worth an estimated €300 million could also be signed for the maintenance of the 23 Rafale aircraft already in 
service with the Egyptian Air Force.
Technology and Innovation
	
 The group’s self-financed R&D expenditure amounted to €437 million in 2024, representing 7% of revenues.
	
 In June 2025, the French Ministry of Armed Forces awarded a €30 million funding for the development of the 
future dual-use VORTEX reusable space aircraft (surveillance, scientific research, institutional services, etc.).
DASSAULT AVIATION

RIEGL

[Page 52 / 226 | chars: 3738]
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

51
Business trends
	
 Founded in 2011 under the name Delair-Tech, the French company Delair specialises in the design and 
production of professional, electrically powered UAVs for dual missions. It stands out for its fixed-wing drones, 
whereas many competitors offer rotor drones. In 2012, it also obtained certification for beyond visual line of 
sight (BVLOS) flight operations.
	
 Supported by a surge in production rates (from 100 drones per year to more than 250), Delair revenues grew 
strongly in 2024. Turnover tripled to €30 million, half of which came from exports and 80% from defence, and 
workforce grew to 200 people.
	
 Delair plans to double revenues in 2025 to €60 million. In June 2025, the company announced that it was 
preparing a new round to raise funds.
	
 In October 2024, Delair acquired the French company Squadrone System (€2 million in revenue and around 
20 employees). Specialising in the design of multicopter drones for complex uses, Squadrone System is 
participating in an R&D programme managed by the French Defense Innovation Agency (AID) and the French 
defence procurement agency (DGA) on drone swarms. It is also involved in the RapidEagle anti-drone project.
Home market
	
 Delair is a supplier to the French Armed Forces. The UX11 drone is used in particular by the French Army and 
Special Forces for observation and intelligence missions. 100 units were ordered by the French Ministry of 
Armed Forces in February 2024.
	
 An evaluation test of the DT26 UAV was carried out by the French Armed Forces in Djibouti in April 2024.
	
 The DT46 UAV is a reversible drone that can be used in fixed-wing mode or in vertical take-off and landing 
(VTOL) mode thanks to two vertical rotor arms. It was delivered to the French Armed Forces in December 2024 
and deployed in Romania as part of the Eagle mission. It was also tested by the French Navy on a landing 
helicopter dock in early 2024 and entered service with the French Gendarmerie in 2025.
	
 As part of the Colibri call for projects, KNDS France and Delair were selected in June 2024 to supply short-
range loitering munitions (MTO CP), based on Delair’s UX11 drone and optimised for BVLOS flights. The French 
Armed Forces ordered 2,000 units in autumn 2024, partly destined for Ukraine. The delivery of 460 munitions 
named Damoclès, with a 10 km range, 40-minute endurance and approximately 500-gram warhead, is 
scheduled for the end of 2025.
	
 In June 2025, Delair presented the DT61, a long-range fixed-wing VTOL observation UAV with a radio range 
of over 100 km and a payload capacity of 15 kg (for a total weight of 100 kg). Currently in the final qualification 
phase, the drone is expected to carry out its first operational missions in the second half of the year and be 
commercially available by the end of 2025.
Export markets
	
 The company has customers in 70 countries. Ukraine, which has received several hundred drones, accounts 
for a significant share of Delair’s exports. In addition to Europe, Africa is also one of the company’s main 
markets (delivery of DT26 UAVs to Niger, Ivory Coast, etc.).
Technology and Innovation
	
 Delair is working on a hydrogen-powered UAV project called Hydrone, based on the DT26. The aim is to 
significantly increase the range compared to electric propulsion, with the Hydrone expected to have twice the 
range of the DT26.
	
 In February 2025, Delair partnered with French company Ascendance, a specialist in hybrid propulsion, to 
develop a hybrid-electric version of the DT46 UAV. The project aims to increase the drone’s range and flexibility 
to carry out observation missions for the DGA Essais de missiles site in southwestern France.

54
Business trends
	
 Diehl Defence revenues grew strongly again in 2024 (+60%) to reach €1,827 million. The division recorded 
sustained demand for air defence systems, missiles and artillery ammunition. It accounted for 39% of the Diehl 
Group’s total turnover and 25% of its workforce in 2024.
	
 Diehl Defence recently acquired two German companies: explosives manufacturer Dynamit Nobel in 
November 2024 and military training and simulation systems specialist e.sigma Systems in June 2025.
Home market
	
  As a key supplier to the German Armed Forces, Diehl Defence generated 31% of its sales in its domestic 
market in 2024. During the year, the division undertook expansion work at its German sites in Röthenbach and 
Nonnweiler to meet increased demand.
	
 In January 2024, the Bundeswehr launched its LVS NNbS short- and very-short-range air defence system 
programme. The ARGE NNbS consortium, formed by Diehl Defence, Rheinmetall and Hensoldt, is tasked 
with developing two versions of the system by 2028, one armed with a 30mm Skyranger 30 turreted gun and 
the other with IRIS-T SLS missiles.
	
 In July 2024, Germany’s Federal Office of Bundeswehr Equipment (BAAINBw) ordered 155mm calibre 
artillery ammunition for Germany and allied countries from the ARGE DiNa 155mm consortium, consisting 
of Diehl Defence and Nammo. This contract is the largest ammunition order ever received by Diehl Defence.
	
 In December 2024, the BAAINBw also awarded Diehl Defence a contract for the development and series 
production of the new version of the IRIS-T Block II missile on behalf of Germany, Sweden, Spain and Italy.
	
 Already partners in artillery ammunition production, the division and Elbit Systems extended their cooperation 
in March 2025 to include the 70mm Euro-GATR rocket, intended to equip the German Army’s H145M 
helicopters (Airbus).
Export markets
	
 The division generated 69% of its revenue from exports in 2024, mainly in Europe, North America and China.
	
 Diehl Defence and Korea Aerospace Industries (KAI) signed an agreement in February 2024 to equip FA-50 
and KF-21 fighter aircraft with the IRIS-T missile.
	
 As part of the European Sky Shield Initiative (ESSI), Slovenia and Bulgaria acquired IRIS-T SLM missiles in 
2024. Several countries also ordered missiles in the summer of 2025: Slovenia (two additional units, including 
IRIS-T SLM missiles and their launch systems), Sweden (seven units), Switzerland (five units) and Denmark 
(one unit).
	
 Diehl Defence reached agreements with Raytheon (part of RTX) in August 2025 to produce components for 
the Stinger missile in Europe, and with Lockheed Martin in October 2025 to participate in the production of 
PAC-3 MSE interceptor missiles.
Technology and Innovation
	
 At the Enforce Tac trade fair in Nuremberg (Germany) in February 2025, Diehl Defence presented its 
Sky Sphere anti-drone system, designed for dual applications. The interception system is based on CICADA 
eMissile projectiles that can catch target drones with a net or destroy them.
	
 Diehl Defence and Safran Electronics & Defense signed a cooperation agreement in June 2025 to develop 
modular air-to-ground light ammunition for all types of platforms.

EMBRAER
STRATEGIC HIGHLIGHTS
Business trends
	
 Embraer revenues grew 21.4% in US dollars in 2024 to $6,395 million (€5,910 million). The group recorded 
higher revenues in its Commercial Aviation division (+19.6%), thanks to higher deliveries of civil aircraft 
(76 aircraft delivered, compared with 64 in 2023), and Executive Aviation division (business jets, +25.2%). 
The  Defense & Security division, which includes most of the group’s defence activities, also recorded a sharp 
increase in revenues (+39.9%) and delivered three C-390 Millennium aircraft (one each to Brazil, Hungary and 
Portugal). The division is also assembling three F-39E fighter aircraft for the Brazilian Air Force.
	
 In December 2024, Embraer inaugurated a new subsidiary in Portugal, named Embraer Defense Europe. 
Based in Lisbon, it will support the group’s efforts to develop sales to NATO and European Union countries.
	
 In May 2025, the group established a subsidiary in New Delhi, India, with the aim of strengthening its local 
presence in both the civil and military sectors.
Home market
	
 Embraer is the main beneficiary of the major military programmes launched by the Brazilian government in 
the last decade (F-39 fighter aircraft, C-390 Millennium tactical transport aircraft, etc.). 25% of the Defense & 
Security division’s revenues came from its domestic market in 2024.
	
 The Brazilian Air Force awarded Embraer a contract in December 2024 to supply a second firefighting kit 
for installation on the C-390 Millennium aircraft. This kit, known as MAFFS II (Modular Airborne Fire Fighting 
System), can drop approximately 12,000 litres of water in 7 seconds.
	
 In December 2024, the group also signed a BRL 102 million contract (€18 million) with the Brazilian Army to 
produce a SABER M200 Vigilante aircraft detection and tracking radar.
	
 The Águas Azuis consortium, consisting of tkMS, Embraer and its subsidiary Atech, laid the keel for the 
third Tamandaré-class multi-mission frigate, named Cunha Moreira, at the Itajaí shipyard in June 2025. 
The mothership is undergoing sea trials with delivery scheduled for December 2025, and the second unit, 
named Jerônimo de Albuquerque, was launched in August 2025. 
 
Export markets
	
 The Defense & Security division generated 75% of its revenues from exports in 2024, a share that has increased 
by 23 percentage points over the 2021-2024 period. This upward trend follows the sharp rise in sales in Europe, 
in particular for the C-390 Millennium tactical transport aircraft.
	
 In December 2024, the Portuguese Air Force ordered 12 A-29N Super Tucano trainer and ground attack 
aircraft, a version equipped with a NATO-standard communication system, for approximately €200 million.
	
 In February 2025, Embraer began assembly of the first C-390 Millennium tactical transport aircraft for Austria, 
which ordered four aircraft in July 2024.
	
 Embraer recorded three new orders in 2025 for its C-390 Millennium aircraft. Sweden and Lithuania ordered 
four units in March and three units in June respectively. Portugal, which has already received three aircraft, 
also ordered a sixth unit in June.
	
 Panama acquired four EMB 314 Super Tucano aircraft in September 2025 for $78 million (€72 million). 
These aircraft will be assigned to the National Aeronaval Service and will be used for surveillance and 
protection missions.
Technology and Innovation
	
 In October 2024, Visiona, a joint venture between Embraer and Telebras, presented the first images collected 
by its VCUB1 Earth observation nanosatellite, the first one to be developed entirely by Brazilian players, and 
announced that the satellite is now operational.

STRATEGIC HIGHLIGHTS
EURENCO
Business trends
	
 Eurenco posted revenues of €478 million in 2024, up 36.6% year-on-year, and recorded €2.2 billion in orders 
during the year. The group benefited from international geopolitical instability, particularly with the war in 
Ukraine. It aims for €1 billion sales in 2028.
	
 In December 2024, Eurenco acquired Thiot Ingénierie, a French company that specialises in impact 
technologies, ballistic testing and materials engineering. The company generates revenue of €9 million, 64% of 
which comes from exports and three-quarters from defence, and employs around 40 people.
	
 Eurenco is committing to a €650 million investment plan over the 2024-2026 period, mainly self-financed, to 
double the production capacity of its various sites in France, Belgium and Sweden.
	
 In March 2025, the group inaugurated a production line for 155mm shell powder at its Bergerac site in 
southwestern France. The plant has an annual capacity of 1,200 tonnes of large-calibre powders (enabling 
it to supply 500,000 additional modular charges per year) and the target is to reach an annual production of 
1,800 tonnes.
	
 In July 2025, Eurenco began work on expanding its Belgian site in Clermont, which will accommodate around 
100 additional employees. The aim is to double the production capacity for small-calibre powders, for which 
the group has orders until 2032.
Home market
	
 Around 20% of the group’s revenues are generated in its home market. For instance, it supplies modular 
charges for the 155mm artillery systems of KNDS France, as well as explosive charges for the F21 heavy 
torpedo (Naval Group) and the ANL / Sea Venom light anti-ship missile (MBDA, Franco-British programme).
Export markets
	
 Exports account for about 80% of the group’s revenues. Its main customers include Rheinmetall, Czechoslovak 
Group, BAE Systems and Saab.
	
  In June 2025, Eurenco entered into a long-term partnership with FN Herstal, a subsidiary of the Belgian group 
FN Browning Group, to supply high-performance powders for small-calibre ammunition.
	
 In July 2025, the group also signed a five-year agreement with Czech small-calibre ammunition manufacturer 
Sellier & Bellot, part of Colt CZ Group, to supply high-performance powders produced by Eurenco in Belgium.
	
 As part of an agreement signed in 2024, Eurenco and Poland’s Mesko (part of PGZ) announced in 
August 2025 the launch of a production line for 155mm modular charges in Pionki, Poland, with a production 
target of 100,000 units per year. Eurenco is supplying the technology and modular charge kit, while Mesko is 
responsible for local assembly using its own powders. The two companies also signed a multi-year agreement 
for the supply of nitrocellulose produced at Eurenco’s Bergerac site to Mesko.
	
 Eurenco signed an agreement with the Italian group Beretta Holding in September 2025 to supply small-
calibre powders for seven years. The powders will be produced at Eurenco’s Belgian (Clermont) and Swedish 
(Karlskoga) plants and are intended for military and civilian use in Europe and America.
Technology and Innovation
	
 Eurenco relies on a vast network of partners (laboratories, universities, public and private research centres) 
around the world to develop new innovative solutions: explosives and powders with improved performance, 
less toxic ‘green’ powders, smaller and lighter explosives for drones, products and processes for civil and 
military applications, etc.

63
STRATEGIC HIGHLIGHTS
Business trends
	
 Exail Technologies revenues reached €373 million in 2024, up 16% year-on-year thanks to strong growth 
in both divisions. The group recorded new orders worth €451 million in 2024 and had an order book of 
€708 million at year-end, representing almost two years of sales.
	
 In January 2025, Exail announced the acquisition of French photonics company Leukos, which specialises in 
lasers for metrology, spectroscopy and imaging applications. Leukos generates revenue of around €3 million 
and employs around 20 people.
Home market
	
 Created by the combination of iXblue and ECA Group, the new company Exail is a major player in various 
fields, including maritime autonomous robotic systems, very high performance inertial navigation solutions, 
and photonic, quantum and space solutions.
	
 In November 2024, the French defence procurement ageny (DGA) selected Exail and Thales to supply 
16 unmanned underwater vehicles, including 8 optional units, to the French Navy as part of the SLAMF mine 
countermeasures programme. Exail’s A18-M AUVs will be equipped with Thales’ SAMDIS 600 sonar.
	
 Babcock France selected Exail in May 2025 to supply flight simulators for the Pilatus PC-7 MKX aircraft as part 
of the Mentor 2 programme for training French military pilots.
Export markets
	
 Exail Technologies generated 75% of its revenues from exports in 2024, and has customers in 80 countries.
	
 The group is positioned on several major defence contracts, in particular since 2019 alongside Naval Group 
(Belgium Naval & Robotics consortium) for the design, construction and maintenance of 12 mine warfare 
vessels equipped with mine-clearing drones for the Belgian and Dutch navies. Exail’s share of this contract 
amounts to €500m over 10 years, out of a total value of €2.1 billion.
	
 In February 2025, Exail Technologies won a contract worth nearly €500 million to supply a foreign Navy with 
various unmanned vehicles (USVs, AUVs and UAVs) for mine countermeasures, to be delivered over a period 
of four years.
	
 In May 2025, the Republic of Singapore Navy ordered various robotic systems from Exail to equip unmanned 
surface vessels supplied by ST Engineering for mine countermeasures, including inertial navigation systems, 
underwater acoustic positioning systems and forward-looking sonars (FLS). The contract also includes 
SEASCAN and K-STER underwater drones.
	
 In May 2025, Spain selected Exail’s UmiX 40 inertial measurement unit (IMU) to equip SIRTAP tactical UAS 
supplied by Airbus Defence and Space (27 UAS ordered at the end of 2023).
	
 In June 2025, Exail signed a contract with the Indonesian Navy to supply the UMIS integrated mine 
countermeasures system, including four Inspector 90 USVs and SEASCAN and K-STER underwater drones. 
The programme is expected to last three years.
	
 A European Navy ordered five DriX H-8 USVs in June 2025 for maritime surveillance.
	
 In August 2025, Exail announced the first sale of a DriX H-9 USV to a foreign hydrographic service for naval 
defence, with delivery scheduled for 2026. Measuring 9 metres long and weighing 2.1 tonnes, the H-9 is an 
enlarged version of the H-8 model with twice the range.
Technology and Innovation
	
 The group’s R&D expenditure reached €40.8 million in 2024, representing 10.9% of revenues.
	
 In September 2025, the DriX O-16 USV took part in NATO’s REPMUS (Robotic Experimentation and Prototyping 
using Maritime Unmanned Systems) exercises in Portugal, after travelling 2,000 km autonomously over 6 days 
from Exail’s site in La Ciotat (near Marseille).
EXAIL TECHNOLOGIES (FORMERLY GROUPE GORGÉ)

66
Business trends
	
 Fincantieri revenues increased 6.2% in 2024, to €8,128 million. Growth was driven by the Offshore and 
Specialized vessels division as well as the Equipment, Systems and Infrastructure division. On the other hand, 
revenues in the Shipbuilding division fell by 2.3% year-on-year, following declines in both naval vessels (-2.2%) 
and civil ships (-2.5%).
	
 Revenues in the Naval vessels segment reached €2,015 million in 2024 (before intra-group eliminations). 
During the year, Fincantieri delivered the Trieste Landing Helicopter Dock (LHD) and a fourth Thaon di Revel-
class multipurpose offshore patrol vessel (PPA) to the Italian Navy, as well as the second San Giorgio-class/
BDSL Landing Platform Dock (LPD) to the Qatari Emiri Navy. In addition, the Vard subsidiary handed over the 
third and last Jan Mayen-class coastguard vessel to the Norwegian Coast Guard.
	
 In January 2025, the group completed the acquisition of Leonardo’s Italian subsidiary WASS, which 
manufactures torpedoes (A184, A244, Black Shark, MU90) and sonars (ATAS), for a fixed amount of 
€287 million and a variable amount of approximately €120 million. WASS employs more than 500 people in 
Livorno and generates approximately €160 million in revenue.
	
 Fincantieri signed a letter of intent with the Polish group PGZ in September 2025 to cooperate on the Polish 
Navy’s acquisition programme comprising up to four Orka-class submarines.
Home market
	
 With nine shipyards and nearly 12,000 employees in Italy, Fincantieri is the prime contractor for the Italian 
Navy’s main programmes (surface ships, special vessels, submarines under German licence, naval systems, 
etc.). However, the group generates a small share of its sales on its domestic market (20% in 2024).
	
 In December 2024, the Italian Navy commissioned the Trieste Landing Helicopter Dock (LHD), built by 
Fincantieri. The ship can also carry F-35B Lightning II fighter aircraft (Lockheed Martin) after being equipped 
with a ski jump. It complements the aircraft carrier Cavour, also built by the group, and replaces the Garibaldi, 
which was decommissioned in October 2024.
	
 The joint venture (with Leonardo) Orizzonte Sistemi Navali delivered the ninth and tenth FREMM Bergamini-
class multipurpose frigates, named Spartaco Schergat (F598) and Emilio Bianchi (F599), to the Italian Navy 
in April and July 2025, respectively. In addition, the two partners laid the keel for the first FREMM EVO 
multipurpose frigate in July 2025. Named Bersagliere, it is scheduled for delivery in 2029.
	
 In June 2025, the Italian Navy ordered two Thaon di Revel-class multipurpose offshore patrol vessels (PPA) 
from Fincantieri in Light+ configuration to replace the two units sold to Indonesia in March 2024. These ships 
are expected to be delivered in 2029 and 2030.
Export markets
	
 Export sales accounted for 80% of the group’s revenues in 2024. Through its subsidiaries Marinette Marine 
and Vard, Fincantieri is well positioned in the United States and the Nordic countries.
	
 Maestral (a joint venture between Fincantieri and EDGE Group) announced in February 2025 the forthcoming 
signing of a €500 million five-year contract with the United Arab Emirates Navy for the maintenance and 
logistical support of its entire fleet.
	
 In July 2025, the group delivered its first Thaon di Revel-class multipurpose offshore patrol vessel (PPA), 
named Brawijaya (320), to the Indonesian Navy. This vessel was originally intended for the Italian Navy under 
the name Marcantonio Colonna (P433).
Technology and Innovation
	
 The group’s total R&D expenditure amounted to €175 million in 2024, representing 2.2% of revenues.
STRATEGIC HIGHLIGHTS
