69
Business trends
	
 FN Browning Group posted revenues of €934 million in 2024, up 2.9% year-on-year. Growth was driven by 
the Defence & Security division, in particular the FN Herstal subsidiary, which benefited from strong demand 
(machine guns, spare parts, equipment and maintenance/modernisation services). On the other hand, the 
Hunting & Sport Shooting division suffered from sluggish conditions in Europe and North America, its main 
markets.
	
 FN Herstal plans to invest around €100 million between 2024 and 2027 to expand its production capacity. 
By the end of 2025, its machine gun production is expected to have doubled since 2022, and its ammunition 
production to have quadrupled compared to 2020. To support the group, in June 2025 the Walloon Region 
approved the financing of a €100 million capital increase, without bringing in new shareholders.
	
 In September 2025, FN Browning Group completed the acquisition of French group Sofisport, which 
specialises in the production of ammunition for hunting and sport shooting (Nobel Sport, Cheddite, Eley Hawk 
brands) and cartridge components (powder, cases, primers, etc.). Sofisport employs around 1,000 people 
and has some 15 production sites in France, Spain, Italy, the United Kingdom and Canada. The combined 
turnover of the two groups is estimated at nearly €1.4 billion.
	
 However, FN Browning Group’s offer to take over Verney-Carron was turned down. Based in Saint-Étienne, the 
French firearm manufacturing company was acquired in June 2025 by the French company Rivolier.
Home market
	
 Through its Defence & Security division, the group produces small arms and their components, small-calibre 
ammunition and integrated weapon systems for land, naval and air platforms. It has a presence in Belgium 
(FN Herstal), the United States (FN America), the United Kingdom (FN UK) and Finland (Noptel, which 
produces ballistic and sighting devices).
	
 In June 2024, the Belgian government signed a strategic partnership with FN Herstal. Worth €1.7 billion, the 
agreement provides for the supply of small-calibre ammunition (5.56mm, 5.7mm, 7.62mm and 12.7mm) to the 
Belgian Armed Forces and law enforcement agencies over a 20-year period. It also includes MRO services for 
small arms in service (pistols, rifles and machine guns) and joint R&D projects with the Belgian Armed Forces.
Export markets
	
 The group generates a large share of its sales from exports, mainly to Europe and the United States, and 
has customers in 130 countries. FN Herstal supplies almost all NATO countries, which account for 80% of its 
production.
	
 In March 2025, France announced a call for tenders to relaunch its small-calibre ammunition production and 
recreate a national industry. FN Browning Group is expected to be one of the bidders.
	
 In March and September 2025, the FN America subsidiary was awarded two contracts by the Defense 
Logistics Agency in the United States to supply M240 and M249 machine gun barrels for a ceiling value of 
$46.5 million (€42.5 million). In July 2025, it also signed a $4.9 million (€4.2 million) contract to supply 7.62mm 
FN M240L machine guns to the U.S. Army.
	
  In September 2025, the FN UK subsidiary signed a 10-year contract worth more than £20 million (€23 million) 
with the UK Ministry of Defence for the mid-life upgrade of FN M2HB QCB heavy machine guns, known as 
L111A1 in the United Kingdom, and associated support services.
Technology and Innovation
	
 In February 2024, FN Herstal, John Cockerill and the Walloon Region of Belgium joined forces to support a four-
year research programme at the University of Liège on the uses of artificial intelligence in defence, particularly 
in the use of drones.
STRATEGIC HIGHLIGHTS
FN BROWNING GROUP

72
Business trends
	
 FNSS revenues reached TRY 3,343 million (€94 million) in 2024, down 43.7% year-on-year in local currency. 
As one of Turkey’s leading producers of armoured vehicles, along with Otokar and BMC, FNSS has delivered 
more than 4,000 vehicles since its creation. The group has established itself as one of the preferred suppliers 
to the Turkish Armed Forces, notably with the ACV-15 (about 2,300 units in service in Turkey), the Kaplan 10 and 
the Pars vehicles (208 and 261 units ordered respectively since 2016).
	
 FNSS also sets up cooperation or technology transfer agreements to win contracts abroad, such as the 
partnerships with PT Pindad in Indonesia and Deftech in Malaysia.
	
 In December 2024, BAE Systems sold its 49% stake in FNSS, which the British group had held since 2005, to 
Nurol Holding for £20 million (€24 million). Nurol Holding now owns the entire share capital of FNSS.
	
 FNSS and Anadolu Defence signed a letter of intent in October 2024 to cooperate in tactical wheeled logistics 
vehicles and civilian vehicles (particularly firefighting and ambulance versions) for export customers.
Home market
	
 FNSS is a major supplier to the Ministry of National Defense of Turkey and is expected to benefit from the 
country’s increased military spending in the coming years. Turkey is particularly interested in developing its 
capabilities in land systems, in order to reduce its dependency on arms imports and strengthen the country’s 
strategic autonomy.
	
 In February 2025, the Turkish Armed Forces awarded FNSS its first contract to mass-produce 25 Pars Alpha 
6x6 and 8x8 armoured vehicles in three variants: command post for the 6x6, tank destroyer and recovery 
vehicle for the 8x8. Up to 2,000 vehicles could be ordered.
Export markets
	
 Traditionally, FNSS generated most of its revenues from exports, but the situation reversed in recent years as 
a result of the global health crisis and the completion, in 2021, of two major contracts in Malaysia and Oman. 
The group aims to achieve a more balanced distribution in the near future, notably by targeting new markets 
such as South America and Central Asia.
	
 In October 2024, the group agreed with PT Pindad to develop and produce the new 30-tonne Kaplan APC 
armoured infantry fighting vehicle for the Indonesian Army. The vehicle will be able to carry 10 troops and 
will be available in several versions. A prototype was presented at the Indo Defence exhibition in Jakarta in 
June 2025, and the first two vehicles will be delivered to the Indonesian Army for testing at the end of 2026.
	
 FNSS and UAE-based company Al Taif (a subsidiary of EDGE Group) signed a letter of intent in February 2025 
to upgrade the mechanical, electrical and electronic systems of approximately 615 BMP-3 tracked armoured 
infantry fighting vehicles from Kurganmashzavod (part of the Rostec group) belonging to the United Arab 
Emirates Army.
	
 The group and Saudi Arabia’s SAMI reached an agreement in July 2025 for the production of 8x8 wheeled 
armoured vehicles and turrets in Saudi Arabia, with technology transfer.
Technology and Innovation
	
 FNSS, Belgium’s John Cockerill and Spain’s TSD (military vehicle design and production) and Grupo Oesía 
(digital and industrial engineering) signed a letter of intent in May 2025 to develop a future 6×6 armoured 
cavalry vehicle for the Spanish Army. This vehicle would be based on FNSS’s Pars 6x6 and equipped with a 
manned turret from John Cockerill.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

75
Business trends
	
 GE Aerospace, which became independent following the demerger of the General Electric (GE) conglomerate 
in April 2024, is the world’s leading supplier of engines for commercial aircraft (through CFM International, its 
joint venture with Safran) and is one of the world’s top three engine manufacturers for military aircraft, alongside 
Rolls-Royce and Pratt & Whitney (a division of RTX).
	
 GE Aerospace revenues reached $38.7 billion (€35.8 billion) in 2024, up 9.5% year-on-year in local currency. 
Growth was driven by civil activities (+14.5%), with the delivery of more than 1,900 engines and stronger 
demand for maintenance services. Military activities also performed well (+4.1%), despite a decline in engine 
deliveries (490 units in 2024, compared with 556 in 2023).
	
 GE Aerospace plans to invest approximately $1 billion (€924 million) in its manufacturing facilities (new 
machinery, building upgrades, increased use of innovative materials, etc.) and supply chain throughout 2025, 
50% more than in 2024. $200 million (€185 million) will be allocated to military engines.
	
 In June 2025, GE Aerospace reached an agreement with the American group Kratos to develop and produce 
the GEK800 and GEK1500 UAV engines, which could be used to power «loyal wingman» UCAVs, among 
other applications.
Home market
	
 In February 2025, GE Aerospace announced the completion of the detailed design review of its XA102 
adaptive cycle engine, developed as part of the U.S. Air Force’s Next Generation Adaptive Propulsion (NGAP) 
programme to design an engine for the future sixth-generation F-47 air superiority aircraft developed by Boeing 
(NGAD programme). The group can now move on to the next phase (assembly and testing of a demonstrator). 
Pratt & Whitney is working on a competing concept, the XA103 engine.
	
 In March 2025, the group secured a contract worth up to $5 billion (€4.6 billion) with the U.S. Air Force to supply 
F110-GE-129 turbojet engines for F-15 (Boeing) and F-16 (Lockheed Martin) fighter aircraft operated by the 
U.S. Air Force and allied countries under FMS procedures.
	
 In May 2025, the U.S. Army announced that it was likely to abandon the Improved Turbine Engine Program 
(ITEP) to re-engine more than 1,300 UH-60 Black Hawk (Sikorsky) and 600 AH-64 Apache (Boeing) helicopters 
for budgetary reasons. GE Aerospace was leading this programme with its T901 turboshaft engine, which 
casts doubt on the continued development of the engine.
Export markets
	
 Most of GE Aerospace’s export sales (55% of turnover in 2024) relate to civil engines, mainly in Europe and 
Asia. European subsidiaries include Italian aerospace company Avio Aero and British engineering company 
Dowty Propellers.
	
  In January 2025, GE Aerospace was awarded a contract to supply 210 T700 turboprop engines to power the 
96 AH-64E Apache Guardian helicopters (Boeing) currently being acquired by Poland.
	
 In April 2025, GE Aerospace signed a contract with HJ Shipbuilding & Construction to supply LM500 gas 
turbines to power the Republic of Korea Navy’s PKX-B Batch-II (Yoon Youngha-class) patrol boats numbered 
9 to 12 (two turbines per ship).
Technology and Innovation
	
 Self-financed R&D expenditure amounted to $1,286 million (€1,189 million) in 2024, representing 3.3% of 
revenues.
	
 In the United States, the Federal Aviation Administration (FAA) certified the Catalyst turboprop engine 
from subsidiary Avio Aero in February 2025. The Catalyst is set to power the future European MALE RPAS 
reconnaissance drone (Eurodrone programme).

78
Business trends
	
 General Atomics posted revenues of around $3.1 billion (€3 billion) in 2022 (latest figure available). In 2024, 
the group delivered one MQ-9A Reaper Extended Range UCAV to the U.S. Marine Corps and the second of 
16 MALE Protector RG Mk1 UAVs (armed version of the MQ-9B SkyGuardian UAV) ordered by the Royal Air 
Force in 2020.
	
 In March 2025, the group acquired the American company North Point Defence, which specialises in the 
development of signals intelligence (SIGINT) data exploitation software and sensor integration.
	
 In October 2025, General Atomics and Hanwha agreed to develop and produce the Mojave short take-off and 
landing (STOL) UAV for the global market. This partnership followed the successful flight demonstration of the 
Mojave in November 2024 from the Republic of Korea Navy’s Dokdo amphibious assault ship.
Home market
	
 General Atomics is a key drone supplier to the U.S. government. However, U.S. drone requirements are 
changing and the U.S. Air Force may stop purchases of MQ-9A Reaper UAVs in the near future, as they may 
no longer be suitable against new threats.
	
 General Dynamics awarded a subcontract to General Atomics in February 2025 for the production of Virginia 
Payload Tubes (VPT) missile launch systems for the three future Block VI Virginia-class nuclear-powered attack 
submarines. Each submarine will be equipped with two VPTs capable of firing six Tomahawk missiles each.
	
 In August 2025, General Atomics began flight testing its future XQ-67A UCAV, intended to act as a «loyal 
wingman» UCAV to the future sixth-generation F-47 fighter aircraft (Boeing). It is being developed as part of the 
U.S. Air Force’s Collaborative Combat Aircraft (CCA) programme, in which the group is competing with Anduril 
and its Fury UCAV. The winner of this programme is expected to be announced in 2026.
Export markets
	
 The group’s export strategy is constrained by the U.S. Administration’s ITAR regulations and Missile Technology 
Control Regime (MTCR). Most sales are made under the FMS (Foreign Military Sales) programme and mainly 
on the basis of unarmed offers. Western European contries (Spain, France, Italy, the Netherlands and the 
United Kingdom) are among the target markets for General Atomics.
	
 The group recently won three export contracts for its MQ-9B MALE surveillance UAVs. In December 2024, 
three MQ-9B SkyGuardian UAVs and two ground control stations were sold to Poland for $310 million 
(€287 million) and two MQ-9B SeaGuardian UAVs were sold to Japan. Denmark also acquired four MQ-9B 
SkyGuardian UAVs and three ground control stations in July 2025.
	
 General Atomics and the Belgian Ministry of Defence conducted the maiden flight of the first of four MQ-9B 
SkyGuardian MALE surveillance UAVs destined for Belgium at the Belgian Air Force’s Florennes air base in 
September 2025.
Technology and Innovation
	
 The U.S. Navy awarded General Atomics a contract in December 2024 to continue development of its Long 
Range Maneuvering Projectile (LRMP), a 155mm guided artillery projectile that can also be used for ISR 
missions. The range of this projectile would be 120 km, four times greater than standard artillery projectiles of 
this calibre. Until then, this R&D project had been self-financed by the group.
	
 In January 2025, General Atomics announced that it had successfully completed impact tests on its future 
nuclear thermal propulsion (NTP) reactor, which could be used for long-distance space missions (such as 
missions to Mars), at NASA’s Marshall Space Flight Center (MSFC) in Huntsville, Alabama.

81
GENERAL DYNAMICS
Business trends
	
 General Dynamics revenues reached $47.7 billion (€44.1 billion) in 2024, up 12.9% year-on-year in local 
currency. Growth was driven by the Aerospace division (+30.5%), which recorded higher sales of business 
jets (136 in 2024, compared with 111 in 2023), including the new G700 ultra-wide jet. The Marine Systems 
division also recorded strong growth (+15.1%), driven by shipbuilding activities for the U.S. Navy, particularly 
on the future Columbia-class SSBN and Virginia-class SSN submarines programmes.	
	
 The group has a very diversified portfolio of defence activities (69% of total revenues in 2024): surface ships 
and submarines, armoured vehicles, weapon systems and ammunition, cybersecurity, etc. The Aerospace 
division, however, is predominantly civilian-oriented (15% of defence turnover only in 2024).
Home market
	
 The United States remains General Dynamics’ largest market, accounting for 83% of total revenues in 2024. 
The U.S. Department of Defense alone accounted for 57% of revenues in 2024.
	
 In April 2025, the U.S. Navy awarded a $12.4 billion (€11.5 billion) contract to General Dynamics Electric Boat 
for the construction of two additional Virginia-class SSNs, named Baltimore (SSN-812) and Atlanta (SSN-813), 
to be delivered by 2036. These units will be the last of the Block V series. The group has five other Block V 
submarines at various stages of construction.
	
 General Dynamics was awarded two contracts worth a combined $1.43 billion (€1.32 billion) by the 
U.S. Army in the first half of 2025 for the production of 120mm calibre ammunition. M829A4 ammunition and 
Insensitive Munition High Explosive - Tracer (IMHE-T) ammunition are to be delivered by June 2029 and 2030, 
respectively.	
	
	
 In July 2025, the U.S. Navy ordered one additional Arleigh Burke-class Flight III destroyer, named Kyle 
Carpenter (DDG-148), from General Dynamics Bath Iron Works. Seven other units are under construction: 
Harvey C. Barnum Jr. (DDG-124), Louis H. Wilson Jr. (DDG-126), Patrick Gallagher (DDG-127), William 
Charette (DDG-130), Quentin Walsh (DDG-132), John E. Kilmer (DDG-134) and Richard G. Lugar (DDG-136).
	
 General Dynamics is currently competing for three U.S. Armed Forces programmes: XM30 Mechanized 
Infantry Combat Vehicle (up against Rheinmetall), Advanced Reconnaissance Vehicle (up against Textron), 
and Robotic Combat Vehicle (reconnaissance and escort UGV, up against Oshkosh Defense).
Export markets
	
 The group generated 17% of its revenues from exports in 2024 (without FMS programmes), mostly through 
General Dynamics European Land Systems (based in Germany, Austria, Denmark, Spain, the Czech 
Republic, Romania and Switzerland) and General Dynamics UK (United Kingdom). 
	
 General Dynamics European Land Systems - Santa Bárbara Sistemas signed a €373 million contract in 
January 2025 with the Ministry of Defence of Latvia for the production of 42 ASCOD 2 tracked armoured 
infantry vehicles.
	
 In February 2025, the Bundeswehr ordered 256 Piranha 5 8x8 wheeled armoured vehicles from General 
Dynamics European Land Systems - MOWAG, to be delivered from 2026 onwards. These vehicles will serve 
as support for the Bundeswehr’s future Tactical Wide Area Network for Land Based Operations (TaWAN LBO) 
mobile communication system, for which Rheinmetall is the prime contractor.
	
 The Danish Defence Acquisition and Logistics Organisation (DALO) awarded a contract in September 2025 to 
General Dynamics European Land Systems for the supply of an M3 amphibious bridge.
Technology and Innovation
	
 Self-financed R&D expenditure amounted to $565 million (€522 million) in 2024, representing 1.2% of revenues.

84
HEMERIA
Business trends
	
 Hemeria posted revenues of €70 million during fiscal year 2024-2025 (ended June 30th), up 9.9% year-on-
year, with approximately 95% of this revenue generated in France. The group benefits from a certain degree of 
recurrence thanks to long-term contracts in the space sector with Airbus, ArianeGroup, Beyond Gravity, OHB 
and Thales Alenia Space. Growth is also driven by new contracts won for Hyperion multi-sensor trajectory 
tracking turrets for the French defence procurement agency (DGA), satellite platforms (e.g. for Kinéis) and 
space equipment (e.g. for the Idéfix rover developed by the French and German space agencies).
	
 In October 2024, the group acquired a 70% stake in Astareon, a subsidiary of ONERA (French National Office 
for Aerospace Studies and Research) dedicated to the surveillance of space objects in low orbit. ONERA will 
initially retain a 30% stake in the company, renamed Hemeria Surveillance, before Hemeria gradually takes 
full control of the capital. With this acquisition, the group took over the Graves radar and the Medoc passive 
ground reception station. The data collected is then resold to institutional and private customers.
	
 In June 2025, Hemeria signed a letter of intent with the Canadian company ABB at the Paris Air Show to 
cooperate on solutions dedicated to low-orbit space surveillance. In particular, ABB’s payloads could be 
integrated into Hemeria’s satellite platforms.
Home market
	
 In October 2023, the French Space Agency (CNES) awarded a €10 million contract to the Hemeria 
Airship subsidiary for the production of 90 helium-filled stratospheric balloons, to be delivered between 
September 2025 and the end of 2027. Designed to collect data at altitudes of between 15 and 40 km, these 
balloons will be used for the CNES’ Stratéole-2 mission to study atmospheric phenomena at the equator.
	
 The DGA-EM has entrusted Hemeria with the design, production and MRO of its new trajectory tracking 
systems. In 2025, a trajectory tracking turret was installed on the Monge test and measurement building (BEM) 
and the first turrets were delivered to the DGA’s test centres.
	
 Hemeria is in charge of the YODA (Yeux en Orbite pour un Démonstrateur Agile) project, notified by CNES 
on behalf of the DGA, whose aim is to design and supply a demonstrator of two patrol nanosatellites that will 
operate in geostationary orbit to protect French military satellites from suspicious or even hostile manoeuvres. 
They will be operated by the French Space Command.
Export markets
	
 Hemeria has several international clients, such as Beyond Gravity, Israel Aerospace Industries and OHB. 
In addition to Europe, the group is targeting countries in the Middle East, Asia and Africa.
	
 The European Space Agency (ESA) awarded Hemeria a €9.8 million contract in November 2024 to 
supply space data on the ionosphere as part of the Space Weather programme, which aims to provide 
satellite operators with information on the potentially harmful effects of space weather on their navigation 
and communication systems. The group will provide its HP-IoT satellite platform, into which it will integrate 
electronic systems (radiation monitors, X-ray monitors, Langmuir probes, GNSS receivers, etc.). The satellite 
is scheduled for launch in 2026.
Technology and Innovation
	
 Hemeria Airship and CNES (project owner) successfully completed the first flight of the envelope alone, 
without manoeuvrability, in the stratosphere (at an altitude of 22 km) of the BalMan maneuvering stratospheric 
balloon from the Guiana Space Centre in Kourou in October 2024. A second flight is scheduled for late 2025 to 
test the platform with manoeuvrability and payload capacity, including several Hemeria subsystems (on-board 
computer, piloting interface, etc.). The objective for BalMan is to remain above a geographical area of interest, 
at an altitude of several tens of kilometres, for longer than a drifting balloon, aircraft or drone can do.

HENSOLDT
STRATEGIC HIGHLIGHTS
Business trends
	
 Hensoldt revenues increased 21.3% in 2024 to €2,240 million. Growth was mainly driven by the Sensors 
division (+23.5%), thanks to the integration of the German engineering group ESG from April 2024 and sales of 
TRML-4D air defence radars. Revenues in the Optronics division grew by 12.7% in 2024, mainly due to higher 
sales of civil solutions for industry and systems for land platforms.
	
 At the DSEI exhibition in London in September 2025, Hensoldt signed a letter of intent with the British group 
KX to jointly develop faster situational awareness systems for the German Armed Forces and allied countries. 
KX’s real-time analysis solutions will enable Hensoldt’s sensor data to be transformed into actionable 
intelligence more quickly.
Home market
	
 In January 2025, Hensoldt signed a new contract for the production of ECRS Mk 1 CAPTOR-E AESA radars for 
the German and Spanish Air Forces’ Typhoon fighter aircraft (Eurofighter), bringing the total amount received 
by the group for this programme to around €350 million. This radar made its maiden flight aboard a Spanish Air 
Force Typhoon aircraft in December 2024.
	
 In May 2025, Germany’s Federal Office of Bundeswehr Equipment (BAAINBw) awarded Hensoldt a contract 
to upgrade the capabilities of the ASUL anti-drone defence system, previously developed by ESG.
	
 In September 2025, the group delivered the first TRS-4D LR long-range radar, developed in partnership with 
ELTA Systems, to the German Navy’s Naval Engineering School in Parow. Three additional radars are then to 
be installed on the Sachsen-class F124 frigates (one radar per ship).
Export markets
	
 The group generated 43% of its revenues from exports in 2024, mainly in Europe.
	
 In March 2025, the Czech Republic ordered additional Taranis Adler C4 and fire support systems from 
Hensoldt for integration into armoured vehicles. Compared to those previously delivered, these systems 
include a new mapping service.
	
 Hensoldt received a €340 million order to supply TRML-4D and Spexer 2000 3D MkIII air defence radars to the 
Ukrainian Armed Forces. The TRML-4D radar is integrated into the IRIS-T SLM system (Diehl Defence), while 
the Spexer 2000 3D MkIII radar can be integrated into the Skyranger 30 system (Rheinmetall) or used on its 
own on a vehicle or fixed surveillance unit.
	
 In September 2025, the group signed an initial contract with an unspecified NATO country to supply a naval 
version of the Spexer 2000 radar. This version is dedicated to anti-drone defence and the detection of anti-ship 
missiles and submarine periscopes.
Technology and Innovation
	
 The group’s self-financed R&D expenditure amounted to €32 million in 2024, representing 1.4% of revenues.
	
 In November 2024, the EuroDASS consortium (Leonardo, ELT Group, Hensoldt and Indra Sistemas) presented 
details of the Praetorian eVolution future self-protection system for the Typhoon fighter aircraft. The upgrades 
(characterisation of complex threats, interfaces for an electronic attack pod, countermeasure system linked to 
an AESA radar, etc.) will enable the aircraft to remain in service beyond 2060.
	
 The group unveiled a new optronic sensor system for target acquisition and reconnaissance, named TAROSS, 
at the DSEI exhibition in London in September 2025. This system can be installed on remote-controlled 
weapon stations, unmanned ground vehicles and light reconnaissance vehicles.

90
HII (HUNTINGTON INGALLS INDUSTRIES)
Business trends
	
 HII revenues reached $11.5 billion (€10.7 billion) in 2024, virtually unchanged year-on-year in local currency 
(+0.7%). The Mission Technologies division recorded the strongest growth (+8.9%), ahead of shipbuilding 
activities (-2.7% for Newport News and +0.6% for Ingalls).
	
 HII partnered with Thales in September 2025 to develop underwater mine countermeasure capabilities by 
integrating the French group’s SAMDIS 600 sonar into the REMUS 620 medium-sized unmanned underwater 
vehicle. Equipped with this technology, the UUV will be able to carry out mine detection, classification and 
image transmission missions.	
 	
	
	
	
 In September 2025, HII and Babcock also signed a letter of intent to enable the British group’s WHLS torpedo 
handling and launch system to launch and recover REMUS UUVs from submarine torpedo tubes.
Home market
	
 HII operates almost exclusively in the U.S. naval defence sector, with the U.S. Navy and U.S. Coast Guard as 
its main customers.
	
 In January 2025, Ingalls began steel cutting for the San Antonio-class landing platform dock Philadelphia 
(LPD-32), the third unit in the Flight II class. The shipyard is still working on the construction of the two previous 
units, Harrisburg (LPD-30) and Pittsburgh (LPD-31).
	
 In March 2025, Ingalls launched a third Arleigh Burke Flight III destroyer for the U.S. Navy, named 
Jeremiah Denton (DDG-129). The ship is equipped with Raytheon’s (RTX) AN/SPY-6(V)1 Air and Missile 
Defense Radar (AMDR) and Lockheed Martin’s Aegis Baseline 10 combat system. The shipyard is still working 
on four other Flight III ships, Ted Stevens (DDG-128), George M. Neal (DDG-131), Sam Nunn (DDG-133) and 
Thad Cochran (DDG-135).
	
 In July 2025, Newport News launched the Virginia-class nuclear-powered attack submarine named Arkansas 
(SSN-800), its last Block IV unit. The shipyard is simultaneously building four Block V submarines: Oklahoma 
(SSN-802), Barb (SSN-804), Wahoo (SSN-806) and Silversides (SSN-807).
Export markets
	
 HII’s export sales are mainly made of unmanned underwater vehicles, which were sold to some 30 countries. 
Export sales include three REMUS 100 and five REMUS 300 UUVs for the Royal Navy (for underwater 
exploration, surveillance and mine countermeasures missions) in May 2024 and one REMUS 300 UUV for 
the German Navy. 
	
 The Mission Technologies division signed a five-year, $296 million (€274 million) contract in January 2025 with 
the U.S. Air Force in Europe and Africa (USAFE-AFAFRICA) to assist in the planning, integration and execution 
of command and control, ISR, and air and missile defence missions.
Technology and Innovation
	
 Self-financed R&D expenditure amounted to $27 million (€25 million) in 2024, representing 0.2% of revenues. 
Most of the R&D costs of the group’s programmes are funded by government contracts.
	
 In March 2025, the Mission Technologies division was commissioned by the U.S. Army to develop the 
architecture for a ground-based or vehicle-based high-energy laser (HEL) weapon system designed to 
counter unmanned aerial vehicles.
	
 Newport News unveiled a new unmanned surface vehicle named Romulus in September 2025. This USV, 
nearly 6 metres long, is designed for several types of collaborative actions with surface, underwater and 
aerial platforms, such as anti-UAS warfare, ISR missions, strike operations, and the launch and recovery of 
unmanned underwater and aerial vehicles.

93
Business trends
	
 Honeywell revenues reached $38.5 billion (€35.6 billion) in 2024, up 5% year-on-year in local currency. 
Three of the group’s four divisions reported higher sales, including Building Automation (construction, +8.4%) 
and Energy and Sustainability Solutions (solutions for fuel and chemical production, technological materials, 
etc., +3%). Revenues in the Aerospace Technologies division (civil and military aeronautics, space) grew 13.5%, 
exceeding their 2019 level, driven by the recovery in the civil aeronautics sector and the increase in deliveries 
of military equipment.
	
 In February 2025, Honeywell initiated a project to split the group into three independent entities listed in 
the United States, whith completion expected in the second half of 2026. These three entities will be (1) the 
Aerospace Technologies division, which will become a group focused entirely on aeronautics, defence and 
space, (2) the building and industrial activities (Building Automation and Industrial Automation divisions, 
excluding the personal protective equipment activities that were sold to Protective Industrial Products in 
May 2025, and the UOP segment of the Energy and Sustainability Solutions division), and (3) the Advanced 
Materials segment of the Energy and Sustainability Solutions division, which was floated on the stock market 
under the name Solstice Advanced Materials in October 2025.
Home market
	
 In 2024, Honeywell generated 43% of its revenues in the United States. Sales to the U.S. Department of 
Defense amounted to $3.8 billion (€3.5 billion), or 63% of the group’s defence activities.
	
 Under multi-year contracts, Honeywell is involved in the maintenance and modernisation of various key 
equipment for the U.S. Armed Forces. The group is in particular in charge of upgrading its T55 turboshaft 
engine that powers the U.S. Army’s CH-47 Chinook helicopters (development of the T55-GA-714C version, 
which provides a 22% increase in power and an 8% reduction in fuel consumption compared with the 
T55-GA-714A version).
	
 The U.S. Navy awarded Honeywell a $92 million (€85 million) contract in December 2024 to supply 
6,096 spare parts over five years for the F/A-18 Hornet fighter aircraft (Boeing), such as wheels, disc brakes, 
stators, hydraulic pistons, etc. The contract includes the sale of parts under FMS procedures to foreign 
operators of the aircraft (Canada, Spain, Finland, Switzerland, Kuwait and Malaysia).
	
 In June 2025, Bell (part of Textron) selected Honeywell’s 36-150 auxiliary power unit to power its future MV-75 
(formerly V-280 Valor) utility tiltrotor aircraft.	
	
 The group signed a $24 million (€22 million) contract with the U.S. Navy in September 2025 to produce spare 
parts for 69 auxiliary power units installed on F/A-18 Hornet fighter aircraft (Boeing).
Export markets
	
 Honeywell generated 57% of its revenues from exports in 2024. 43% of production is located outside the 
United States, mainly in Europe and Asia (but only 29% in the Aerospace Technologies division).
	
 MRO contracts are key drivers of export sales. They are supported by engine production licences and 
partnerships, for example with Japan’s KHI (for the T55 engines of the CH-47JA helicopters), Turkey’s Turkish 
Aerospace (for the CTS800 engines of the T129 ATAK and T625 Gökbey helicopters) and Taiwan’s AIDC (for 
the F125 engines of the F-CK-1 Ching-kuo multirole combat aircraft).
Technology and Innovation
	
 Self-financed R&D expenditure amounted to $1.5 billion (€1.4 billion) in 2024, representing 4% of revenues.

96
INDRA SISTEMAS
Business trends
	
 Indra revenues rose by 11.5% in 2024 to €4.8 billion. Revenues in the Defence division recorded strong growth 
(+26.2%) and exceeded €1 billion, thanks in particular to the ramp-up of the FCAS programme and to activities 
dedicated to simulation and space (Galileo programme).
	
 In February 2025, Indra reached an agreement to acquire a 89.68% stake in Spanish satellite operator 
Hispasat for €725 million. The transaction includes Hispasat’s 43% stake in Hisdesat, which manages services 
to governments (including military applications) and in which Indra already holds a 7% stake.
	
 In June 2025, Indra increased its stake in the TESS Defence joint venture, which manufactures Dragón 8x8 
armoured vehicles, to 51.01%. The three other shareholders (EM&E Group, GDELS-Santa Bárbara and SAPA) 
each retained 16.33% of the shares.
	
 In July 2025, Indra acquired the Spanish UAV manufacturer Aertec Defence & Aerial Systems (DAS), which 
TARSIS family of drones was tested by the Spanish Armed Forces.
Home market
	
 Indra and Rheinmetall agreed in May 2025 to cooperate on armoured vehicle projects for the Spanish Armed 
Forces. The two groups are already partners in the production and modernisation of various systems for 
Spanish Leopard 2 tanks.	
	
	
 Indra signed two contracts with the European Space Agency (ESA) in October 2025 to upgrade the S3TSR 
space surveillance radar to version V2 and to develop an electronic navigation receiver for low Earth orbit 
satellites, improving Galileo signal reception.
Export markets
	
 The group generated 49% of its sales from exports in 2024, mainly in the civilian sector.
	
 In February 2025, Indra was commissioned by Abu Dhabi Ship Building (ADSB), a subsidiary of UAE-based 
EDGE Group, to equip its FA-400 offshore patrol boat with 3D radar and electronic warfare and anti-drone 
systems.
	
 Indra signed two contracts to supply Indra Park Air T6 military radios: in Canada in February 2025 (supply of 
600 radios for more than €13 million) and in Brazil in May 2025 (supply of more than 800 radios).
	
 In April 2025, the Sultanate of Oman ordered Lanza 3D long-range early warning radars from Indra 
to strengthen its air defence system. The contract includes the supply of fixed radars and radio voice 
communication systems.
	
 The first Lanza-N 3D radar, a naval version of the Lanza radar, was commissioned in September 2025 by Indra 
and Tata Advanced Systems on an Indian Navy ship.
Technology and Innovation
	
 The group’s total R&D expenditure amounted to €428 million in 2024, representing 8.8% of revenues.
	
 Indra is developing digital self-protection systems for aircraft and helicopters that are more effective against 
missile fire. The solution combines the ALR-400 radar warning receiver with the InWarner and InShield DIRCM 
(Directed Infrared Countermeasure) systems.
	
 In October 2025, Indra conducted tests on its NIMBUS system, a combat cloud connecting different sensors, 
platforms and simulators offering entities generated by artificial intelligence. The group is also conducting 
flight tests of the Valero Multi-Purpose Air Vehicle (MAV), designed in particular for electronic warfare missions.
	
 During the Spanish Army’s ATLAS25 exercise in October 2025, Indra presented its anti-drone systems 
deployed on land (ARACNE) and on board combat ships (CROW).

ISRAEL AEROSPACE INDUSTRIES (IAI)
Business trends
	
 IAI revenues reached $6,112 million (€5,649 million) in 2024, up 14.7% year-on-year in US dollars. Growth was 
driven by military activites, which grew by 15.5% year-on-year (in US dollars) and accounted for 85% of sales. 
During the year, the group delivered BARAK MX air defence and missile defence systems to Morocco and 
Cyprus. It also continued integration of the BARAK MX system on the Israeli Navy’s Sa’ar 6 corvettes, as well 
as development work on the future Arrow 4 missile defence system in partnership with the Missile Defence 
Agency in the United States.
	
 In December 2024, IAI’s CEO confirmed that the group was ready to float up to 49% of its capital on the stock 
market, exclusively for domestic investors, and that it was awaiting authorisation from the Israeli government 
for this operation.
	
 In April 2025, IAI and India’s DCX Systems agreed to create a joint venture, called ELTX, which will be 
responsible for technology transfers in airborne radars and ground systems for the benefit of the Indian 
defence industry as part of the Atmanirbhar Bharat and Make in India initiatives.
	
 In May 2025, IAI signed a letter of intent with Hellenic Aerospace Industry to offer its large autonomous 
underwater vehicle BlueWhale to the Hellenic Navy.
Home market
	
 IAI generated 34% of its turnover in its domestic market in 2024. The group is a key partner of the Israel Defense 
Forces and the second-largest national defence group, behind Elbit Systems and ahead of Rafael.
	
 The Ministry of Defense of Israel ordered Arrow 3 missile defence system interceptor missiles from IAI in 
December 2024 and July 2025.  
	
 In the summer of 2025, two satellites produced by IAI were placed into orbit on behalf of the Israel Defense 
Forces: the Dror-1 telecommunications satellite (by SpaceX’s Falcon 9 Block 5 launcher) in July and the Ofek-19 
reconnaissance satellite (by the group’s Shavit-2 micro-launcher) in September.
Export markets
	
 In December 2024, the European Border and Coast Guard Agency (Frontex) awarded a four-year contract to 
Airbus and IAI to continue their surveillance missions over the Mediterranean Sea using the group’s Maritime 
Heron MALE UAS.
	
 IAI signed a €560 million contract in December 2024 with Slovakia to supply its BARAK MX air and missile 
defence system.
	
 In December 2024, the group signed a $7.2 million (€6.7 million) contract with the Colombian Aerospace Force 
for the maintenance of its Kfir C.10/12 fighter aircraft, 11 of which are in service, until July 2026.
	
 In February 2025, the Bundeswehr began construction work on the infrastructure for IAI’s Arrow 3 missile 
defence system at the Holzdorf air base (south of Berlin). This work is expected to be completed in 2028. 
Two other sites in Germany, which have not yet been specified, will also host the Arrow 3 system.
	
 In March 2025, the U.S. Department of Defense awarded a contract to the ELTA North America subsidiary for 
the supply of its M3S2 (Multi-Mission/Multi-Sensor Suite) protection suite for land vehicles for testing purposes. 
This suite includes an AESA radar for detecting ground and air threats (including small drones), EO/IR sensors 
for identifying and tracking threats, and an acoustic fire detection system.
Technology and Innovation
	
 Self-financed R&D expenditure amounted to $333 million (€308 million) in 2024, representing 5.4% of revenues.

102
ITP AERO
Business trends
	
 ITP Aero posted revenues of €1,612 million in 2024, up 23.5% year-on-year, thanks in particular to the strong 
performance of the civil aeronautics market, as well as defence and maintenance activities. Growth was also 
driven by the acquisition in February 2024 of the American company BP Aero, which provides after-sales 
services for civil and military aircraft engines. The group is a subcontractor to Rolls-Royce for the Trent family 
of turboshaft engines used on Airbus and Boeing passenger aircraft, and to the U.S. groups GE Aerospace, 
Honeywell and Pratt & Whitney, for whom it produces engine parts for business jets.
	
 In October 2024, the group inaugurated an extension to its Querétaro site in Mexico dedicated to the production 
of cast parts and logistics activities. Following this extension, up to 250 new employees will join the site, which 
already has more than a thousand employees, within three years.
	
 In February 2025, ITP Aero inaugurated an R&D centre in Zamudio (Spain). Named ADMIRE, this centre is 
dedicated to 3D printing technologies, in particular for the NEFE propulsion system of the future European Next 
Generation Fighter aircraft (a programme led by MTU Aero Engines and Safran, partners in the EUMET joint 
venture), but also for civil aviation. This centre will employ more than 150 people.
	
 Turkish Aerospace signed letters of intent with several Spanish groups in May 2025 as part of the Spanish Air 
and Space Force’s acquisition of 30 Hürjet advanced trainer and light attack aircraft. ITP Aero will be involved 
in the propulsion part of this programme.
Home market
	
 ITP Aero is Spain’s leading engine manufacturer. In the defence sector, the group operates mostly through 
partnerships with the other main European engine manufacturers (Avio Aero, MTU Aero Engines, Rolls-Royce 
and/or Safran), to which it supplies parts and components. ITP Aero is part of several consortiums: EuroJet 
Turbo (EJ200 turbofan engine for the Typhoon fighter), Europrop International (TP400-D6 turboprop for the 
A400M Atlas transport aircraft) and MTRI (MTR390-E turboshaft engine for the Tiger attack helicopter). 
The group also assembles the EJ200 and CT7-8F5 engines for Spain’s Typhoon aircraft and NH90 helicopters 
respectively.
	
 In December 2024, the EuroJet Turbo consortium (comprising ITP Aero, Avio Aero, MTU Aero Engines and 
Rolls-Royce) signed a contract with NATO Eurofighter & Tornado Management Agency (NETMA) to supply 
59 EJ200 turbojet engines to power the 25 Typhoon fighter aircraft ordered that same month by the Spanish Air 
and Space Force (Halcón II programme). The various modules of the turbojet engines will be produced by the 
four consortium members, with final assembly carried out by ITP Aero at its site in Ajalvir (Spain) and deliveries 
scheduled between 2029 and 2034.
Export markets
	
 In export markets, ITP Aero supplies GE Aerospace with components for the F414-GE-400 (F/A-18E/F Super 
Hornet fighter aircraft) and J85 (F-5E/F Tiger II fighter aircraft and T-38 Talon trainer aircraft) turbojet engines, 
and for the LM2500 gas turbine installed on several frigates (FREMM, Álvaro de Bazán-class, Independence-
class, etc.).
	
 The Royal Netherlands Air and Space Force awarded a contract in May 2025 to ITP Aero for the maintenance 
and repair of the T700-701D turboshaft engines (GE Aerospace) which power its AH-64E Apache attack 
helicopters (Boeing). The work will be carried out at ITP Aero’s site in Albacete (Spain).
Technology and Innovation
	
 The group’s self-financed R&D expenditure amounted to €102 million in 2024, representing 6.3% of revenues.

105
IVECO DEFENCE VEHICLES
Business trends
	
 Iveco Defence Vehicles (IDV) once again recorded strong growth in 2024. Revenue rose by 15.1% year-on-
year to reach €1,133 million, a record level and up 50.7% since 2022. Over the year, the division delivered 
around 3,600 military vehicles (Centauro II 8x8 tank destroyers for Italy and Brazil; VBTP-MR Guarani 6x6 
amphibious armoured vehicles for Brazil; 4x4 light vehicles for Austria, the Netherlands and Sweden; logistics 
vehicles for Germany and Romania, etc.) and civilian vehicles (light vehicles for the police, the Carabinieri and 
the Guardia di Finanza, and ASTRA trucks).
	
 Iveco Group reached an agreement with Leonardo in July 2025 for the sale of IDV for €1.7 billion. 
The transaction is expected to close by March 31, 2026. However, the military truck production business could 
be resold by Leonardo to Rheinmetall at a later date.
Home market
	
 Directly and through the Iveco-OTO Melara consortium (CIO), Iveco Defence Vehicles is a major supplier 
of land vehicles to the Italian Army. A major acquisition programme is planned for Centauro II and Freccia 
armoured vehicles, produced by the CIO consortium (with a total value of €2.2 billion over the 2020-2032 
period), as well as VTLM Lince 2 multirole vehicles.
	
 IDV signed a €755 million contract in December 2024 with the Italian Army for the supply of 1,453 4x4, 8x8 
and 10x10 logistic trucks in several configurations (troop transport, equipment transport, water or fuel tankers, 
breakdown or towing vehicles, etc.). Deliveries will be spread out between 2025 and 2038.
	
 In December 2024, the Italian Land Armament Directorate (DAT) awarded the CIO consortium a contract to 
supply 76 VBM Freccia Plus 8x8 armoured infantry fighting vehicles, bringing the number of units ordered 
to 152. Compared to previous versions, these vehicles will be armed with OTO Melara’s new 30mm X-Gun 
cannon, will have a more powerful engine (720 hp compared to 550 hp) and improved protection, and will 
incorporate a C4I system.
	
 In July 2025, the CIO consortium delivered the first Ariete main battle tank upgraded to the C2 standard to the 
Italian Army. These tanks have a 20% increase in engine power (reaching 1,500 hp) and a new braking system, 
as well as several modernised systems (digital fire control system, software radio, etc.).
Export markets
	
 Iveco Defence Vehicles targets export markets, mainly in Europe and Brazil but also in the United States 
through its partnership with BAE Systems for the production of the Amphibious Combat Vehicle (ACV) for the 
U.S. Marine Corps.
	
 The Spanish Marine Infantry selected IDV’s SuperAV in March 2025 for its programme to replace its 19 AAV-7 
amphibious armoured vehicles (BAE Systems). The SuperAV could be produced locally in Iveco Group 
factories with the participation of Spanish subcontractors, such as Indra for the electronic components.
	
 IDV won a contract in May 2025 from the Royal Netherlands Army to supply 785 6x4 and 8x8 military logistic 
trucks, to be delivered between 2027 and 2029.
Technology and Innovation
	
 The NATO Support and Procurement Agency (NSPA) awarded IDV a three-year R&D contract in July 2025 
to design robotic and autonomous systems based on the LMV 4x4 multirole light vehicle and the Viking 6x6 
multirole unmanned ground vehicle.

108
JOHN COCKERILL
Business trends
	
 John Cockerill revenues grew 18% in 2024 to €1,417 million, of which approximately 30% from defence 
activities. Over the year, the group booked €1,697 million in new orders (civil and military sectors combined), 
including approximately €500 million contributed by Arquus (acquired in July 2024).
	
 The share of defence revenues declined in the early 2020s following John Cockerill’s acquisitions of several 
civil companies, including the Hamon group in 2022 (industrial heat recovery systems, with operations in 
Belgium and France) and the French company STIN in 2023 (welding and pipework for the nuclear sector). 
However, this share rose again in 2024 thanks to the acquisition of the French manufacturer of high-mobility 
armoured vehicles Arquus (turnover of around €600 million) from Volvo for an estimated €300 million.
	
 In March 2025, John Cockerill and the Indian company Electro Pneumatics & Hydraulics Ltd (EPHL) agreed 
to set up a joint venture that will focus on the production, assembly and commissioning of turrets armed with a 
105mm calibre gun for the Indian Army’s Zorawar light tanks. John Cockerill Défense will hold a 60% stake in 
this future joint venture, which will be based near Mumbai.
	
 In May 2025, John Cockerill, Turkey’s FNSS and Spain’s TSD (military vehicle design and production) and 
Grupo Oesía (digital and industrial engineering) signed a letter of intent at the FEINDEF exhibition in Madrid to 
develop a future 6×6 armoured cavalry vehicle for the Spanish Army. This vehicle would be based on FNSS’ 
Pars 6x6 vehicle and equipped with a manned turret from John Cockerill.
Home market
	
 The group is a key partner of the Belgian Ministry of Defence for land programmes (design, production and 
integration of turrets, maintenance, simulation, training, etc.), just like FN Browning Group (production of assault 
rifles and machine guns).
	
 Through Arquus, John Cockerill is also participating, together with KNDS France, Thales and Mol, in the Belgian 
CaMo (Motorised Capability) programme to procure 406 Griffon VBMR/MEPAC vehicles, 60 Jaguar EBRC 
vehicles and 28 CAESAR self-propelled howitzers from 2025 onwards. The contract includes the supply of the 
chassis, engine, suspension and T-40 remote controlled weapon station for the Griffon and Jaguar vehicles, 
the chassis for the CAESAR artillery systems, as well as training, instruction and maintenance services.
Export markets
	
 The Arquus subsidiary, KNDS France and Thales are jointly in charge of the Scorpion programme for the 
supply of wheeled armoured vehicles to the French Army. An order for 1,872 Griffon VBMR/MEPAC and 
300 Jaguar EBRC vehicles is expected, and around half of the vehicles should be delivered by 2025. 
151 Griffon and 35 Jaguar vehicles were delivered in 2024, bringing the total number of vehicles delivered 
since 2019 to 726 and 95 respectively.
	
 In April 2025, Arquus delivered the first five Grizzly reconnaissance vehicles and the first Pégase vehicle for 
the Special Forces, produced in series at its Limoges plant (in southwestern France). Arquus plans to deliver 
50 Grizzly and 40 Pégase vehicles to the Special Operations Command (COS) and the French Army in 2025.
Technology and Innovation
	
 At the Indo Defence exhibition in Jakarta (Indonesia) in June 2025, the group presented a 6x6 VAB Mk3 
multi-purpose tactical armoured vehicle from Arquus equipped with the 1030 turret from John Cockerill 
Défense. This vehicle was produced locally by PT Pindad with technical assistance from Arquus as part of the 
Indonesian Army’s Anoa 3 programme.

111
KAWASAKI HEAVY INDUSTRIES (KHI)
Business trends
	
 Kawasaki Heavy Industries (KHI) posted revenues of 2.129 trillion yen (€13 billion) during fiscal year 2024-2025 
(ended March 31st), up 15.1% year-on-year in local currency. In particular, revenues in the Aerospace Systems 
division rose by 43.3%, driven by increased orders from Japan’s Ministry of Defense, Boeing (aerostructures 
and components for passenger aircraft), for which KHI is a subcontractor, and civil engine manufacturers.
	
 The group’s military activities are concentrated in two divisions: Aerospace Systems (military aircraft and 
helicopters) and Energy Solution & Marine Engineering (submarines). In the coming years, KHI intends 
to benefit from an increased level of orders in its domestic market. Japan’s Ministry of Defense intends to 
spend between 40 and 43 trillion yen (between €283 and €305 billion) on defence between April 2023 and 
March 2028, compared to 27.5 trillion yen (€213 billion) between April 2018 and March 2023.
Home market
	
 KHI generated 41% of its revenues in its domestic market in 2024-2025, including almost all of its defence 
activities. The group is a key supplier to the Japan Self-Defense Forces as prime contractor for the C-2 tactical 
transport aircraft and P-1 maritime patrol aircraft programmes. KHI is also the co-builder, alongside Mitsubishi 
Heavy Industries (MHI), of the Taigei-class attack submarines, powered by lithium-ion batteries. Both groups 
are tasked with building four submarines each (eight units in total).
	
 In October 2024, Japan’s Acquisition, Technology & Logistics Agency (ATLA) ordered 17 CH-47J/JA Chinook 
Block II heavy transport helicopters from KHI (to be assembled locally under licence from Boeing) for 
232 billion yen (€1,419 million), including 12 and 5 helicopters for the Japan Ground and Air Self-Defense 
Forces respectively.
	
 In December 2024, Japan’s Ministry of Defense presented its budget for the 2025 fiscal year (which begins 
on April 1st). It includes the acquisition of several items of equipment from KHI, in particular two P-1 maritime 
patrol aircraft for 84.8 billion yen (€518 million), one RC-2 electronic intelligence (ELINT) aircraft for 45.7 billion 
yen (€279 million) and Middle Range MPM anti-tank missiles for 5.6 billion yen (€34 million).
	
 The second Taigei-class lithium-ion-powered attack submarine built by KHI, named Raigei (SS-516), entered 
service with the Japan Maritime Self-Defense Force in March 2025. This submarine is the first of its class to 
be equipped with the new 12V 25/31 diesel engine supplied by the group. KHI is building a third submarine 
(SS-518, not yet named), which is scheduled to enter service in 2027.
Export markets
	
 KHI generated 59% of its revenues from exports in 2024-2025, mainly in the civil sector. In defence, KHI intends 
to win new markets by offering the Soryu-class conventionally-powered attack submarine, the C-2 tactical 
transport aircraft and the P-1 maritime patrol aircraft to foreign armed forces, although yet unsuccessfullly.
	
 The Italian Air Force has expressed interest in acquiring the group’s P-1 maritime patrol aircraft to regain the 
anti-submarine capabilities it lost when its Br 1150 Atlantic aircraft (Breguet) were withdrawn from service in 
2017. If acquired, these aircraft would be equipped with Italian systems (Leonardo’s ATOS mission system or 
Fincantieri’s MU90 Impact light torpedo).
Technology and Innovation
	
 The group’s R&D expenditure amounted to 49 billion yen (€299 million) in 2024-2025, representing 2.3% of 
revenues.
	
 In May 2025, KHI presented a naval version of its anti-drone laser weapon at the DSEI Japan exhibition in 
Chiba. Japan’s Acquisition, Technology & Logistics Agency could soon initiate a programme to develop such 
a weapon, for which the group hopes to be selected.

114
KNDS
Business trends
	
 KNDS posted revenues of approximately €3.8 billion in 2024, up 16.9% year-on-year. Growth was driven by 
deliveries of Jaguar, Griffon and Serval armoured vehicles to France; RCH 155 self-propelled howitzers to 
Ukraine; Boxer armoured vehicles to Lithuania; and CAESAR artillery systems for export. Following the increase 
in new order intake (€11.2 billion, compared to €7.8 billion in 2023), the group’s order book reached a solid 
€23.5 billion at the end of 2024.
	
 KNDS France signed a definitive agreement with Texelis in July 2025 to acquire its defence activities 
(e.g. the chassis for the Serval 4x4 armoured vehicle) in spring 2026. Texelis is based in Limoges, in 
southwestern France.
	
 In September 2025, KNDS CEO Jean-Paul Alary announced that the group could be listed on the Frankfurt 
Stock Exchange (Germany) around June 2026.
Home markets (France, Germany)
	
 KNDS France, Arquus (part of John Cockerill) and Thales are jointly in charge of the Scorpion programme for 
the supply of wheeled armoured vehicles to the French Army. An order for 1,872 Griffon VBMR/MEPAC and 
300 Jaguar EBRC vehicles is expected, and around half of the vehicles should be delivered by 2025. 
151 Griffon and 35 Jaguar vehicles were delivered in 2024, bringing the total number of vehicles delivered 
since 2019 to 726 and 95 respectively.
	
 In December 2024, Germany’s Federal Office of Bundeswehr Equipment (BAAINBw) awarded KNDS 
Deutschland and Rheinmetall a six-year, €1.98 billion contract (split roughly in half) to integrate new digital radio 
transmitters and new C2 systems into more than 10,000 Bundeswehr armoured vehicles (D-LBO programme).
	
 In April 2025, KNDS Deutschland, KNDS France, Rheinmetall and Thales established a temporary consortium, 
each with a 25% stake, to develop the future MGCS main battle tank, which is expected to be operational in 
2045. Known as MGCS Project Company, this consortium is based in Cologne, Germany.
Export markets
	
 Together with Thales and Arquus, KNDS France is participating in the CaMo (Motorised Capability) programme 
to supply the Belgian Army with 382 Griffon VBMR and 60 Jaguar EBRC vehicles from 2025 onwards. 
The €1.6 billion contract includes training and maintenance services. Flemish company Mol is responsible for 
the final assembly of the Griffon vehicles and the Jaguar’s T-40 turrets in Belgium.
	
 In November 2024, the Swiss Army selected KNDS Deutschland’s 155mm Artillery Gun Mobile module, which 
will be integrated onto 10x10 chassis of Piranha IV armoured vehicles from MOWAG (a subsidiary of General 
Dynamics).
	
 KNDS Deutschland received two orders for its Leopard 2A8 main battle tank in December 2024: 44 units for 
Lithuania worth €950 million (including the supply of spare parts) and 44 units for Sweden. In addition, Sweden 
notified KNDS Deutschland of a contract to upgrade 66 Leopard 2A5 tanks (new weapon system, higher level 
of protection, etc.), starting in 2028.
	
 The Czech Republic acquired 44 Leopard 2A8 main battle tanks from KNDS Deutschland in September 2025 
for €1.34 billion (including logistics services). The contract includes an option for 14 additional units.
	
 KNDS France signed two contracts for its 155mm CAESAR NG 6x6 artillery system in December 2025: 
18 units each for Croatia and Lithuania. Croatia also ordered 15 Serval 4x4 light multirole armoured vehicles.
Technology and Innovation
	
 In September 2025, the EuroTrophy consortium, comprising KNDS Deutschland and Rafael, announced the 
successful integration of the Trophy Active Protection System (APS) into an 8x8 Boxer wheeled armoured 
vehicle.

117
Business trends
	
 Kongsberg revenues rose 20.3% in local currency in 2024, to NOK 48.9 billion (€4,203 million). Revenues in the 
Kongsberg Defence & Aerospace division grew at a similar pace (+20.1%), thanks in particular to the production 
of NASAMS air defence systems (for Hungary, Lithuania, Qatar and Australia) and aerostructures for the 
F-35 Lightning II fighter aircraft. Aircraft maintenance operations also contributed to this growth. The division 
generated revenues of NOK 19 billion (€1,637 million) in 2024, accounting for 39% of the group’s total turnover, 
and had 4,648 employees.
	
 Kongsberg and Thales signed an agreement in June 2025 to create a joint venture specialising in secure 
communications. This future company is expected to be operational in 2026 pending approval by competition 
regulators and will combine the activities of the two groups in Norway. It will employ 350 people (including 
220 from Thales) in Oslo, Asker and Trondheim and is expected to generate initial revenues of €130 million.
	
 In July 2025, the Kongsberg Discovery division announced that production of HUGIN autonomous underwater 
vehicles for civil and military applications was to begin soon in Lynnwood (United States) in order to take 
advantage of the growing demand for these systems in the country.
	
 In July 2025, the group partnered with Polish company Advanced Protection Systems to develop and produce 
anti-drone systems. It will contribute its expertise in air defence systems, while Advanced Protection Systems 
will supply its FIELDctrl radars and SKYctrl anti-drone systems.
Home market
	
 The group generated 18% of its turnover in its domestic market in 2024. Defence revenues generated in 
Norway reached NOK 4 billion (€348 million), representing 21% of total defence revenues.
	
 Norway received its fifty-second and final F-35 Lightning II fighter aircraft from Lockheed Martin in April 2025. 
It also unveiled its first JSM cruise missile, developed by Kongsberg, the only missile in its class that can be 
integrated into the aircraft.
Export markets
	
 Kongsberg generated 79% of its defence sales from exports in 2024. The group’s main export market is North 
America (39% of defence revenues), ahead of Europe excluding Norway (29%).
	
 In January 2025, the Danish Defence Acquisition and Logistics Organisation (DALO) acquired Kongsberg’s 
NSM anti-ship missiles for NOK 2.1 billion (€181 million) to arm Iver Huitfeldt-class frigates.
	
 The German Air Force ordered JSM cruise missiles from the group in June 2025 for NOK 6.5 billion 
(€559 million) to arm its F-35 fighter aircraft.
	
 The U.S. Marine Corps awarded Kongsberg a $118 million (€109 million) contract in August 2025 to supply 
PROTECTOR RT20 remote controlled weapon stations (armed with a 30mm cannon) for installation on 
BAE Systems’ ACV-30 8x8 amphibious armoured vehicles.
	
 In August 2025, the group signed a contract with ArianeGroup to produce critical components for the Ariane 6 
launcher over the next three years, namely Optical Safety Barriers (OSB) and Upper Part Attachment (UPPA) 
mechanisms.
	
 Kongsberg Maritime won two contracts in the summer of 2025 to supply propeller systems for naval vessels: 
with Damen for the Colombian Navy’s future frigates in June and with Irving Shipbuilding for the Royal 
Canadian Navy’s future River-class destroyers in September.
Technology and Innovation
	
 Kongsberg Defence & Aerospace’s R&D expenditure amounted to NOK 195 million (€17 million) in 2024, 
representing 1% of the division’s revenues.

KONGSBERG

[Page 121 / 226 | chars: 3596]
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

120
Business trends
	
 Korea Aerospace Industries (KAI) posted revenues of 3.634 trillion won (€2,463 million) in 2024, down 4.9% 
year-on-year in local currency. This decline was mainly due to a drop in international aircraft sales (-38.8% year-
on-year), with, for example, a year-and-a-half delay in the delivery of the FA-50PL aircraft ordered by Poland. 
Helicopter sales decreased slightly (-0.9%) despite the delivery of the first two LAH-1 Miron helicopters to the 
Republic of Korea Army, whereas aerostructures sales increased by 5.6%.
	
 South Korea and Indonesia reached an agreement in June 2025 to reduce the latter’s share in the financing 
of the KF-21 Boramae / F-33 fighter aircraft programme from 1.7 trillion won (€1.15 billion) to 600 billion won 
(€407 million), or 7.5% of the total cost of the project. In return, PT Dirgantara Indonesia, which is expected to 
assemble 48 aircraft for the Indonesian Air Force, will receive fewer technology transfers from KAI.
Home market
	
 As South Korea’s sole aircraft manufacturer, KAI is involved in the main programmes of the Republic of Korea 
Air Force (ROKAF). In particular, the group is in charge of developping the future KF-21 Boramae fighter 
aircraft, in partnership with PT Dirgantara Indonesia, as well as the future Marine Attack Helicopter (MAH) for 
the Republic of Korea Marine Corps and Mine Countermeasure Helicopter (MCH) for the Republic of Korea 
Navy.
	
 In April 2025, SpaceX’s Falcon 9 Block 5 launcher placed the fourth satellite of South Korea’s Defence 
Acquisition Program Administration (DAPA) Project 425, assembled by KAI, into low Earth orbit. A fifth satellite 
is to be launched at a date yet to be specified.
	
 In June 2025, South Korea’s DAPA ordered a second batch of 20 KF-21 Boramae fighter aircraft from KAI for 
2.39 trillion won (€1.62 billion), to be delivered by 2028.
Export markets
	
 The group generated 41% of its revenues from exports in 2024, mainly through the sale of aircraft and 
aerostructures.
	
 In December 2024, KAI won its first export contract for its KUH-1 Surion transport helicopter, with two units 
ordered by the Ministry of Interior of Iraq for 135.6 billion won (€92 million), including the adaptation of the 
aircraft for firefighting missions and the provision of maintenance training services. Deliveries are scheduled 
to be completed by March 2029.
	
 The group signed a contract worth $64 million (€59 million) in March 2025 with the Indonesian Air Force to 
extend the service life of 20 KT-1B Woongbi basic trainer aircraft.
	
 KAI signed a 975.3 billion won (€661 million) contract in June 2025 with the Department of National Defense 
of the Philippines to supply 12 FA-50PH Fighting Eagle Block 20 light fighter aircraft, with deliveries scheduled 
until June 2030.
Technology and Innovation
	
 Self-financed R&D expenditure amounted to 80.5 billion won (€55 million) in 2024, representing 2.2% of 
revenues.
	
 The MAH attack helicopter prototype, derived from the MUH-1 Marineon, made its maiden flight in 
December 2024, lasting 20 minutes and reaching a maximum altitude of 30 metres. It is expected to be 
operational in the second half of 2026.
	
 The MCH mine countermeasure helicopter prototype, also derived from the MUH-1 Marineon, made its 
maiden flight in June 2025. Development is expected to be completed in the second half of 2026. KAI will 
become the third group to offer this type of helicopter, alongside Leonardo and Sikorsky (a subsidiary of 
Lockheed Martin).	 	
STRATEGIC HIGHLIGHTS
KOREA AEROSPACE INDUSTRIES (KAI)

123
Business trends
	
 KSOE revenues reached 25.539 trillion won (€17.3 billion) in 2024, up 19.9% year-on-year in local currency. 
The Shipbuilding division recorded a 24.7% increase during the year. Hyundai Heavy Industries (HHI), the only 
KSOE shipyard involved in military shipbuilding, posted revenues of 14.476 trillion won in 2024 (€9.8 billion, up 
21.1% year-on-year in local currency).
	
 HHI signed two letters of intent in April 2025: one with the American classification society ABS to evaluate the 
designs and ensure their compliance with international standards for the HDF-3200 frigate, the HDP-2200 
offshore patrol vessel and the HDA-24000 auxiliary vessel, which could facilitate their sales in the United States 
and allied countries, and the other with the Peruvian public group SIMA to jointly develop a new class of small 
submarine based on the HDS-1500 design for the Peruvian Navy.
Home market
	
 HHI is a major supplier to the Republic of Korea Navy (ROKN), alternating or competing (depending on the 
programme) with its domestic peer Hanwha Ocean.
	
 The fourth KDX-III Sejong Daewang-class stealth destroyer and first unit of the second batch built by 
HHI, named Jeongjo Daewang, entered service with the ROKN in November 2024. The fifth unit, named 
Dasan Jeong Yak-yong, was launched in September 2025.
	
 In December 2024, HHI delivered the first FFX-III frigate, named Chungnam, to the ROKN. However, 
SK Oceanplant and Hanwha Ocean will supply the following ships (three and two units respectively).
	
 In June 2025, HHI delivered the submarine Yun Bong-Gil (SS-077) to the ROKN after refurbishment work that 
began in January 2023. The submarine is the fifth unit of the Son Won-il class, based on the Type 214 from 
tkMS, and was built under licence by HHI.
Export markets
	
 HHI has a marginal export presence. However, the shipyard won contracts, in 2022 and 2024 respectively, 
with the Philippine Navy to supply six HDP-2200+ offshore patrol vessels, and with the Peruvian Navy to supply 
four ships: one 3,400-tonne frigate, one 2,200-tonne offshore patrol vessel and two 1,400-tonne landing ships.
	
 In January 2025, Peru’s SIMA began construction of the first of four vessels ordered in April 2024 at the Callao 
shipyard, with technical assistance from HHI. Delivery of the vessel is expected from the end of 2026.
	
 HHI and the Portuguese Navy entered into a strategic partnership in April 2025 for the construction and repair 
of naval vessels, and in particular to design two small submarines based on HHI’s HDS-2300, HDS-1500 or 
HDS-800 submarine.
	
 The first frigate ordered from HHI by the Philippine Navy, named Miguel Malvar, entered service in May 2025. 
The second unit, Diego Silang, was delivered in September 2025. In addition, HHI launched the first of six 
HDP-2200+ offshore patrol vessels, named Rajah Sulayman, for the Philippine Navy at its Ulsan shipyard in 
June 2025. The OPV is scheduled for delivery in March 2026, with the remaining five to be delivered by 2028.
Technology and Innovation
	
 Self-financed R&D expenditure amounted to 194 billion won (€132 million) in 2024, representing 0.8% of 
revenues.
	
 In April 2025, the ROKN awarded HHI a contract to design an unmanned surface vehicle by December 2025. 
This USV will be able to carry out reconnaissance and close combat missions in place of manned ships. 
It will be approximately 30 metres long with a displacement of 100 tonnes, will be electrically powered and will 
incorporate artificial intelligence components supplied by the American company Anduril.

L3HARRIS TECHNOLOGIES
Business trends
	
 L3Harris Technologies revenues grew 9.8% in local currency in 2024, to $21.3 billion (€19.7 billion), driven in 
part by the full-year inclusion of Aerojet Rocketdyne. Higher sales of tactical and high-speed communication 
systems, as well as night vision systems, also contributed to revenue growth.
	
 L3Harris Technologies signed a letter of intent with Thales UK in May 2025 to develop a short-range air defence 
C2 system for the British Armed Forces, combining the group’s Target Orientated Tracking System with 
Thales UK’s Agile C4I at the Edge (ACE) system.
	
 The group inaugurated two extensions in 2025 at its production sites: in Fort Wayne, Indiana, in April and in 
Palm Bay, Florida, in August. These extensions required a cumulative investment of $225 million (€208 million) 
and will be used to produce missile warning satellites for the future Golden Dome missile defence system to 
be developed in the United States.
	
 In August 2025, L3Harris Technologies  began production of EMX series optronic and infrared systems at its 
new 2,000 m² factory in Katowice, Poland.
Home market
	
 The U.S. market is crucial for the group’s business (79% of revenues in 2024). The U.S. Department of Defense 
accounted for 76% of revenues (including Foreign Military Sales).
	
 L3Harris Technologies delivered its first OA-1K Skyraider II light armed ISR aircraft to U.S. Special Operations 
Command (SOCOM) in April 2025. The aircraft is expected to be operational by the end of 2026.
	
 Following an initial delivery in February 2025, L3Harris Technologies delivered a second Global 6500 business 
jet from Bombardier, modified for ISR missions, to the U.S. Air Force in July 2025. 
	
 The group received a $292 million (€270 million) order from Raytheon-Lockheed Martin Javelin JV in 
September 2025 to continue production of propulsion systems for the FGM-148 Javelin anti-tank missile for 
five years.	
	
	
	
Export markets
	
 21% of the group’s revenues came from exports in 2024, mainly from Australia, Canada and the UK.
	
 The group received two orders for its Falcon IV tactical radios in spring 2025: one from the Netherlands 
for a maximum of €1 billion in March (FOXTROT programme) and the other from Germany for $214 million 
(€198 million) in May (D-LBO programme).
	
 The Belgian Ministry of Defence ordered 14 medium-sized T4 mine clearance robots from the group in 
June 2025. These robots have already been sold to the United Kingdom and Australia.
	
 The group signed a contract with the Royal Moroccan Air Force in July 2025 to upgrade the avionics of C-130H 
Hercules transport aircraft (Lockheed Martin) by 2029.	
	
	
	
 In July 2025, the Italian Air Force ordered two EA-37B Compass Call electronic warfare aircraft from L3Harris 
Technologies for $300 million (€277 million). The group will work on this programme in partnership with 
BAE Systems (supply of the mission system).
	
 The Polish Air Force selected the group’s AN/ALQ-254 Viper Shield electronic warfare suite in August 2025 to 
upgrade its fleet of F-16 Fighting Falcon Block 52+ fighter aircraft (Lockheed Martin) to the Block 70 standard.
Technology and Innovation
	
 The group’s self-financed R&D expenditure amounted to $515 million (€476 million) in 2024, representing 2.4% 
of revenues.

LEIDOS
Business trends
	
 Leidos posted revenues of $16.7 billion (€15.4 billion) in 2024, up 7.9% year-on-year in local currency, mainly 
driven by growth in the Health & Civil division (+18.3%). The National Security & Digital and Defense Systems 
divisions, which include the group’s defence activities, grew by 2.3% and 8.1% respectively.
	
 In May 2025, Leidos acquired the American company Kudu Dynamics, a specialist in offensive cybersecurity 
solutions used in particular for military applications. Worth $300 million (€266 million), this transaction is part of 
the NorthStar 2030 strategy presented by the group at the end of 2024.
Home market
	
 Leidos is the U.S. government’s leading provider of digital and telecom services according to Washington 
Technology’s 2025 rankings, ahead of Booz Allen Hamilton and Lockheed Martin.
	
 In December 2024, the group won a contract worth up to $987 million (€912 million) over 10 years to provide 
engineering and support services to foreign countries purchasing F-16 fighter aircraft.
	
 In January 2025, Leidos signed a contract worth up to $120 million (€111 million) over five years to provide 
various digital services, particularly in the areas of cybersecurity and cryptographic key management, to 
U.S. defence and intelligence agencies.
	
 In April 2025, the National Security Agency (NSA) awarded Leidos a contract worth up to $390 million 
(€363 million) over five years to provide signals intelligence (SIGINT) capabilities, engineering and analysis 
services, and various other services (training, maintenance, etc.).
Export markets
	
 Leidos generated 8% of its revenues from exports in 2024. In defence, Australia and the UK are the group’s 
main international customers.
	
 In February 2025, Leidos signed a seven-year contract with the UK Ministry of Defence to create an intelligence 
service called THOR to collect, analyse and transmit meteorological and oceanographic data to the UK’s 
National Centre for Geospatial Intelligence.
	
 The group was selected in June 2025 to lead a project to modernise NATO’s IT systems, in particular to 
increase resistance to cyber threats. Several partners wil be involved in the project, including Thales and 
Leonardo. The contract has a ceiling value of $87 million (€79 million).
	
 In September 2025, Leidos began construction in Australia of a new unmanned surface vehicle, named 
Sea Archer. Capable of reaching speeds of 40 knots (74 km/h) and carrying a payload of over 900 kg, the USV 
is designed for various tasks including attack, refuelling, ISR and electronic warfare missions.
	
 In November 2025, Leidos Australia partnered with Kongsberg to evaluate the possibility of integrating the 
NSM anti-ship missile onto the group’s Sea Archer and Longbow USVs.
Technology and Innovation
	
 Self-financed R&D expenditure amounted to $150 million (€139 million) in 2024, representing 0.9% of revenues.
	
 Leidos is testing its small (90 kg) Black Arrow cruise missile, following a successful launch test in 
November 2024 from an AC-130J aircraft.
	
 In 2025, Leidos unveiled the Sea Dart unmanned underwater vehicle, designed to meet the needs of the 
U.S. Navy. The group also presented the Sea Dagger Commando Insertion Craft, developed for the 
Royal Navy and UK Commando Force, and aims to deliver 24 units.
	
 In the United States, Leidos received a contract from the Defense Innovation Unit in June 2025 to develop an 
alternative navigation technology to GPS, able to thwart jamming, for military applications.

132
LEONARDO
Business trends
	
 Leonardo revenues reached €17.8 billion in 2024, up 16.2% year-on-year. The Aircraft division was the only 
one to record a decline in revenues (-4.8% year-on-year), due to lower deliveries of Typhoon fighter aircraft 
to Kuwait. Revenues in the Helicopters division rose by 11.1%, thanks in particular to increased deliveries of 
military and civil helicopters (191 units in 2024, compared with 185 in 2023).
	
 In June 2025, Leonardo and Baykar created a 50-50 joint venture specialising in the design, development, 
production and maintenance of aerial drones for the European market. Named LBA Systems, its headquarters 
will be based in Italy and production sites will be shared between the two countries. Leonardo will provide 
software, electronic systems and artificial intelligence solutions.
	
 In June 2025, BAE Systems, Leonardo and Japan Aircraft Industrial Enhancement Co. (JAIEC, including 
Mitsubishi Heavy Industries), each holding a 33.33% stake, created the Edgewing joint venture. It will be 
responsible for designing and developing the future sixth-generation GCAP (Global Combat Air Programme) 
fighter aircraft, known as Tempest in the United Kingdom. The joint venture is based in the United Kingdom and 
its first Chief Executive Officer is Italian.
	
 Leonardo reached an agreement with Iveco Group in July 2025 for the acquisition of Iveco Defence Vehicles 
(IDV) for €1.7 billion. The transaction is expected to close by March 31, 2026. However, IDV’s military truck 
production business could be resold to Rheinmetall at a later date.
Home market
	
 Leonardo is one of the main suppliers to the Italian Armed Forces. The group is involved in the design and 
production of Italian military aircraft and helicopters and is a partner of Fincantieri in many naval defence 
programmes, in particular through their joint subsidiary Orizzonte Sistemi Navali. In 2024, Leonardo generated 
18% of its turnover on its domestic market.
	
 The Italian Air Force signed a contract with the NATO Eurofighter and Tornado Management Agency (NETMA) 
in December 2024 for the acquisition of 16 Typhoon fighter aircraft. Eight additional aircraft will be ordered at a 
later date. These aircraft will be assembled at Leonardo’s Caselle Torinese site.
	
 The Ministry of Defence of Italy ordered the first 17 AW249 Fenice attack helicopters from Leonardo in 
February 2025 (out of a target acquisition of 48 units). This helicopter is intended to replace the group’s 
A129 Mangusta helicopter from 2027 onwards.
	
 Leonardo’s M-345 trainer aircraft entered service with the Italian Air Force in June 2025. It will gradually replace 
the group’s MB-339 aircraft currently in service.
Export markets
	
 The group generated 82% of its revenues from exports in 2024, mainly to the United States and the United 
Kingdom (combined share of 35% of turnover), through its subsidiaries Leonardo DRS and Leonardo US 
Aircraft on the one hand and Leonardo UK on the other.
	
 In March 2025, the UK Ministry of Defence awarded the group a £165 million (€195 million) contract to continue 
maintenance of its 54 AW101 Merlin multirole helicopters.
	
 In December 2025, the Austrian Air Force awarded Leonardo a contract to supply 12 M-346 F trainer and light 
attack aircraft, to be delivered from 2028 onwards.
Technology and Innovation
	
 Leonardo’s Falco Xplorer UAV carried out a validation campaign for the UAV detection and avoidance system 
EUDASS (European Detect and Avoid System) in November 2024, paving the way for its integration into civilian 
and military remotely piloted drones. This project is led by Saab and funded by the European Defence Fund.
