135
LIG NEX1
Business trends
	
 LIG Nex1 revenues reached 3.276 trillion won (€2.2 billion) in 2024, up nearly 42% year-on-year in local 
currency. All divisions recorded solid growth, particularly the C4I Systems division.
	
 In August and September 2025, the group relocated and expanded its office in Riyadh (Saudi Arabia) and 
opened an office in Munich (Germany) to strengthen its presence in the Middle East and Europe.
Home market
	
 As one of South Korea’s main defence groups, LIG Nex1 benefits from a leading position in its main business 
segments.
	
 In January 2025, LIG Nex1 was commissioned by South Korea’s Defense Rapid Acquisition Technology 
Research Institute (DRATRI) to develop communication systems for surveillance and reconnaissance drones, 
vehicles and soldiers. Valued at 15.3 billion won (€10.3 million), the MANET (Mobile Ad-hoc NETworking 
systems) project is due to be completed by 2027.
	
 In April 2025, the group signed a 321 billion won (€217 million) agreement to participate in the development of 
the South Korean meteorological satellite GEO-KOMPSAT 5, scheduled for launch in 2031.
	
 LIG Nex1 partnered with Hyundai Heavy Industries and Korea Aerospace Industries in May 2025 to develop 
a multi-purpose unmanned mother ship.
	
 In June 2025, South Korea’s Defense Acquisition Program Administration (DAPA) awarded LIG Nex1 a 
33.4 billion won (€22 million) contract to develop a  simulation system for anti-ballistic missile operations training 
by 2028.
Export markets
	
 The group generated 24% of its revenues from exports in 2024.
	
 Hyundai Heavy Industries awarded LIG Nex1 a 60 billion won (€55 million) subcontract in November 2024 to 
supply combat, electronic warfare and communication systems to equip two Peruvian Navy ships by 2030, a 
frigate and an offshore patrol vessel.
	
 The group signed several agreements with American players in April and May 2025: with Anduril to integrate 
the American company’s artificial intelligence solutions into its weapon and autonomous systems (particularly 
unmanned maritime systems), with Raytheon (part of RTX) to participate in the production of the RAM Block-II 
anti-ship missile, and with Shield AI, a company specialising in AI-based autonomous flight, in the field of 
autonomous systems.
	
 LIG Nex1 partnered with Babcock Marine in May 2025 to jointly develop MRO activities for ships and 
unmanned surface vehicles, as well as new shipbuilding projects. The agreement aims to combine LIG Nex1’s 
ship equipment and armament solutions with the British group’s capabilities in naval design and shipbuilding.
	
 LIG Nex1 signed an agreement in June 2025 with the Indonesian group PT Dirgantara Indonesia (PTDI) to 
develop joint projects in guided munitions and ISR systems, including local production.
	
 In September 2025, LIG Nex1 and Airbus Defence and Space agreed to cooperate in the field of air defence, 
particularly for export (Middle East and Southeast Asia).
Technology and Innovation
	
 Total R&D expenditure amounted to 928 billion won (€629 million) in 2024, representing 28.3% of revenues.
	
 In September 2025, DAPA selected the bid submitted by LIG Nex1 and Korean Air over the competing bid 
from Korea Aerospace Industries and Hanwha Systems for the project to develop a South Korean electronic 
warfare aircraft, with a budget of 1.778 trillion won (€1.2 billion) and aiming to deliver four aircraft by 2034. Within 
the consortium, LIG Nex1 is leading the R&D work and will be responsible for the electronic systems.

138
LOCKHEED MARTIN
Business trends
	
 Lockheed Martin revenues reached $71 billion (€65.7 billion) in 2024, up 5.1% year-on-year in local currency. 
Three of the group’s divisions recorded an increase in revenues: Missiles and Fire Control (+12.7%), Rotary 
and Mission Systems (+6.3%, with 89 military helicopters delivered in 2024, compared with 63 in 2023), and 
Aeronautics (+4.2%, with the deliveries of 110 F-35 Lightning II and 16 F-16 Fighting Falcon fighter aircraft, as 
well as 21 C-130J Super Hercules tactical transport aircraft). 
	
 In June 2025, Lockheed Martin completed the acquisition of the Rapid Solutions business of Amentum, an 
engineering company specialising in space intelligence and airborne ISR technologies.
Home market
	
 The group is highly dependent on the U.S. market (74% of its revenue in 2024). The U.S. Department of 
Defense, its largest customer, accounted for 65% of sales, excluding Foreign Military Sales.
	
 Lockheed Martin won a $4.94 billion (€4.57 billion) contract in March 2025 from the U.S. Army to produce 
Precision Strike Missile (PrSM) tactical ballistic missiles, which can be deployed by M142 HIMARS and 
M270A2 MLRS rocket launchers.
	
 In June 2025, the group delivered an eighth THAAD missile defence system to the U.S. Department of Defense, 
consisting of three launchers, one fire control unit and one AN/TPY-2 radar (Raytheon).
	
 The U.S. Army ordered AGM-179 JAGM and AGM-114 Hellfire air-to-surface missiles from the group in 
August 2025 for $720 million (€665 million). Some of the missiles are intended for export customers under FMS 
procedures (Spain, Italy, Poland, the Czech Republic, the United Kingdom and Canada).
	
 In September 2025, the Raytheon-Lockheed Martin Javelin JV joint venture received an order worth 
$901 million (€833 million) to supply FGM-148 Javelin anti-tank missiles to the U.S. Armed Forces, as well as to 
Brazil and Tunisia under FMS procedures.
	
 In September 2025, the U.S. Army awarded the group a $9.8 billion (€9.1 billion) contract to produce 
1,970 PAC-3 MSE interceptor missiles for the Patriot missile defence system (Raytheon).
	
 The Sikorsky subsidiary signed a $10.9 billion (€10.1 billion) contract in September 2025 with the U.S. Marine 
Corps to produce batches 9 to 13 of CH-53K King Stallion heavy-lift transport helicopters, representing up to 
99 units, to be delivered between 2029 and 2034.
	
 The F-35 Joint Programme Office ordered batches 18 and 19 of F-35 Lightning II fighter aircraft (representing 
296 aircraft) in September 2025 from Lockheed Martin for $24.3 billion (€22.5 billion).
Export markets
	
 In February 2025, Lockheed Martin delivered to the Bulgarian Air Force the first of eight F-16 Fighting Falcon 
Block 70 fighter aircraft ordered in 2019.
	
 In April 2025, Lockheed Martin completed deliveries of F-35A fighter aircraft to Norway (52 units).
	
 The Swedish Defence Materiel Administration (FMV) selected the group’s AN/TPY-4 AESA air surveillance 
radar, becoming its second export customer after Norway. Deliveries will begin in 2027.
Technology and Innovation
	
 Self-financed R&D expenditure amounted to $1.6 billion (€1.5 billion) in 2024, representing 2.3% of revenues.
	
 The U.S. Navy awarded Lockeed Martin a $383 million (€354 million) contract in January 2025 to develop the 
next generation of the UGM-133A Trident II nuclear-armed ballistic missile. This version, named D5LE2, will be 
deployed by the future Columbia-class SSBNs (General Dynamics).
	
 In the United States, the Missile Defense Agency awarded the group a contract in February 2025 to improve 
the capabilities of the THAAD system over a period of five years (with an option for a further five years), for a 
ceiling value of $2.8 billion (€2.6 billion).

141
LOFT ORBITAL
Business trends
	
 Founded in the United States in 2017 by a French-American team, Loft Orbital (also known as Loft) offers the 
entire management of space missions for its customers. It purchases standardised satellite platforms from 
third-party operators, and has developed a payload adapter, called the Hub, as well as the Cockpit software 
for operating payloads. Loft Orbital also prebooks places on launchers and manages launch and in-orbit 
operations.
	
 The customers’ payloads can be integrated into a satellite dedicated to their mission, carrying other payloads 
(rideshare) or forming part of a constellation. Customers can also access Loft Orbital resources (satellites and 
payloads) using their own software or the Cockpit software.
	
 Loft Orbital owns and operates the YAM (Yet Another Mission) satellites. The YAM-8 satellite, launched in 
March 2025, uses the Longbow platform, an upgraded version of Airbus’ Arrow 150 platform that can carry 
more complex payloads. The company has sold missions on more than 30 satellites since its creation, 
representing more than €500 million in contracts signed with private customers (60%) and institutional 
customers (NASA, ESA, French Space Agency CNES, etc.; 40%).
	
 Having already raised €140 million since its creation, Loft Orbital raised €170 million in January 2025 from 
several investors including Tikehau Capital, Axial Partners, Bpifrance and Singapore-based investment 
company Temasek. Loft Orbital aims to increase its satellite launches from a few per year to more than 10.
Home markets (United States, France)
	
 In the United States, the company operates in California and Colorado. The Loft Federal subsidiary, created 
in early 2023, is dedicated to government and defence operators in the U.S. market. The launch of the first 
satellites in a constellation of 10 units for the Space Development Agency is scheduled for 2025.
	
 Loft Orbital is one of 12 players selected by the U.S. Space Force in May 2025 as part of the STEP 2.0 
(Space Test Experiments Platform) contract, with a ceiling value of $237 million (€210 million) over 10 years. 
The objective is to supply and operate small satellite platforms capable of carrying various sensors and military 
equipment for conducting tests in orbit. Contract notifications are due to begin in January 2026.
	
 Based in Toulouse, the group’s French subsidiary generated revenues of €74 million in 2024 and employs 
around 100 people. It sources its satellites, equipment and software from French space industry players, 
including Airbus and Thales Alenia Space.
	
 The development of the DESIR (Démonstrateur des Éléments Souverains en Imagerie Radar) demonstrator, a 
€50 million project led by CNES, was entrusted in June 2025 to Loft Orbital, in partnership with Thales Alenia 
Space and Portugal’s Tekever. Loft Orbital will be responsible for the platform and integration of the space 
system, while Thales Alenia Space will be responsible for the payload.
Export markets
	
 Loft Orbital and UAE-based Marlan Space joined forces in August 2024 to create a joint venture in the United 
Arab Emirates, named Orbitworks. A 5,000 m² factory is under construction in Abu Dhabi, with the aim of 
producing up to 50 satellites per year and beginning launches in 2026.
	
 The first of 10 Earth observation satellites from Canadian company EarthDaily Analytics (EDA) was launched in 
June 2025, with the entire constellation scheduled to be operational in 2026. Loft Orbital is responsible for the 
construction, launch and operation of the satellites.	
	
	
	
 In February 2025, Loft Orbital partnered with German company Helsing to develop and deploy a constellation 
of satellites equipped with various payloads (cameras, RF sensors, etc.) and an on-board AI solution in low 
Earth orbit. The aim is to provide real-time information for defence and security missions (border surveillance, 
troop movement tracking, protection of sensitive sites, etc.). The launch of the first satellites is scheduled for 
2026.

STRATEGIC HIGHLIGHTS
Business trends
	
 MBDA revenues grew 10.4% in 2024 to reach €4,913 million. Missile production capacity was up 33% year-on-
year, and the group expects its production volumes to double in 2025 compared to 2023.
	
 The group and its subsidiary Matra Électronique (MEL), which specialises in the manufacture of high-precision 
electronic equipment, created a centre of excellence for defence electronics in Venette (near Compiègne) at 
the end of 2024.
	
 MBDA and Leonardo signed a letter of intent in December 2024 to develop a directed energy laser weapon 
firing unit, complementing the solutions developed by the group in the United Kingdom.
	
 In December 2024, MBDA also acquired Safran’s 50% stake in Roxel (solid propulsion systems for rockets and 
missiles) and integrated the company as a wholly-owned subsidiary.
Home markets (France, Germany, United Kingdom, Italy, Spain)
	
 TAURUS Systems (a joint venture between MBDA and Saab) signed a contract in December 2024 with the 
German Army for the maintenance and technological upgrade of the TAURUS air-launched cruise missile, 
ensuring its operational availability until 2045.
	
 In March 2025, France, the United Kingdom and Italy ordered 218 additional Aster 15 and Aster 30 missiles 
from Eurosam, an economic interest grouping (EIG) between MBDA France, MBDA Italia and Thales. As part 
of this contract, the three countries asked the EIG to accelerate its production rates, which are expected to 
decrease from 42 months in 2022 to less than 18 months in 2026, enabling a 50% increase in production.
	
 The group signed a contract with the Italian Army in June 2025 to develop a very short-range air defence 
missile against fighter aircraft, helicopters and small drones, called FULGUR. The first delivery of the missile 
and its portable launcher is scheduled for 2028.
	
 The UK Ministry of Defence awarded MBDA a contract in August 2025 to produce six Land Ceptor medium-
range air defence systems, which use CAMM missiles. These systems form the architecture of the British Sky 
Sabre air defence system.
	
 MBDA won a contract from the Italian Navy’s National Armament Directorate in September 2025 to begin 
production of the new-generation TESEO MK2/E anti-ship missile. This missile will equip several Italian Navy 
ships, such as FREMM EVO frigates and PPA offshore patrol vessels.
	
 In September 2025, the French defence procurement agency (DGA) entrusted the development of a sovereign 
high-power directed energy weapon demonstrator to a consortium of French companies led by MBDA France 
as part of the SYDERAL contract.
Export markets
	
 The group secured two contracts for the acquisition of Mistral 3 short-range surface-to-air missiles in 2025: 
with the Cypriot National Guard in May and with Estonia in July.
	
 In June 2025, the Swedish Defence Materiel Administration (FMV) ordered AKERON MP anti-tank missiles 
and their launchers from MBDA. This system includes a band 2 IR camera, a laser rangefinder and a modular 
architecture to enable its integration into combat vehicles.
	
 In September 2025, Denmark selected MBDA’s SAMP/T NG air and missile defence system over Raytheon’s 
(RTX) Patriot system, and may order two batteries.
Technology and Innovation
	
 After an initial ground test firing in February 2025, MBDA successfully fired the AKERON LP missile from a 
Tiger helicopter in March 2025.
	
 At the Paris Air Show in June 2025, MBDA presented its One Way Effector project, a new long-range munition 
for mass combat.

MTU AERO ENGINES
Business trends
	
 MTU Aero Engines revenues increased sharply in 2024 (+38.2%) to reach €7,411 million. Growth was 
particularly strong in the Commercial engines division (+158% year-on-year).
	
 Sales in the Military engines division, whose main source of revenue is the EJ200 engine for the Typhoon 
fighter aircraft (within the EuroJet Turbo consortium), rose 13.8% year-on-year. The share of military activities 
decreased by 1.7 points to 8.1% of the group’s total revenue in 2024.
	
 In December 2024, the group acquired the German company 3D.aero, which employs around 40 people. 
The company specialises in automation and measurement technologies, particularly sensors for 
high-precision surface inspection and systems engineering in aeronautics. These technologies should enable 
MTU Aero Engines to carry out its maintenance operations more quickly.
	
 In June 2025, Avio Aero (a subsidiary of GE Aerospace) joined MTU Aero Engines and Safran in the EURA 
(European Military Rotorcraft Engine Alliance) joint venture, which is responsible for developing the ENGHE 
propulsion system for future heavy military helicopters, scheduled to enter service by 2040. The workload will 
be divided equally between the three operators, but Avio Aero will not acquire a stake in EURA.
Home market
	
 MTU Aero Engines generated 10.3% of its turnover in Germany in 2024, a share that has decreased by 
2.2 percentage points year-on-year.
	
 As a major partner of the German Air Force, the group is notably responsible for the maintenance of the EJ200 
(Typhoon) and RB199 (Tornado) turbojet engines and of the MTR390 turboshaft engine (Tiger helicopter). 
MTU Aero Engines also ambitions to be selected for the maintenance of the F135 turbojet engine powering the 
F-35 Lightning II fighter aircraft due to be delivered to the German Armed Forces from 2026.
	
 MTU Aero Engines is involved in the development of the Next European Fighter Engine (NEFE) for the future 
NGF European fighter aircraft, in partnership with Safran within the EUMET (European Military Engine 
Team) 50-50 joint venture. The two groups have since been joined by ITP Aero and the industrial load for 
the development of the NEFE engine will be shared equally between Germany, France and Spain. Phase 1B 
of the FCAS programme (which covers the development of a flying demonstrator by 2029) was launched in 
April 2023 for a three-year period.
Export markets
	
 Export sales rose by 41.6% in 2024, to €6,645 million, and accounted for 89.7% of the group’s total revenue.
	
 The C-390 Millennium tactical transport aircraft (Embraer) received six orders in 2024-2025: four units for 
Austria, five for the Netherlands, one for Portugal, two for the Czech Republic, four for Sweden and two for 
Uzbekistan. These aircraft will be powered by International Aero Engines’ V2500 turbofan engine, for which the 
group provides 16% of the workload.	
	
 MTU Aero Engines benefits from orders for Typhoon fighter aircraft placed in December 2024 by Spain 
(25 units planned as part of the Halcon II programme) and Italy (24 units). Following these orders, the NATO 
Eurofighter & Tornado Management Agency (NETMA) awarded two contracts to the EuroJet Turbo consortium 
to produce 59 and 56 EJ200 turbojet engines for these aircraft, respectively. The group produces around 30% 
of the EJ200 turbojet engine.
Technology and Innovation
	
 Total R&D expenditure amounted to €342 million in 2024 (4.6% of revenues), of which more than 25% (or 
€88 million) were devoted to military engines. Two-thirds of R&D expenditure is self-financed (€231 million).

150
NAMMO
Business trends
	
 Nammo revenues grew 18.3% in Norwegian kroner in 2024 to NOK 10.9 billion (€935 million). All divisions 
reported higher revenues, particularly in artillery ammunition, portable rocket launchers and missile propulsion 
systems.
	
 The group’s order book stood at NOK 39.8 billion (€3.4 billion) at the end of 2024, up 56.7% year-on-year in 
Norwegian kroner, and reached its highest level ever (3.7 years of revenue). Growth was mainly driven by the 
signing of several ammunition contracts, as many countries mean to replenish their stocks following Russia’s 
invasion of Ukraine.
	
 In December 2024, Nammo began construction of a new production facility for artillery ammunition and missile 
propulsion systems in Raufoss, Norway. This project is part of a 15-year, NOK 1,083 million (€93 million) grant 
awarded by the Norwegian government to support the group in increasing its production capacity.
	
 In February 2025, the Ministry of Defence of Denmark selected Nammo to restart production at its former 
ammunition factory in Elling from the second half of 2027. The factory will produce and/or fill small and large 
calibre ammunition. The group will own 85% of this factory, alongside the Danish government.
Home markets (Norway, Sweden, Finland)
	
 The merger of three Nordic defence companies, Nammo continues to have a strong presence in its domestic 
markets (Norway, Sweden, Finland), where it generated 26% of its sales in 2024.
	
 In May 2025, the Swedish Defence Materiel Administration (FMV) placed an order with Nammo for small-
calibre ammunition worth SEK 1.8 billion (€157 million), to be delivered between 2026 and 2028. This contract 
is the largest ever won by the group in this field from the Swedish Armed Forces.
	
 In July 2025, the Swedish FMV signed a framework agreement with Nammo for the supply of 155mm long-
range artillery projectiles for Archer self-propelled howitzers and placed an initial order worth approximately 
SEK 1 billion (€87 million).
Export markets
	
 Nammo generated almost three quarters of its sales from exports in 2024. Europe (excluding Norway, Sweden 
and Finland) is the group’s largest international market with 41% of total sales during that year.
	
 In July 2024, Germany’s Federal Office of Bundeswehr Equipment (BAAINBw) ordered 155mm calibre 
artillery ammunition from the ARGE DiNa 155mm consortium (made up of Nammo and Diehl Defence). 
The contract, which value was undisclosed, is the largest ever won by Nammo. The ammunition is destined for 
Germany and allied countries.
	
 In December 2024, the group signed a contract worth A$ 23 million (€14 million) with the Royal Australian Air 
Force for the production of its new 25mm x 137 APEX PGU-47/U ammunition for the F-35A Lightning II fighter 
aircraft (Lockheed Martin). In September 2025, Germany, Belgium, Denmark, Poland and Switzerland jointly 
ordered this ammunition for approximately €60 million.
Technology and Innovation
	
 Nammo aims to spend 10% of its annual revenues on R&D activities, which include long-range ammunition, 
medium-calibre ammunition (30mm and 40mm) and ammunition for the F-35 Lightning II fighter aircraft.
	
 Raytheon (part of RTX) awarded Nammo a contract in April 2025 to study the possibility of establishing 
production lines for the Mk 72 solid propellant system for the RIM-161 SM-3 interceptor missile. The Mk 72 
is currently produced by L3Harris Technologies, but tensions in the missile propulsion systems market led 
Raytheon to seek alternative suppliers.

NAVAL GROUP
Business trends
	
 Naval Group revenues grew 2.3% in 2024 to reach €4,355 million. Growth was driven by the Services division 
(+7.7%). During the year, the group delivered the Tourville SSN and the third and last refurbished La Fayette-
class frigate (Aconit) to the French Navy, as well as the second Gowind corvette (El Amarat) to the United Arab 
Emirates. Naval Group also carried out maintenance on the French aircraft carrier Charles de Gaulle and the 
Le Vigilant SSBN (Le Triomphant-class).
	
 In September 2025, the group signed an industrial cooperation agreement with PGZ at the MSPO trade fair in 
Kielce (Poland) for the construction of submarines (potentially the future Orka-class for the Polish Navy) and 
specialised vessels.
Home market
	
 Naval Group is the prime contractor for the French Navy’s main construction and modernisation programmes: 
the future new generation aircraft carrier (PA-NG); FREMM, FDI and Horizon frigates; the future third-generation 
nuclear-powered ballistic missile submarine (SNLE 3G in French); Suffren-class SSNs; etc.
	
 The French Navy awarded Naval Group a contract in the second half of 2024 to begin production of F21 MK2 
heavy torpedoes for submarines, to be delivered from 2028 onwards.
	
 The French Ministry of Armed Forces ordered the first six VSR700 shipborne helicopter drones in June 2025 
from Airbus and Naval Group. The group will integrate these drones on four FREMM frigates and two FDI 
frigates of the French Navy.
	
 The first FDI frigate for the French Navy, named Amiral Ronarc’h, was delivered in September 2025. The next 
three units (Amiral Louzeau, Amiral Castex and Amiral Nomy) are under construction and are expected to be 
delivered between 2027 and 2031.
Export markets
	
 The sixth and last Kalvari-class submarine for the Indian Navy (Vagsheer), based on the Scorpène family and 
built locally by Mazagon Dock Shipbuilders Ltd (MDL), entered service in January 2025.
	
 In May 2025, the first FDI frigate for the Hellenic Navy, named Kimon, began sea trials off the coast of Lorient 
(on the Atlantic coast). The next two units, Nearchos and Formion, were launched in September 2024 and 
May 2025, respectively. A fourth frigate, named Themistokles, was ordered in November 2025.
	
 The contract signed by Naval Group with the Indonesian Navy for the construction of two Scorpène Evolved 
submarines equipped with lithium-ion batteries came into force in July 2025. The vessels will be built in 
Indonesia by the PT PAL shipyard.
	
 In the summer of 2025, Naval Group signed two contracts worth a total of €528 million with the Brazilian Navy 
for the provision of engineering and technical consulting services (excluding the nuclear power plant) for the 
construction of the future Almirante Álvaro Alberto nuclear-powered submarine.
	
 The first City-class mine warfare vessel for Belgium, named Oostende, entered service in October 2025. 
Kership (a joint venture between Naval Group and Piriou) is building three other units (Tournai, Brugge and 
Liège) at the Concarneau shipyard in Brittany.
	
 Argentina selected the Scorpène in November 2025 and could acquire three submarines for €2.3 billion.
Technology and Innovation
	
 The group’s self-financed R&D expenditure amounted to €92 million in 2024, representing 2.1% of revenues.
	
 At the Euronaval trade show in Villepinte (near Paris) in November 2024, a letter of intent was signed for the 
integration of weapons, namely 20mm and 40mm ammunition from KNDS, as well as 68mm and 70mm 
rockets and Lightweight Multi-role Missiles (LMM) from Thales, on the modular multi-purpose launcher (LMP) 
being developed by Naval Group. This weapon system aims to improve the protection of ships against UAVs 
and USVs.

[Page 157 / 226 | chars: 3712]

156
Business trends
	
 Navantia revenues increased 6.6% in 2024 to reach €1,528 million. Shipbuilding, the group’s main division, 
recorded a 10.6% drop in sales, but this decline was offset by better performance in the other divisions, 
particularly MRO, Modernisation & Services (+6.2%) and Green Energy (+70.6%). However, despithe the 
launching of a restructuring plan in 2017, Navantia stlil recorded losses of €197 million in 2024 (a loss ratio of 
-12.9%), due in particular to an estimated cost overrun of between €1.5 billion and €1.8 billion on the Spanish 
Navy’s Isaac Peral-class submarine programme.	
	
	
 In January 2025, Navantia acquired the British shipyard Harland & Wolff, its partner in the Royal Navy’s 
Fleet Solid Support Ship (FSS) programme. Harland & Wolff owns four shipyards in the United Kingdom and 
employs around 1,000 people.
Home market
	
 The group remains heavily dependent on orders from the Spanish Navy, whith two main programmes running 
until the end of the decade (five Bonifaz-class frigates and three Isaac Peral-class submarines to build). 
Its domestic revenues increased 7.2% in 2024 and accounted for 59% of total sales.
	
 In May 2025, the Spanish Navy awarded the group a €703 million contract to build a second Cantabria-class 
replenishment oiler. The work will be carried out at the Ferrol shipyard. This vessel is due to replace the Patiño, 
in service since 1995, in the early 2030s.
	
 In September 2025, the Spanish Navy ordered two additional Meteoro-class offshore patrol vessels (BAM) 
from Navantia for €716 million. The vessels will be delivered from 2030 onwards and will complement the six 
units already built by the group, which entered  service between 2012 and 2019.
	
 In September 2025, Navantia also launched the first Bonifaz-class multi-mission frigate at the Ferrol shipyard. 
The ship is scheduled to enter service in 2028. In addition, the group laid the keel of the second unit (Roger de 
Lauria) and began steel cutting for the third unit (Menéndez de Avilés) in April 2025.
	
 In October 2025, Navantia launched the second of four Isaac Peral-class (S-80 Plus) conventional attack 
submarines for the Spanish Navy, named Narciso Monturiol. Its delivery is expected in 2026.
Export markets
	
 Export sales increased 5.7% in 2024 and accounted for 41% of the group’s revenue. This share fell by 
21 percentage points compared with 2020, mainly as a result of the delivery that year of the third and last 
Hobart-class destroyer to the Royal Australian Navy.
	
 In May 2025, Navantia launched the Avante 1800 offshore patrol boat, ordered by the Royal Moroccan Navy 
in 2022, at the San Fernando shipyard.
	
 In July 2025, at the San Fernando shipyard, the group laid the keel of the first of three Avante 2200 corvettes 
ordered by the Royal Saudi Navy in the second half of 2024. This vessel will be built entirely in Spain. However, 
the integration of the Hazem combat system for the second and third units, under construction since 
March 2025 and July 2025 respectively, will be carried out locally by SAMI (Saudi Arabian Military Industries). 
The three ships are due to be delivered by 2028.
Technology and Innovation
	
 The group’s total R&D expenditure amounted to €81 million in 2023, representing 5.6% of revenues.
	
 In December 2024, Navantia unveiled the design of the two future coastal hydrographic vessels ordered in 
2023 by the Spanish Navy. These vessels will be tasked with scanning the seabed in shallow waters using 
various equipment such as echo sounders and side-scan sonars in order to update nautical charts and ensure 
the safety of civil and military navigation.

159
NORTHROP GRUMMAN
Business trends
	
 Northrop Grumman revenues went up by 4.4% in local currency in 2024 to reach $41 billion (€37.9 billion). 
Growth was mainly driven by the Aeronautics Systems division (+11.8%), thanks to progress on several 
programmes including the B-21 Raider future strategic bomber, the F-35 Lightning II fighter aircraft (Lockheed 
Martin) and the MQ-4C Triton maritime surveillance HALE UAS (in initial production). Revenues in the Mission 
Systems division also grew (+3.9%), thanks in particular to increased production of G/ATOR (Ground/Air Task 
Oriented Radar) systems and certain classified programmes.
	
 Northrop Grumman sold its mission training and satellite ground network communications software business 
(MT&S) to the British group Serco for $327 million (€302 million) in May 2025. This business employs 
approximately 1,000 people and generates an annual revenue of $300 million (€277 million).
Home market
	
 The U.S. is the group’s main market (88% of revenues in 2024). Northrop Grumman is one of the main 
manufacturers of surveillance aircraft (E-2C/D Hawkeye) and UAS (MQ-4C Triton). It also supplies fuselage 
parts for Boeing and Lockheed Martin fighter aircraft. 
	
 Northrop Grumman received a second contract from the U.S. Air Force in the fourth quarter of 2024 to continue 
production of the future B-21 Raider strategic bomber. At least three units have been completed and two are 
scheduled for delivery in 2026.
	
 The U.S. Navy awarded Northrop Grumman a $3.46 billion (€3.20 billion) contract in January 2025 to 
design the future E-130J Phoenix II airborne command post, to be used in the context of a nuclear response 
(TACAMO). Intended to replace the E-6B Mercury (Boeing), the aircraft will be based on the C-130J Super 
Hercules (Lockheed Martin). Up to nine of these aircraft could be ordered.
	
 In May 2025, the U.S. Space Force awarded Northrop Grumman a $244 million (€226 million) contract to 
produce ground relay stations in the United States and the United Kingdom for the Next-Gen OPIR Polar 
missile warning satellite programme. The group is also to supply two of these satellites, to be launched from 
2028 onwards.
	
 Northrop Grumman won a $355 million (€328 million) contract in June 2025 from the U.S. Army to continue 
production of M1002 120mm calibre tank training ammunition until September 2026.
Export markets
	
 The group generated 12% of its revenues from exports in 2024, a share that has decreased by 2 percentage 
points since 2021.
	
 In December 2024, the first of three E-2D Advanced Hawkeye airborne early warning aircraft ordered in 2020 
by the French Navy entered production. From 2027, these aircraft will replace the E-2C Hawkeye 2000 aircraft 
that have been in service since 1998.
	
 In December 2024, the Ministry of National Defence of Poland announced that the group’s IBCS C2 system 
achieved initial operational capability (IOC). IBCS will be the core battle management system for the Polish 
Armed Forces’ Narew (short-range) and Wisla (medium-range) air defence and missile defence systems.
	
 The Ministry of National Defence of Poland also ordered 200 AGM-88G AARGM-ER anti-radar missiles in 
January 2025 for $745 million (€689 million), to be delivered between 2029 and 2035. The Netherlands and 
Australia are also expected to order this missile in the near future.	
	
 In August 2025, the U.S. Department of Defense awarded Northrop Grumman a contract for the supply of an 
AN/TPS-78 long-range air surveillance radar under the Foreign Military Sales (FMS) programme.
Technology and Innovation
	
 Self-financed R&D expenditure amounted to $1.2 billion (€1.1 billion) in 2024, representing 2.9% of revenues.

162
OHB
Business trends
	
 OHB revenues fell by nearly 13% in 2024 to €1.03 billion. However, the group’s sales value declined by only 
4.4% over the year, mainly due to delivery delays. The Space Systems and Aerospace divisions were the 
most affected by these delays. Margins also contracted, penalised by costs related to the group’s internal 
transformation programme.
	
 Since acquiring a stake in the summer of 2024, the U.S.-based investment firm KKR has held nearly 29% of all 
OHB shares. The Fuchs family remains the majority shareholder with nearly two-thirds of the shares.
	
 In March 2025, OHB created a subsidiary in Bristol (United Kingdom), in particular to strengthen its ties with 
local players in the sector (ESA, UK Space Agency, Space West, etc.). The site will employ up to 50 engineers.
Home market
	
 OHB is a key supplier to the European Space Agency (ESA), its largest customer, and to Germany (SARLupe/ 
SARah, Heinrich Hertz, EnMAP satellite programmes, etc.). The group also supplies around 10% of the 
structures and components of the Ariane 6 launcher. In September 2025, its MT Aerospace subsidiary 
signed a contract with ArianeGroup to supply tanks and structural elements for the main and upper stages of 
Ariane 6 for flights 16 to 42 of the launcher.
	
 OHB is a partner in the SpaceRISE consortium, which is responsible for deploying the future European IRIS² 
constellation by 2030. The group plans to act as one of the main subcontractors (alongside Thales Alenia 
Space) for the production of the 18 medium Earth orbit (MEO) satellites, which will be supplied and operated 
by SES.
	
 In January 2025, ESA awarded OHB System a €175.5 million contract for the production of the third satellite for 
the Copernicus CO2M (Sentinel-7) mission, whose payload will be supplied by Thales Alenia Space. The first 
two satellites are under construction, with launch scheduled for 2027.
	
 In February 2025, OHB System signed a contract with ESA to launch the next A/B1 phase of the OpSTAR 
(Optical Synchronized Time And Ranging) programme. OHB is leading a consortium of around 30 players 
to develop optical inter-satellite links, reducing dependence on ground stations and on-board atomic clocks, 
while improving satellite navigation systems.
	
 In June 2025, OHB started construction of the LISA (Laser Interferometer Space Antenna) constellation for 
ESA, under an €839 million contract. In partnership with Thales Alenia Space, the group will supply three 
satellites designed to detect gravitational waves, scheduled for launch in 2035 by Ariane 6.
	
 Following the failure of the maiden flight of its RFA One mini-launcher in August 2024, the Rocket Factory 
Augsburg subsidiary is preparing a new launch at the end of 2025 from the SaxaVord Spaceport in Scotland.
Export markets
	
 In February 2025, Canadian company MDA Space selected OHB System to supply the satellite control 
software and operational simulator to ensure the deployment of the Telesat Lightspeed constellation (nearly 
200 satellites to be launched from 2026 onwards).
	
 The NAOS (National Advanced Optical System) satellite, Luxembourg’s first observation satellite produced by 
OHB Italia, was launched in August 2025 by SpaceX. The data collected can be used for military and civilian 
applications by Luxembourg and allied countries.
Technology and Innovation
	
 The group’s total R&D expenditure reached €25.7 million in 2024, representing 2.5% of revenues.
	
 OHB is part of the consortium in charge of the European METASAT (Modular Model-Based Design and Testing 
for Applications in Satellites) project, which was finalised in February 2025 after two years of development. 
The research work focuses on the design and testing of complex satellites (using digital twins and AI).

[Page 166 / 226 | chars: 3545]
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

165
Business trends
	
 Patria revenues increased 12.5% in 2024 to reach €826 million. Growth was mainly driven by two armoured 
vehicles programmes: the 6x6 CAVS (Common Armoured Vehicle System) vehicles, ordered by Finland, 
Latvia and Sweden, and the AMVXP 8x8 vehicles, ordered by Slovakia and Japan. The group also delivered 
night vision goggles and optronic systems (through its subsidiary Senop) to the Finnish Defence Forces and 
Sonac ACS and DTS sonars to the Royal Norwegian Navy. However, revenues from services (55.7% of sales) 
declined by 5.5% over the year.
	
 In March 2025, the group invested €40 million in its armoured vehicle production site in Hämeenlinna (Finland) 
in order to double its capacity. These new workshops are expected to be operational in 2027.
	
 Rheinmetall and Patria entered into a strategic partnership in April 2025 to integrate at least 24 NEMO 120mm 
mortars into a specific version of the Lynx KF41 tracked armoured infantry fighting vehicle being procured by 
Hungary.	
	
	
Home market
	
 With almost two-thirds of its sales generated in Finland in 2024, Patria remains highly dependent on its 
domestic market. 	 	
	
	
	
 The Finnish Defence Forces ordered compact EVA M (or VVM 25) night vision goggles from the Senop 
subsidiary in January 2025 for €20 million. These goggles are an improved version of the EVA goggles 
delivered to the Finnish Defence Forces between 2019 and 2024.
	
 In January 2025, Patria and its partner Solita, grouped together in the SPOT consortium, won a contract to 
develop applications for the new Finnish Border Guard information system, which is to be implemented by 
2027 (RAVALU programme).
Export markets
	
 The group’s export markets mainly include European countries (Norway, Sweden, Estonia, Latvia, Poland, 
Belgium and the Netherlands), the United Arab Emirates and Japan. 
	
 Germany selected Patria’s new 6x6 armoured vehicle in the fourth quarter of 2024 for its Fuchs armoured 
personnel carrier (Rheinmetall) replacement programme and placed an initial order for 300 vehicles. These 
vehicles will be produced in Germany by Defence Service Logistics (DSL, a subsidiary of KNDS Deutschland) 
and Flensburger Fahrzeugbau (FFG). Some of these vehicles will be armed with the NEMO 120mm mortar.
	
 In July 2025, Denmark ordered 129 6x6 armoured vehicles from the group for approximately DKK 1.9 billion 
(€255 million). The first unit was delivered in September 2025.
	
 Patria and its partner Japan Steel Works (JSW) delivered the first AMVXP 8x8 armoured vehicle to the Japan 
Self-Defense Forces in September 2025. Another 81 vehicles are on order (out of a target acquisition of 
810 units) and will be assembled locally by JSW with technical assistance from Patria.
	
 In September 2025, Norway and the United Kingdom joined the Patria-led 6x6 Common Armoured Vehicle 
System programme, becoming the sixth and seventh participating nations, alongside Finland, Germany, 
Denmark, Latvia and Sweden. Vehicles for the United Kingdom will be assembled locally by Babcock.
Technology and Innovation
	
 The group’s self-financed R&D expenditure amounted to €15 million in 2024, representing 1.9% of revenues.
	
 At the DSEI exhibition in London in September 2025, Patria presented the Trackx all-terrain tracked armoured 
personnel carrier, developed as part of the European FAMOUS (Future Highly Mobile Augmented Armoured 
Systems) programme led by the group. The vehicle could go into series production from 2027.

PATRIA

168
POLSKA GRUPA ZBROJENIOWA (PGZ)
Business trends
	
 PGZ revenues reached PLN 13.94 billion (€3,237 million) in 2024, 72% of which in defence activities. Growth 
was driven by contracts won in recent years with the Ministry of National Defence of Poland, while export  sales 
grew at a slower pace. In 2025, PGZ aims to achieve sales of more than PLN 20 billion (€4.6 billion), thanks in 
particular to the expected rise in Polish military spending (4.5% of GDP in 2025).
	
 Under an agreement signed in 2024, the subsidiary Mesko and Eurenco announced in August 2025 the launch 
of a production line for 155mm modular charges in Poland, in Pionki (with a target of 100,000 units per year). 
Eurenco is supplying the technology and modular charge kit, while Mesko is responsible for local assembly 
using its own powders.
	
 The group entered into a strategic partnership with BAE Systems in September 2025 to establish a new 
155mm calibre ammunition plant in Poland by 2027-2028, which will be run by the Dezamet subsidiary. 
BAE Systems will provide technology transfers to PGZ.
Home market
	
 PGZ remains highly dependent on its domestic market (more than 80% of revenues). The group is a key partner 
for foreign players wishing to enter the Polish defence market.
	
 In December 2024, the Polish Army awarded the Rosomak subsidiary a contract worth PLN 4.3 billion 
(€947 million) for the supply of 80 KTO Rosomak-L 8x8 wheeled armoured vehicles, equipped with the ZSSW-30 
remote controlled weapon station from the Huta Stalowa Wola subsidiary, to be delivered in 2027-2028.
	
 Huta Stalowa Wola won a PLN 9 billion (€2 billion) contract in December 2024 from the Polish Army for the 
production by 2029 of 96 Krab 155mm self-propelled tracked howitzers, including 48 units in an upgraded 
version featuring a 360° observation system and a remote controlled weapon station armed with a 12.7mm 
calibre machine gun.
	
 In December 2024, Huta Stalowa Wola and Rosomak also signed a PLN 8 billion (€1.8 billion) contract with the 
Ministry of National Defence of Poland for the supply of more than 250 support vehicles for the K9A1 Thunder 
self-propelled howitzers (Hanwha) and training services. 
	
 In March 2025, the Polish Army ordered 111 Borsuk armoured infantry fighting vehicles from Huta Stalowa 
Wola for PLN 6.5 billion (€1.5 billion), to be delivered by 2029. Approximately 1,400 vehicles are to be procured, 
including more than 300 auxiliary vehicles. The five prototypes already delivered also entered service.
	
 The PGZ Stocznia Wojenna subsidiary began steel cutting for the second of three future Wicher-class frigates 
at the Gdynia shipyard in May 2025. The ship is expected to enter service in 2030.
	
 The Polish Armament Agency awarded a PLN 5.8 billion (€1.3 billion) contract in September 2025 to the PGZ-
NAREW consortium, which comprises 10 PGZ companies, to supply up to 46 passive radars for the Narew 
short-range air defence system by 2038.
Export markets
	
 The group generates less than 20% of its revenues from exports. Ukraine, South America, the Middle East and 
Southeast Asia are the main target markets for PGZ.	
	
 In March 2025, the Swedish Defence Materiel Administration (FMV) ordered an unspecified number of Piorun 
portable surface-to-air missiles from Mesko for SEK 3 billion (€262 million). Deliveries will begin in the first 
quarter of 2026.
Technology and Innovation
	
 PGZ is involved in the development of two future armoured vehicles: the NKTO wheeled personnel carrier (by 
Rosomak) and the CBWP tracked heavy IFV (by Huta Stalowa Wola), some of which are equipped with the 
ZSSW-30 remote controlled weapon station.

171
RAFAEL ADVANCED DEFENSE SYSTEMS
Business trends
	
 Rafael Advanced Defense Systems (Rafael) posted revenues of $4,820 million (4,455 million) in 2024, up 
26.8% year-on-year in US dollars. Growth was mainly driven by air defence systems, Spike missiles and 
armoured vehicle protection systems. New order intake amounted to $8,230 million (€7,606 million) during the 
year, allowing the order book to reach a record level of $17.8 billion (€17.1 billion), or nearly four years of sales.
	
 In February 2025, Rafael and Kratos created a 50-50 joint venture called Prometheus Energetics to produce 
solid propellant propulsion systems for the U.S. Armed Forces from 2027 onwards. The plant will be based in 
Crane, Indiana.
	
 Rafael and General Atomics signed a letter of intent in April 2025 to produce the Bullseye long-range precision-
guided missile, derived from the group’s Ice Breaker, for the U.S. Armed Forces. The missile will be assembled 
by General Atomics in the United States, while Rafael will contribute its expertise in aerodynamics, engines, 
guidance systems, integration and testing.
	
 Rafael and Hyundai Rotem reached an agreement in September 2025 to produce and integrate the Trophy 
Active Protection System (APS) on the South Korean group’s K2 main battle tank (including the K2PL version 
intended for the Polish Army).
Home market
	
 A key partner of the Israel Defense Forces, Rafael is the third-largest Israeli defence company, behind Elbit 
Systems and Israel Aerospace Industries (IAI).
	
 In January 2025, the Ministry of Defense of Israel ordered air defence systems from Rafael worth $5.2 billion 
(€4.8 billion). The group will produce Tamir interceptor missiles for the Iron Dome system with ELTA Systems 
(a subsidiary of IAI) and mPrest Systems (a 50% subsidiary of Rafael) as subcontracting partners, as well as 
unspecified components for the David’s Sling and Iron Beam systems.
	
 In September 2025, the Ministry of Defense of Israel and Rafael announced that development of the Iron Beam 
laser air defence system had been completed and that deliveries would begin in late 2025-early 2026.
Export markets
	
 Export sales accounted for 46% of total revenues in 2024.
	
 In February 2025, the German government finalised a long-term framework agreement with the subsidiary 
Dynamit Nobel Defence for the production of RGW 90 portable rocket launchers and their cartridges, as well 
as cartridges for Panzerfaust 3 portable rocket launchers.
	
 In July 2025, the Ministry of National Defence of Romania ordered six Spyder short-range air defence systems 
from Rafael for $2.3 billion (€2.1 billion), the largest export contract won by the group. Two systems are to be 
delivered by 2028 and the other four by 2032.		
	
 In August 2025, the German Bundestag approved the order for 90 Litening 5 targeting pods from the group, to 
be installed on the German Air Force’s Typhoon fighter aircraft (Eurofighter).
Technology and Innovation
	
 Self- financed R&D expenditure represented approximately 8% of revenues in 2024.
	
 The group and the U.S. Army signed an R&D agreement in March 2025 to develop an American version of the 
Spike SR, Spike ER and Spike LR2 anti-tank missiles, as well as the Spike Firefly loitering munition.
	
 The EuroTrophy consortium, comprising KNDS Deutschland and Rafael, announced in September 2025 that 
it had successfully integrated the Trophy Active Protection System (APS) into an 8x8 Boxer wheeled armoured 
vehicle.

174
RHEINMETALL
Business trends
	
 Rheinmetall revenues reached €9,751 million in 2024, up 35.9% year-on-year. Of the three divisions 
encompassing the group’s defence activities (78% of total sales in 2024), Weapon & Ammunition recorded 
the strongest growth (+58.5%), thanks to a sharp increase in ammunition deliveries to Germany, other NATO 
countries and Ukraine. Revenues in the Vehicle Systems and Electronic Solutions divisions grew by 45.3% and 
31% respectively year-on-year.
	
 In April 2025, KNDS Deutschland, KNDS France, Rheinmetall and Thales established a temporary consortium, 
each with a 25% stake, to develop the future MGCS (Main Ground Combat System) main battle tank, which is 
expected to be operational in 2045. Known as MGCS Project Company, this consortium is based in Cologne 
(Germany).
	
 At the Paris Air Show in June 2025, Rheinmetall entered into a strategic partnership to produce Anduril’s 
Barracuda cruise missiles and Fury UCAVs at its own factories.
	
 In September 2025, Rheinmetall reached an agreement with Lürssen Group to acquire the NVL Group military 
shipyard, which is involved in the construction of the German Navy’s future F126 frigates. Completion of the 
transaction is expected in early 2026.
Home market
	
 Germany is an important market for Rheinmetall, accounting for 30% of total sales in 2024, and 34% for defence 
activities.
	
 In January 2025, the Bundeswehr signed a 10-year, multi-billion euro framework agreement with Rheinmetall to 
implement the Tactical Wide Area Network for Land Based Operations (TaWAN LBO) mobile communication 
system, which will be mounted on the Piranha 5 8x8 wheeled armoured vehicle.
	
 In February 2025, the group signed a €3.1 billion framework contract with Germany’s Federal Office of 
Bundeswehr Equipment (BAAINBw) to continue supplying IdZ-ES VJTF 2023 soldier equipment until 2030 (up 
to 368 systems, each capable of equipping 34 soldiers).
	
 In August 2025, the Bundeswehr awarded the Rheinmetall MAN Military Vehicles subsidiary a 
€770 million contract to supply 963 logistic trucks, including in a version equipped with armoured cabs, and 
425 unprotected transport vehicles.
Export markets
	
 Rheinmetall has a strong international presence (70% of total sales and 66% of military sales in 2024).
	
 In July 2025, the Swedish Army awarded a contract worth over SEK 4 billion (€350 million) to the Rheinmetall 
Denel Munition subsidiary for the production of 155mm artillery projectiles from the Assegai family for Archer 
self-propelled howitzers, to be delivered by 2027.
	
 In August 2025, Rheinmetall signed a €250 million contract with the Czech Land Forces for the maintenance of 
Leopard 2A4 MBTs (KNDS Deutschland) and Büffel 3 recovery tanks (produced by the group).
	
 Rheinmetall signed a contract worth several hundred million euros in October 2025 to supply Ukraine with 
Skyranger 35 air defence systems. These vehicles are based on the chassis of a Leopard 1 tank (KNDS 
Deutschland) and armed with the group’s 35mm KDG 35/1000 cannon.
Technology and Innovation
	
 The group’s total R&D expenditure amounted to €502 million in 2024 (5.1% of revenues), of which €312 million 
from defence activities (3.2%).
	
 Rheinmetall delivered a Boxer Skyranger 30 8x8 wheeled armoured vehicle dedicated to short-range air 
defence to the Bundeswehr for testing in January 2025. Series deliveries will begin in 2027.

177
ROLLS-ROYCE
Business trends
	
 Rolls-Royce revenues grew 15.8% in local currency in 2024, to £17.8 billion (€21.1 billion). The Civil Aerospace 
division (+23%) benefited from increased engine deliveries (529 units compared with 458 in 2023) and higher 
demand for maintenance services. Growth in the Defence division (+10.9%) was driven by the fighter aircraft 
segment, with the GCAP programme in the UK and the ramp-up of the F130 programme in the U.S. (future 
engine for the B-52 bomber), as well as submarine activities. The Power Systems division also recorded an 
increase in revenues (+7.6%).
	
 As part of its new financial objectives presented in February 2025, the group plans to reach an operating profit 
of £3.6 to £3.9 billion by 2028 and an operating margin of 15% to 17% (14% to 16% for the Defence division).
	
 In July 2025, Rolls-Royce completed the sale of its marine propulsion activities (propellers, water jets) located 
in Pascagoula and Walpole (United States) to the American group Fairbanks Morse Defence (FMD). The sale 
of the Mission Bay Handling System (MBHS) business located in Peterborough (Canada), also to FMD, is 
expected to take place in the coming months.
Home market
	
 Rolls-Royce generated only 14% of its revenues in its domestic market in 2024. However, the group is the 
leader in the naval engines and turbines segment in the UK, and has a monopoly position in submarine nuclear 
propulsion.	
 
	
 Rolls-Royce is also responsible for developing the engine for the future GCAP (Global Combat Air Programme, 
known as Tempest in the UK) fighter aircraft, as part of the programme led by the UK, Italy and Japan. 
The group is working on an engine demonstrator with Italy’s Avio Aero and Japan’s IHI Corporation.
	
 In December 2024, Rolls-Royce signed an £9 billion (€10.6 billion) eight-year contract with the UK Ministry of 
Defence (the «Unity» contract) to provide support services for the Royal Navy’s nuclear submarines currently 
in service (Vanguard SSBNs and Astute SSNs) and under construction (Dreadnought SSBNs), as well as for 
the Royal Australian Navy’s future SSN-AUKUS-class submarines.
Export markets
	
 The U.S., which accounted for 29% of revenues in 2024, remains the group’s largest market. Rolls-Royce is a 
major supplier of propulsion systems for military aircraft and ships to the U.S. Department of Defense.
	
 In the United States, the Defense Security Cooperation Agency (DSCA) awarded Rolls-Royce a $180 million 
(€166 million) contract in May 2025 to produce mtu 8V 199 TE21-D diesel engines for the Israel Defense 
Forces’ Eitan armoured personnel carriers (Oshkosh Corporation and Plasan).
	
 In May 2025, South Korea’s Defense Acquisition Program Administration (DAPA) selected Rolls-Royce’s MT30 
gas turbine to power its future FFX-IV frigates. Hanwha Ocean will build the first two frigates in the programme 
in 2028-2029 (one turbine per ship). The MT30 turbine already powers South Korean Chungnam-class and 
Daegu-class frigates. Rolls-Royce also supplies mtu 12V 4000 M43 diesel engines (four units per ship), which 
will be assembled locally under licence by STX Engine.
Technology and Innovation
	
 Self-financed R&D expenditure amounted to £745 million (€880 million) in 2024, representing 4.2% of revenues.
	
 In December 2024, the group announced that it had completed the critical design review phase for its F130 
turbojet engine, which will be used to re-engine the U.S. Air Force’s B-52H Stratofortress strategic bomber 
(Boeing). 650 engines (including 42 in spare parts) were ordered for the 76 aircraft still in service. Following 
this modernisation, the aircraft will be designated the B-52J Stratofortress.

180
RTX
Business trends
	
 RTX revenues reached $80.7 billion (€74.6 billion) in 2024, 54% of which from defence activities, mainly 
through its Raytheon division. The group is the world’s second-largest defence company by revenues, behind 
Lockheed Martin, and the fourth-largest aerospace company (civil and military) through its Collins Aerospace 
and Pratt & Whitney divisions, behind Boeing, Airbus and AVIC.
	
 In July 2025, the group completed the sale to Safran of Collins Aerospace’s activities in the production and 
maintenance of flight control and actuation systems for aircraft (civil and military) and helicopters for $1.8 billion 
(€1.7 billion).
	
 Raytheon signed a letter of intent with Diehl Defence in August 2025 for the co-production in Europe of 
components for the FIM-92 Stinger air defence missile.
Home market
	
 RTX generated 57% of its revenues on the U.S. market in 2024. The group is a preferred partner of the 
U.S. Department of Defense, alongside Lockheed Martin and Boeing.
	
 In February 2025, Boeing awarded Collins Aerospace a contract for the supply of 144 ACES 5 ejection seats 
to equip the U.S. fleet of F-15EX Eagle II fighter aircraft.
	
 Raytheon signed a $580 million (€536 million) contract in May 2025 with the U.S. Navy to continue production 
of the new NGJ-MB (Next Generation Jammer Mid-Band) suite for the U.S. Navy and Royal Australian Air 
Force’s EA-18G Growler electronic warfare aircraft (Boeing).
	
 In June 2025, Raytheon won a $1.1 billion (€1 billion) contract from the U.S. Navy to produce AIM-9X Sidewinder 
Block II air-to-air missiles. 2,500 missiles will be produced per year under this contract.
	
 In August 2025, the U.S. Navy awarded Pratt & Whitney a $2.8 billion (€2.6 billion) contract to produce 
141 F135 turbojet engines for the F-35 fighter aircraft in batch 18, to be delivered by February 2028.
	
 Raytheon received $205 million (€189 million) in September 2025 from the U.S. Navy to continue production of 
the naval Phalanx CIWS (Close-In Weapon System) defence system until 2029.
	
 In September 2025, the U.S. Army awarded Raytheon a $1.7 billion (€1.6 billion) contract to produce nine 
LTAMDS air and missile defence radars for the U.S. Army and Poland (for the Polish Wisla system).
Export markets
	
 Export sales accounted for 43% of total revenues in 2024 and mostly concerned civil activities. In the military 
sector, the group is benefiting from international demand for Patriot and NASAMS air defence and missile 
defence systems (the NASAMS system was developed in partnership with Kongsberg).
	
 In January 2025, Romania ordered additional Patriot missile defence systems (radars, control stations and 
interceptor missiles) worth $946 million (€874 million). The Netherlands also ordered a Patriot system and 
associated equipment, worth $529 million (€489 million), to replace the unit sold to Ukraine.
	
 In June 2025, Raytheon received a $250 million (€231 million) order from Mitsubishi Electric for the supply of 
RIM-162 ESSM Block 2 naval interceptor missile kits and components, which will be assembled locally under 
licence by the Japanese group.
	
 In October 2025, the German Navy selected Raytheon’s AN/SPY-6(V)1 AESA naval air and missile defence 
radar for installation on the eight future F127 frigates.
Technology and Innovation
	
 Self-financed R&D expenditure amounted to $2.9 billion (€2.7 billion) in 2024, representing 3.6% of revenues.
	
 In June 2025, Raytheon and Northrop Grumman conducted tests on the engine of the Next-Generation Short-
Range Interceptor (NGSRI) missile, intended to replace the FIM-92 Stinger in service with the U.S. Army.

183
SAAB 
Business trends
	
 Saab revenues reached SEK 63.8 billion (€5,576 million) in 2024, up 23.5% year-on-year in local currency. 
Growth was driven by increased deliveries, in particular of CB90 NG fast assault craft; AT4, Carl-Gustaf M4 
and NLAW man-portable weapon systems; and 340 Erieye AEW&C aircraft (two second-hand units delivered 
to Poland). With the exception of the predominantly civil Combitech division, defence activities accounted for 
more than 90% of sales in all divisions (92% for the group as a whole in 2024).
	
 In March 2025, Saab signed a letter of intent with the Ukrainian company Radionix to develop Ukraine’s 
expertise in sensors and defence electronics.
Home market
	
 Saab generated 41% of its revenues in Sweden in 2024. The group is the preferred supplier of the Swedish 
Defence Materiel Administration (FMV), particularly for combat aircraft and naval defence, and is to deliver 
sixty JAS 39 Gripen E aircraft (one aircraft was delivered in October 2025) and two Blekinge-class (A26) 
submarines.
	
 The group received SEK 1.6 billion (€140 million) in May 2025 from the FMV to integrate MBDA UK’s Sea Ceptor 
air defence system on the Swedish Navy’s five Visby-class corvettes from early 2026.
	
 The FMV awarded Saab a SEK 1.5 billion (€131 million) contract in June 2025 for the supply of RBS 70 NG 
short-range air defence systems (including Bolide missiles), to be delivered in 2027-2028. These systems are 
intended for integration into military vehicles.
	
 In June 2025, the FMV also ordered 22 CB90 NG fast assault craft from Saab for SEK 900 million (€79 million) 
to replace the vessels sold to Ukraine (around 30 since early 2024).
	
 Saab received several orders in 2025 from the FMV relating to the JAS 39 Gripen fighter aircraft programme: 
a SEK 2.9 billion (€254 million) contract in June for the supply of equipment for existing Gripen C/D aircraft 
and future Gripen E aircraft, and a SEK 4 billion (€350 million) contract in October to continue support and 
maintenance of aircraft already in service in 2026-2027.
	
 In October 2025, the FMV awarded Saab a SEK 9.6 billion (€840 million) contract to continue construction 
of two future Blekinge (A26) conventionally-powered attack submarines, scheduled for delivery in 2031 and 
2033 respectively.
Export markets
	
 Saab’s export sales amounted to SEK 37.6 billion (€3,290 million) in 2024, representing 59% of total sales.
	
 In May 2025, Saab began deliveries to Airbus of its Arexis electronic warfare suite, which will be installed on the 
German Air Force’s 15 Eurofighter Typhoon EK electronic warfare aircraft.
	
 In July 2025, the Ministry of Defence of the Czech Republic awarded Saab a SEK 1.8 billion (€157 million) 
contract for the supply of RBS 70 NG short-range air defence systems and Bolide missiles. These systems will 
be integrated into MARS S-330 4x4 wheeled vehicles from SVOS.
	
 The group won three export contracts in 2025 for its JAS 39 Gripen E/F fighter aircraft: in Peru for 24 units worth 
$3.5 billion (€3.2 billion) in July, in Thailand for four units (three Gripen E and one Gripen F) worth SEK 5.3 billion 
(€464 million) in August, and in Colombia for 18 units worth $1.9 billion (€1.8 billion) in October.
Technology and Innovation
	
 Total R&D expenditure amounted to SEK 10.5 billion (€921 million) in 2024, representing 16.5% of revenues. 
Self-financed R&D expenditure accounted for 4.2% of group revenues.
	
 In August 2025, Saab signed a contract with the FMV to develop a large unmanned underwater vehicle for the 
Swedish Navy, which is expected to begin sea trials in 2026.

186
SABENA TECHNICS
Business trends
	
 Originally part of the TAT Group, founded in 1968, Sabena technics provides aircraft maintenance, modification 
and modernisation services for government and commercial customers. The group posted revenues of around 
€700 million in 2024 and employs 4,000 people. In France, its main facilities for military aircraft maintenance, 
repair and overhaul (MRO) are located in Bordeaux, Nîmes, Perpignan, Marseille, Istres and Dinard.
	
 Since 2019, the group has been majority-controlled by a consortium of investors led by the Sagard, Bpifrance 
and TowerBrook investment firms, with the TAT group (Marchais family) and management holding minority 
stakes.
	
 Since 2020, Sabena technics has been strengthening its position in the defence sector with the acquisitions of 
the French companies AeroTech Pro (now Sabena technics ATP), which works on the A400M and A330 MRTT 
aircraft, as well as Aeromecanic (now Sabena technics MRS) and Héli-Union, which provide MRO services for 
military helicopters.	
	
 With a view to offering higher value-added services, Sabena technics acquired the French company Action 
Air Environnement (AAE) in 2024, taking over its fleet of around fifteen ISR aircraft and associated personnel 
(35 people: pilots, ground operators, etc.). These aircraft are offered to government and private customers 
for aerial surveillance missions. Renamed Sabena technics ISR, AAE is based at six locations in France and 
Africa.
Home market
	
 Sabena technics provides MRO and modernisation services for various aircraft and helicopters in service with 
the French Armed Forces and supplies associated services (logistics, pilot training, etc.). The group is also 
involved in aircraft conversion (including Airbus A330s into tanker aircraft).
	
 Sabena technics and Thales were selected at the end of 2022 to modernise the cockpits of the French Air 
Force’s 27 CN235 tactical transport aircraft, whose service life will be extended until the 2040s. Sabena 
technics is responsible for the mechanical and electrical modifications, as well as the integration of the avionics 
and inertial navigation systems supplied by Thales. The modernisation of the fleet (19 CN235-200s and 
8 CN235-300s) is scheduled to take place between 2026 and 2031.
	
 In July 2024, Sabena technics signed a contract with Airbus Defence and Space to provide maintenance and 
support services for the French Air Force’s fleet of A330 MRTT aircraft for 12 years. The group will act as a 
subcontractor to Airbus D&S at the Istres air base (in southeastern France).
	
 As part of France’s acquisition of two GlobalEye AEW&C aircraft (+2 optional), Sabena technics and Saab 
signed a framework agreement in June 2025 for the modification of the aircraft (based on Bombardier’s Global 
6000/6500 jet) and the provision of related services. The work will be carried out in Bordeaux (in southwestern 
France).
Export markets
	
 The group generates around half of its turnover from exports and has more than 500 customers worldwide.
	
 Sabena technics, already responsible for the MRO of the French Air Force’s C-135 and KC-135 tanker aircraft 
(Boeing), signed a contract in October 2024 with the American group Metrea to continue maintaining these 
aircraft. Metrea acquired 14 aircraft withdrawn from service in France (11 C-135s and 3 KC-135s) to provide 
in-flight refuelling services for military aircraft, particularly in the United States and India.
Technology and Innovation
	
 Sabena technics has a design office and is investing in the modernisation of its facilities, in particular to digitise 
maintenance processes (digitisation of all information, monitoring of operations on tablets, use of virtual reality, 
etc.).

189
SAFRAN
Business trends
	
 Safran adjusted revenues reached €27.3 billion in 2024, up 17.8% year-on-year. In the civil sector, growth was 
driven by services, while deliveries of LEAP engines fell by more than 10% year-on-year (1,407 engines in 
2024, compared with 1,570 in 2023). Defence activities also grew thanks to guidance systems, optronics and 
embedded systems. However, deliveries of M88 turbojet engines for Dassault Aviation’s Rafale fighter aircraft 
declined slightly (40 units in 2024, compared with 42 in 2023).	
	
 In February 2025, Safran inaugurated a production site for electronic cards and aeronautical and defence 
computers in Bangalore, India, including an electronics R&D centre. The plant will be operational in the first half 
of 2026 and will reach an annual production capacity of around 30,000 units and several hundred references 
by 2030.
	
 In June 2025, Avio Aero (a subsidiary of GE Aerospace) joined Safran and MTU Aero Engines  in the EURA 
(European Military Rotorcraft Engine Alliance) joint venture, which is responsible for developing the ENGHE 
propulsion system for future heavy military helicopters, scheduled to enter service by 2040. The workload will 
be divided equally between the three operators, but Avio Aero will not acquire a stake in EURA.
	
 In July 2025, Safran completed the acquisition of the activities of Collins Aerospace (part of RTX) in the 
production and maintenance of flight control and actuation systems for aircraft (civil and military) and 
helicopters for $1.8 billion (€1.7 billion). These activities employ approximately 4,000 people at eight main sites 
(in Saint-Ouen-l’Aumône and Vernon in France, Italy, the United Kingdom and Indonesia) and generate annual 
revenues of $1.55 billion (€1.43 billion).
Home market
	
 Safran indirectly benefits from several orders placed since 2020 as part of various French defence 
programmes, including 54 Rafale fighter aircraft for the French Air Force, 12 Falcon Albatros aircraft for the 
French Navy, 18 NH90 helicopters for the French Special Forces, and 356 Griffon VBMR and 88 Jaguar EBRC 
vehicles for the French Army.
	
 In November 2024, the group was selected to supply a new multi-mission parachute (PMM) to the French 
Armed Forces, as well as related services.	
	
 Airbus awarded a contract to Safran in June 2025 for the production of TT&C (tracking, telemetry and 
command) systems from its Syrlinks brand for the 100 satellites in Eutelsat’s low-orbit constellation.
Export markets
	
 Safran generated 81% of its revenue from exports in 2024, in particular through CFM International (its joint 
venture with GE Aerospace, specialising in passenger aircraft engines).
	
 In January 2025, Germany’s Federal Office of Bundeswehr Equipment (BAAINBw) ordered the latest 
generation of portable multifunction infrared binoculars from Safran.
	
 In February 2025, the group signed an agreement with the Finnish Defence Forces to supply Geonyx 
advanced inertial navigation systems for various Finnish artillery systems until 2031.
	
 In June 2025, Kongsberg renews its long-term partnership with Safran for the supply of several hundred T-40 
turbojets for the NSM anti-ship missile.
Technology and Innovation
	
 The group’s self-financed R&D expenditure amounted to €1,348 million in 2024, representing 4.9% of revenues.
	
 At the Paris Air Show in June 2025, the group announced that it was beginning development of a new version 
of the M88 turbojet engine for the future Rafale standard F5 fighter aircraft, the M88 T-REX, which will offer 
increased thrust of 9 tonnes with afterburner.

192
Business trends
	
 Sopra Steria revenues reached €5,777 million in 2024, up 5.6% year-on-year. The Aeronautics, Space, 
Defence & Security vertical market accounted for 22% of revenue.
	
 In January 2025, Sopra Steria participated in the €100 million Series B funding round of French start-up 
Alice & Bob, which specialises in quantum computing.
	
 In May 2025, Sopra Steria completed the acquisition of Aurexia, a French consulting firm specialising in 
financial services. The group aims to develop its consulting activities so that this business represents 12% of 
its revenue in 2028 (compared to 9% in 2024). Aurexia posted revenues of nearly €20 million in 2024 (with  80% 
of turnover generated in France), and has 140 consultants.
	
 In December 2025, Sopra Steria completed the acquisition of the French company Neocase, in order 
to strengthen its range of digital solutions for human resources. Already a partner of the group, Neocase 
generated revenues of €16 million in 2024 and employs around 100 people.
Home market
	
 France is the group’s largest market (47% of revenues in 2024).	
	
 Sopra Steria has been working since February 2021 with the French defence procurement agency (DGA) and 
the French Aerospace Maintenance Agency (DMAé, Direction de la Maintenance Aéronautique) to implement 
a new single information system for aerospace MRO and in-service support, called Brasidas. This system 
aims to improve the availability of aircraft, helicopters and UAVs in service with the French Armed Forces by 
gathering all the information on maintenance activities of the various aircraft fleets in a single digital tool.
	
 Sopra Steria is one of the 13 French industrial and academic players involved in the Confiance.ai programme 
since July 2021. Led by the SystemX technological research institute and with a €45 million budget over the 
2021-2024 period, this programme aims to ensure the reliability of artificial intelligence algorithms used in 
critical industrial applications in various sectors (defence, aerospace, automotive, energy, maritime, etc.). 
Since completion of the research work at the end of 2024, the members wish to perpetuate and disseminate 
the methodology developed, then industrialise the programme’s results.
	
 Sopra Steria, through its subsidiary CS Group, is involved alongside Thales in the PARADE programme for the 
deployment of protection systems for military sites, major events and civilian infrastructures against micro and 
mini-UAVs weighing between 100 g and 25 kg. Notified in April 2022, the contract is worth €350 million over a 
period of 11 years. The first six systems were deployed during the Paris 2024 Olympic Games.
	
 In June 2025, Sopra Steria signed an agreement with the French Ministry of Armed Forces to support French 
mid-sized companies, SMEs and start-ups in accessing defence markets, in particular through specific 
support.
Export markets
	
 Sopra Steria generated 53% of its revenues from exports in 2024.	
	
	
 The group has a strong presence in the United Kingdom, in particular through its subsidiary Shared Services 
Connected Ltd (SSCL). In April 2025, SSCL received a contract extension worth more than £300 million 
(€351 million) to provide services to the British government for a further three years. The services cover various 
areas (finance/accounting, pension administration, payroll, procurement and contact centre support) and are 
intended for various government agencies.
Technology and Innovation
	
 The group’s self-financed R&D expenditure amounted to €122 million in 2023, representing 2.1% of revenues.
Sopra Steria aims to have next-generation technologies account for more than 60% of its revenue by 2028.

195
ST ENGINEERING
Business trends
	
 ST Engineering revenues increased 11.6% in local currency in 2024, to SGD 11.3 billion (€7,798 million). 
The two main divisions, Defence & Public Security (encompassing most of the group’s defence activities, 
+16.1%) and Commercial Aerospace (+12.3%), recorded the strongest growth. On the other hand, revenues 
in the Urban Solutions & Satcom division (mainly civil-oriented, but also including the group’s space activities) 
grew by only 0.7%.
	
 ST Engineering and Saab Australia partnered in May 2025 to develop scalable and adaptable support systems 
for field hospitals deployed in armed conflicts, peacekeeping missions or humanitarian aid operations.
Home market
	
 The result of the consolidation of Singapore’s defence industry, ST Engineering is the leading supplier to the 
Ministry of Defence of Singapore.
	
 The group’s Hunter tracked armoured fighting vehicle, in its engineering vehicle version (HT-AEV), entered 
service with the Singapore Army in January 2025. 
	
 In May 2025, the Ministry of Defence of Singapore awarded ST Engineering a contract to supply an underwater 
mine countermeasure suite, including unmanned surface and underwater vehicles and a C2 centre. 
The USVs and UUVs will be equipped with mine detection and classification sensors and will transmit 
information securely to the C2 centre. In addition, the UUVs will have mine identification and neutralisation 
systems. Deliveries will begin in 2027.
	
 In April 2025, ST Engineering began steel cutting for the second of six Multi-Role Combat Vessels at the Benoi 
shipyard. The first MRCV was launched in October 2025.
Export markets
	
 The group is struggling to win international contracts. In recent years, it submitted (with local partners) its 
Bronco, Hunter and Terrex land vehicles for the Cold Weather All-Terrain Vehicle (U.S. Army), Mobile Protected 
Firepower (U.S. Army) and Land 400 Phase 2 (Australian Army) programmes respectively, but was not 
selected.
	
 In November 2024, Exail selected ST Engineering’s iDirect Europe subsidiary to produce the electronic 
systems for the unmanned underwater vehicles that will accompany the 12 future City-class mine warfare 
vessels for the Belgian and Dutch navies being built by Kership (a joint venture between Naval Group and 
Piriou).
	
 The group entered into a strategic partnership with Kazakhstan Paramount Engineering in December 2024 for 
the local production, with its technical assistance from the end of 2025, of its Terrex 8x8 wheeled amphibious 
armoured vehicle, which will operate under the name Barys A. The first vehicle was unveiled in April 2025.
	
 The first of four Falaj-3-class OPV 60 offshore patrol vessels, named Altaf, entered service with the United Arab 
Emirates Navy in February 2025. ST Engineering designed the vessels (derived from the Fearless-class) and 
provided technical assistance to ADSB (a subsidiary of EDGE Group), which is building them locally.
Technology and Innovation
	
 Self-financed R&D expenditure amounted to SGD 190 million (€131 million) in 2024, representing 1.7% of 
revenues.
	
 In 2025, the Satellite Communications Centre of Excellence of the iDirect Europe subsidiary, based in 
Sint-Niklaas (Belgium), moved into phase 2 of the European Protected Waveform (EPW) project, led by the 
Belgian Ministry of Defence. This project, funded by the European Union, aims to develop a solution that 
guarantees the security and interoperability of military and government communications, particularly through 
the future European IRIS2 satellite constellation.
