198
TECHNICATOME
Business trends
	
 TechnicAtome revenues reached €633 million in 2024 (+15.1% year-on-year), of which approximately 85% 
from defence activities. The group recorded a profitability level of 14% in 2024 and had an order book of 
€1.8  billion at the end of the year, representing nearly three years of sales.
	
 TechnicAtome is a major player in nuclear marine propulsion for French Navy submarines and aircraft carriers. 
The group is also involved in project management assistance for the infrastructures of the Brest and Toulon 
naval bases. In particular, it conceives the programme to adapt the ports’ basins.
	
 In October 2024, TechnicAtome and Onet Technologies jointly acquired TechnoPlus Industries (TPI), a French 
precision engineering company operating in the military and civil nuclear sectors as well as in aeronautics 
(notably with Airbus). TechnicAtome became the majority shareholder in TPI, owning 70% of shares.
	
 At the end of 2024, TechnicAtome withdrew from the French Nuward small modular reactor (SMR) project, led 
by EDF with the CEA and Naval Group.	
	
	
	
 In March 2025, TechnicAtome and Framatome jointly acquired the French company Segault, owned by the 
Canadian group Velan. Segault (80 employees, €11 million in revenue in 2023) specialises in valves for extreme 
applications, intended for the civil and military nuclear industries. Its products are used in particular in the 
nuclear reactors of French nuclear submarines (SSNs and SSBNs) and the Charles de Gaulle aircraft carrier. 
With this acquisition, worth approximately €170 million, TechnicAtome and Framatome aim to secure their 
supplies.
	
 In June 2025, TechnicAtome inaugurated two buildings at its site in Aix-en-Provence (in southeastern France), 
which employs more than 800 people. Covering an area of more than 4,000 m² and intended for engineering 
teams, the new buildings will be able to accommodate nearly 300 people.
Home market
	
 TechnicAtome is involved in the Barracuda programme for the construction of six new-generation Suffren-
class nuclear-powered attack submarines (SSNs) by 2030. The group is in charge of the design, production 
and maintenance of the nuclear reactors, their support system and their fuel. The submarine named Tourville, 
the third unit in the programme, entered active service in July 2025. The launch of the fourth submarine, named 
De Grasse, is scheduled for 2026.
	
 Construction of the first of the four future third-generation SSBNs (SNLE 3G, in French) began in March 2024 
with the first steel cutting. Naval Group is the prime contractor for the overall construction of the submarines, in 
partnership with TechnicAtome, which will build the nuclear reactors.
	
 In April 2024, Naval Group and TechnicAtome received notifications to begin construction of the nuclear 
reactors for the future French aircraft carrier (PA-NG, in French), scheduled to last until 2029. TechnicAtome 
will supply two new-generation K22 reactors, more powerful and with extended autonomy. Commissioning of 
the PA-NG is currently scheduled for 2038.
	
 TechnicAtome and Vanatome (a company acquired in mid-2024 by Framatome and TechnicAtome) signed 
an agreement in November 2024 to cooperate until 2037 in the field of hydraulic valves (R&D, production and 
maintenance) intended in particular for French third-generation SSBNs and future aircraft carrier (PA-NG).
Technology and Innovation
	
 TechnicAtome participates in the Mecanum collaborative R&D project, which aims to develop digital twins for 
nuclear facilities. Part of the France Relance programme, this project is led by EDF and involves Framatome, 
the CEA, Orano (formerly Areva) and Naval Group.

TELEDYNE TECHNOLOGIES
Business trends
	
 Teledyne revenues remained virtually stable in 2024 at $5.7 billion (€5.2 billion), up 0.6% year-on-year in local 
currency. The Digital Imaging division (-2.3%) recorded lower sales of X-ray products and unmanned ground 
vehicles. The Aerospace and Defense Electronics (+6.9%) and Instrumentation (+4.3%) divisions recorded 
sustained growth, while revenues in the Engineered Systems division remained stable (+0.3%).
	
 In February 2025, Teledyne completed the acquisition of certain businesses of Excelitas Technologies, 
including the UK-based optics segment Qioptiq and the U.S.-based advanced defence electronics business, 
for approximately $710 million (€656 million). Used for space and military applications, these businesses were 
renamed Teledyne Qioptiq and integrated into the Aerospace and Defense Electronics division.
	
 In July 2025, the group acquired the U.S. company Maretron (Octoplex, MPower and MConnect brands), 
which specialises in monitoring and control systems for recreational and commercial vessels. In October 
2025, Teledyne also completed the acquisition of Saab’s TransponderTech subsidiary, renamed Teledyne 
FLIR TransponderTech. Based in Sweden, the company provides communication and navigation solutions for 
maritime, air and space transport, with civil and military applications.
Home market
	
 The group generated 52% of its sales on the U.S. market in 2024. Sales to the U.S. Department of Defense, its 
largest customer, accounted for almost 20% of revenues.
	
 In September 2024, Teledyne Brown Engineering signed a contract with a ceiling value of $476 million 
(€440 million) with NASA to provide Earth observation data and related services. The subsidiary will use its 
MUSES (Multi-User System for Earth Sensing) platform, based on the International Space Station (ISS).
	
 In January 2025, Teledyne FLIR Defense signed a five-year contract worth up to $74.2 million 
(€68.6 million) with the U.S. Coast Guard to modernise more than 125 electro-optical systems (ESS-M) on MH-60 
Jayhawk and MH-65 Dolphin helicopters. In April 2025, the subsidiary also received a four-year, $74.2 million 
(€66.2 million) contract from the U.S. Army to upgrade the CBRN threat detection systems on the M1135 
Stryker armoured vehicle, including the supply of six prototypes and support services.
Export markets
	
 Teledyne generated 48% of its revenues from exports in 2024. The United Kingdom, China, Germany, Japan 
and France are the group’s main foreign markets and accounted for approximately 19% of total revenues. 
Most of the group’s revenues in these markets come from the civil sector.
	
 In February 2025, Germany’s Federal Office of Bundeswehr Equipment (BAAINBw) awarded Teledyne FLIR 
Defense a $15 milion (€14 million) contract to supply Black Hornet 4 nano-drones, spare parts and training 
services to the German Armed Forces.
	
 Teledyne Marine signed a framework agreement with the Swedish Defence Materiel Administration (FMV) 
in February 2025 to deliver Gavia autonomous underwater vehicles to the Swedish Armed Forces for mine 
countermeasures.
	
 Teledyne FLIR Defense won a $7.8 million (€7.2 million) contract from Saudi Arabia’s Middle East Task Company 
(METCO) in March 2025 to supply LVSS (Lightweight Vehicle Surveillance System) mobile surveillance 
systems to a Saudi military entity, along with support and training services.
Technology and Innovation
	
 Self-financed R&D expenditure amounted to $293 million (€270 million) in 2024, representing 5.2% of revenues.
	
 At the DSEI exhibition in London in September 2025, Teledyne FLIR Defense presented the SkyCarrier 
autonomous platform for launching and recovering aerial drones (in particular the group’s SkyRaider and 
SkyRanger UAS) from moving vehicles or ships.

204
TEXTRON
Business trends
	
 Textron revenues remained stable at $13.7 billion (€12.7 billion) in 2024, up 0.1% year-on-year in local currency. 
Only the Bell division recorded a significant increase in revenue (+13.7%, and even +20.4% for its military 
activities), while revenues in the Textron Aviation and Textron Systems divisions remained relatively stable 
(+1.7% and -0.5% respectively). However, revenues in the Industrial division fell by 8.5%.
	
 The group is taking part in several tenders for U.S. Armed Forces programmes, including Advanced 
Reconnaissance Vehicle (ARV) with its Cottonmouth 6x6 amphibious reconnaissance vehicle, in partnership 
with Elbit Systems and competing with General Dynamics; and Unmanned Ground Commercial Robotic 
Vehicle (UGCRV) with its Ripsaw M3, in partnership with FLIR Systems and potentially against General 
Dynamics, McQ and Oshkosh Defense.
	
 Bell invested $632 million (€584 million) in December 2024 to build a factory dedicated to manufacturing 
components for the U.S. Army’s MV-75 future utility tiltrotor. Located in Alliance, Texas, the facility will be 
operational in 2028.
Home market
	
 The U.S. is Textron’s largest market, accounting for 71% of revenues in 2024. The goup is a key partner of 
the U.S. Armed Forces, thanks in particular to its monopoly in tiltrotor military aircraft with the V-22 Osprey 
(produced in partnership with Boeing) and the future MV-75.
	
 Textron Systems signed a $106 million (€98 million) contract in December 2024 with the U.S. Navy to supply 
mine clearance payload delivery systems (PDS). The contract includes the supply of spare parts and 
engineering services.
	
 In September 2025, Textron Systems won two five-year contracts to provide training services: a $555 million 
(€513 million) contract for U.S. Navy and U.S. Marine Corps pilots as part of the Fleet Fighter Jet Services (FJS) 
programme, and a $198 million (€183 million) contract for U.S. Marine Corps F-35 Lightning II fighter aircraft 
(Lockheed Martin) pilots as part of the ADAIR (Adversary Air Services) programme.
Export markets
	
 Textron generated 29% of its revenues from exports in 2024, mainly in Europe and Asia-Pacific.
	
 In December 2024, the Ministry of Defense of Japan selected Beechcraft’s T-6 Texan II as the future trainer 
aircraft for the Japan Air Self-Defense Force. Around 40 aircraft could be ordered under the designation T-6JP.
	
 In January 2025, Beechcraft received an order for seven King Air 260 trainer aircraft from SkyAlyne (a joint 
venture between CAE and KF Aerospace). These aircraft are intended for training Royal Canadian Air Force 
pilots as part of the FAcT (Future Aircrew Training) programme. Deliveries will begin in the first half of 2028.
	
 In June 2025, the Tunisian Air Force ordered 12 412EPX helicopters from Bell (a version developed in 
partnership with Subaru). The aircraft will be used for multirole military and security missions.
Technology and Innovation
	
 Self-financed R&D expenditure amounted to $491 million (€454 million) in 2024, representing 3.6% of revenues.
	
 In January 2025, Textron Systems introduced the Tsunami range of autonomous surface vessels, designed to 
meet the needs of the U.S. Department of Defense and its allies for a versatile fleet of unmanned multi-mission 
vessels.
	
 In April 2025, Textron Systems announced that its XM204 Top Attack anti-vehicle munition had received 
certification from the U.S. Army, enabling large-scale production and delivery to the U.S. Army and its allies.

207
Business trends
	
 Thales revenues reached €20.6 billion in 2024, up 11.7% year-on-year. The Defence division recorded a 13.9% 
increase in revenue, driven by land and air systems. Revenues in the Aerospace division increased by 4.8%, 
thanks to the positive performance of aeronautics activities and sustained demand in the avionics market 
(original equipment and after-sales services). However, the space segment suffered from a decline in demand 
for geostationary telecommunications satellites.
	
 In April 2025, KNDS Deutschland, KNDS France, Rheinmetall and Thales established a temporary consortium, 
each with a 25% stake, to develop the future MGCS (Main Ground Combat System) main battle tank, which is 
expected to be operational in 2045. Known as MGCS Project Company, this consortium is based in Cologne 
(Germany).
	
 Thales and Tawazun Council signed an agreement in May 2025 for the production of advanced Ground Master 
series air surveillance radars in the United Arab Emirates. Operational by 2027, the factory will assemble, test 
and qualify radars to meet the needs of the domestic and export markets.
Home market
	
 Thales is a key industrial partner of Dassault Aviation (Rafale fighter aircraft, future Falcon Archange intelligence 
aircraft, etc.), Naval Group (FDI and FREMM frigates, etc.) and KNDS (Scorpion programme, future MGCS 
main battle tank, etc.).
	
 In March 2025, the French Integrated Structure for the MRO of Land Equipment (SIMMT) notified the ABSOLU 
contract (End-to-end adaptation of operational support and standardised logistics for Thales land systems). 
This 10-year contract brings together some 30 maintenance contracts for Thales land equipment and 
simulation resources for the French Armed Forces.
	
 In early 2025, the group launched series production of the Ground Fire surveillance and air defence radar. 
Designed for the new SAMP/T NG system, the radar has a range of 400 km and 360° panoramic coverage 
with 90° elevation. Eight of these radars will be supplied to the French Armed Forces from 2026 onwards.
Export markets
	
 Thales generated 71% of its revenues from exports in 2024.		
	
 As part of the Franco-British MMCM (Maritime Mine Counter Measures) project, the group delivered the 
first autonomous maritime mine clearance system to the Royal Navy in March 2025. The solution includes 
autonomous, cyber-secure drones equipped with artificial intelligence modules. 
	
 In April 2025, Thales won a contract to upgrade the TACTIS (Tactical Indoor Simulator) training centre for 
armoured vehicles of the Royal Netherlands Army. The contract covers the simulation of more than 20 new 
types of vehicles, including the CV9035NL tracked infantry fighting vehicle and the future Leopard 2 A8 main 
battle tank.	
	
	
	
 Thales won two contracts in spring 2025 to supply Ground Master 400 Alpha long-range air surveillance 
radars: two units to Malaysia and one to Albania.
Technology and Innovation
	
 The group’s self-financed R&D expenditure amounted to €1,274 million in 2024, representing 6.2% of revenues.
	
 Coordinated by Thales and bringing together 26 entities from 13 EU countries and Norway, the FMBTech 
project was launched in April 2025. Funded by the European Defence Fund, this three-year project aims to 
design battle tanks adapted to the new conditions of hybrid warfare.
	
 At the Paris Air Show in June 2025, Thales and KNDS France unveiled the 40mm RAPIDFire Terre cannon, a 
land variant of the RAPIDFire Naval designed specifically for the protection of sensitive sites.

210
THALES ALENIA SPACE
Business trends
	
 Thales Alenia Space (TAS) revenues increased 1.4% in 2024 to reach €2.23 billion. Observation, Exploration 
and Navigation (OEN) and Defence activities performed well, whereas the commercial telecommunications 
market suffered from by weak demand.
	
 In October 2025, Thales Alenia Space inaugurated the Space Smart Factory, a satellite production facility, in 
Rome. Representing an investment of more than €100 million (co-financed by the Italian Space Agency, Thales 
and Leonardo), the facility is expected to be operational by the end of the year and will be able to produce more 
than 100 satellites per year (weighing less than 300 kg). Initial work will focus on the Galileo 2nd Generation 
and Copernicus programmes and the Sicral 3 telecommunications satellites for the Ministry of Defence of Italy.
	
 Airbus, Thales and Leonardo reached an agreement in October 2025 to merge their satellite activities. The aim 
is to create a joint entity comprising Thales Alenia Space and Telespazio (joint ventures between Thales and 
Leonardo) and owned 35% by Airbus, 32.5% by Thales and 32.5% by Leonardo. The transaction is subject to 
approval by the European Commission. It would create Europe’s leading satellite manufacturer, with revenues 
of around €6.5 billion.
Home markets (France, Italy)
	
 In January 2025, TAS signed two contracts with the European Space Agency (ESA), one worth €862 million 
to supply the Lunar Descent Element (LDE) for the Argonaut lunar lander, and the other worth €367 million 
to produce the EnVision satellite, dedicated to the exploration of Venus from 2033 onwards. The group also 
signed an €88 million contract with OHB to develop the payload for the third satellite of the CO2M mission 
(Copernicus programme).
	
 As prime contractor for ESA’s Moonlight programme to create a constellation of satellites around the Moon, 
Telespazio entrusted TAS with the development of the in-orbit navigation system in March 2025. The group will 
supply four satellites and will be responsible for the ground infrastructure for network management and control.
	
 In April 2025, the European Union Agency for the Space Programme (EUSPA) awarded Thales Alenia Space 
a €51 million contract to extend the operational service of the European satellite navigation system EGNOS 
beyond 2028.
	
 Thales Alenia Space is participating in ESA’s LISA (Laser Interferometer Space Antenna) mission, the first 
space observatory dedicated to the study of gravitational waves, consisting of three satellites to be launched 
in 2035. In June 2025, the group signed a €263 million contract with OHB to supply the avionics and satellite 
control software, the telecommunications system and the Drag-Free and Attitude Control System (DFACS).
	
 As part of the Artemis programme, TAS signed a contract with the Italian Space Agency (ASI) in July 2025 to 
develop the Multi-Purpose Habitation (MPH) module, scheduled for launch in 2033.
	
 In September 2025, Thales Alenia Space was awarded a risk mitigation contract worth over €400 million for 
technologies to be used in the payloads of the satellites in the future European IRIS² constellation.
Export markets
	
 In winter 2025, Norwegian operator Space Norway and Japanese operator SKY Perfect JSAT ordered the 
THOR 8 and JSAT-32 telecommunications satellites, respectively, both scheduled for launch in 2027.
	
 In July 2025, LuxGovSat, a joint venture between the Luxembourg government and satellite operator SES, 
ordered a geostationary secure communications satellite, named GovSat-2. Intended for military applications, 
the satellite will be based on Thales Alenia Space’s Spacebus 4000B2 platform.
Technology and Innovation
	
 In October 2025, Thales Alenia Space signed a contract with the ESA to develop a quantum key distribution 
system designed to secure communications. In July 2025, the group also partnered with Singaporean start-up 
SpeQtral, which specialises in quantum satellite communication technologies.

THYSSENKRUPP MARINE SYSTEMS (TKMS)
Business trends
	
 thyssenkrupp Marine Systems (tkMS) posted revenues of €2,118 million during fiscal year 2023-2024 (ended 
September 30th), up 15.6% year-on-year. The division has historically been involved in the design and 
construction of submarines and the design of surface vessels (construction being largely subcontracted). 
Since the integration of Atlas Elektronik in 2017, tkMS is also active in the design and production of naval 
equipment and systems.
	
 thyssenkrupp, which wanted to divest its naval branch, floated 49% of tkMS’ shares on the stock market in 
October 2025. The division, which could thus finance acquisitions, began discussions in November 2025 to 
acquire the German Naval Yards shipyard in Kiel, owned by the French group CMN Naval.
Home market
	
 tkMS generated 26% of its revenues in its domestic market in 2023-2024. The division is notably in charge 
of the German Navy’s future F127 air defence frigate programme, in partnership with NVL Group. Based on 
tkMS’ MEKO A-400 AMD frigate, the ships will be equipped with Lockheed Martin’s Aegis combat system and 
Raytheon’s (RTX) SPY-6 radar. Six to eight frigates are planned, with the first unit expected to enter service in 
2034.
	
 As part of the 212CD programme, Germany ordered four additional diesel-electric submarines in December 
2024 (bringing the total to six units) for nearly €5 billion. Norway, which has ordered four submarines, may also 
acquire two additional units. Deliveries are scheduled to begin in 2029 for Norway and in 2032 for Germany.
	
 In June 2025, Germany’s Federal Office of Bundeswehr Equipment (BAAINBw) awarded tkMS a contract 
worth more than €800 million for the maintenance of six U212A submarines for 10 years and the modernisation 
of the four oldest units (weapon, navigation and command systems), which entered service between 2005 
and 2007.
	
 In October 2025, the BAAINBw notified a contract to upgrade the sonars of the German Navy’s Frankenthal-
class (Type 332) mine-hunting vessels. tkMS will supply its HMS-12M hull-mounted sonar to replace the Atlas 
Elektronik DSQS-11M sonars.
Export markets
	
 In January 2025, tkMS was selected over Navantia for the Indian P-75I programme, worth €5.5 billion, to 
procure six submarines. Based on tkMS’ Type 214 and Type 212CD submarines, the submarines will be built 
in India in partnership with Mazagon Dock Shipbuilders.
	
 tkMS and Fincantieri partnered in April 2025 to offer two U212 NFS submarines to the Philippines, along with 
related services (training, logistical support, etc.).
	
 In May 2025, tkMS received an order from the Defence Science and Technology Agency (DSTA) of Singapore 
for the construction of two additional Type 218SG submarines, which will be produced in Germany. Of the 
first four submarines in the class, two are in service and two are under construction and scheduled to enter 
service in 2028.
	
 In August 2025, Canada shortlisted tkMS and South Korea’s Hanwha Ocean for the Canadian Patrol Submarine 
Project (CPSP), which aims to acquire up to 12 submarines for an estimated €37 billion. Also in the running, 
Naval Group (Blacksword Barracuda), Navantia (S-80 Plus) and Saab (A26) were not selected.
	
 In September 2025, the Danish Defence Acquisition and Logistics Organisation (DALO) selected Atlas 
Elektronik’s towed sonars (over Thales) for the modernisation of the Royal Danish Navy’s two Absalon-class 
frigates.
	
 However, in November 2025, Argentina selected Naval Group (Scorpène Evolved) over tkMS (Type 209 NG) 
for a potential procurement programme for three submarines, worth an estimated €2.3 billion.

216
TURKISH AEROSPACE
Business trends
	
 Turkish Aerospace revenues increased 22.9% in US dollars in 2024 to reach $3,287 million (€3,038 million). 
Defence activites contributed significantly to this growth, particularly on certain programmes for the Turkish 
Armed Forces (Hürjet and Hürkus aircraft; T625 Gökbey, T70 and T129 ATAK helicopters; Aksungur and Anka 
MALE UAVs, etc.). Continued strong demand for aerostructures (particularly from Airbus and Boeing) also 
boosted sales.
	
 Turkish Aerospace has been building on technology skills acquired through programmes under U.S. and 
European licences. The group now offers two nationally designed aircraft, the Hürkus trainer and light attack 
aircraft and the T625 Gökbey utility helicopter. It is also completing the development of two other aircraft, the 
Hürjet advanced trainer and light attack aircraft and the T929 ATAK 2 heavy attack helicopter.
Home market
	
 Turkish Aerospace is a major supplier to the Turkish Armed Forces in the aerospace sector. The group is 
the prime contractor for most of Turkey’s major aeronautical programmes, including the future TF Kaan air 
superiority fighter aircraft. 
 
	
 Turkish Aerospace delivered two additional T625 Gökbey utility helicopters to the Turkish Gendarmerie in 2025, 
in January and August, bringing the total number of aircraft delivered to four. These helicopters are powered 
by two CTS800-4A turboprop engines from LHTEC (a joint venture between Honeywell and Rolls-Royce).
	
 In July 2025, Turkish Aerospace signed a contract with the Defence Industry Agency of Turkey (SSB) to supply 
57 T625 Gökbey helicopters to six Turkish public operators, to be delivered between 2028 and 2031. These 
future helicopters will be powered by two new TS1400 turboprop engines developed locally by the subsidiary 
TUSAS Engine Industries (TEI).
	
 The group is continuing deliveries of T-70 helicopters to six Turkish public operators. 31 units were delivered 
as of September 2025, but production could stop at 39 units if the U.S. administration cancels the production 
licence held by Turkish Aerospace.
Export markets
	
 In terms of exports, Turkish Aerospace is mostly present in Africa and in Asia. The group also supplies 
aerostructures to U.S. and European aircraft manufacturers.
	
 In the first quarter of 2025, Turkish Aerospace delivered the last two of the six T129 ATAK attack helicopters 
ordered in 2021 to the Nigerian Air Force.
	
 After signing an initial contract in 2024, TEI signed a second contract worth $68 million (€63 million) with the 
U.S. Navy in the second quarter of 2025 for the maintenance of LM2500 gas turbine modules (GE Aerospace), 
which power most of the U.S. Navy’s surface vessels.
	
 The group won its first export contract for its future TF Kaan air superiority fighter aircraft, with 48 units ordered 
by Indonesia in July 2025. Several Indonesian operators, such as PT Dirgantara Indonesia and PT Republik 
Aero Dirgantara, will be involved in this acquisition programme.
	
 In October 2025, the Spanish government approved the acquisition of 45 of the group’s Hürjet advanced 
trainer and light attack aircraft for €3.12 billion, to be delivered from 2028. Airbus España will adapt the aircraft 
to the needs of the Spanish Armed Forces, in partnership with 15 groups, including Indra Sistemas and Sener.
Technology and Innovation
	
 In the first half of 2025, Turkish Aerospace improved the capabilities of its Aksungur UCAV, which broke its 
altitude record with a flight of over 12,000 metres using the PD170 turbodiesel engine developed locally by TEI. 
The UCAV can also fire Aselsan’s LGK-81 and LGK-82 guided munitions.

219
UKROBORONPROM
Business trends
	
 UkrOboronProm revenues reached $3,007 million (€2,779 million) in 2024, up 36.2% year-on-year in 
US dollars. Over the year, the group tripled its production volume compared to 2023 and achieved an average 
production capacity utilisation rate of 75%.
	
 In February 2025, UkrOboronProm and Thales agreed to create a joint venture focused on the development 
and support of air defence systems, as well as radar, electronic warfare, tactical communications and optronic 
systems. Named Developing Technological Defence Solutions, this joint venture is based in Kiev.
	
 In June 2025, UkrOboronProm and Kongsberg partnered to integrate Ukrainian short- and medium-range air 
defence systems into the Norwegian group’s NASAMS C2 system.
	
 In September 2025, the group signed a letter of intent with Renk America for the integration of HMPT-800 
hydromechanical transmissions into future Ukrainian-designed armoured infantry fighting vehicles.
Home market
	
 UkrOboronProm is hampered by production facilities that are partly obsolete due to insufficient investment 
and by opaque governance at the group level and within its various subsidiaries (more than a hundred). As an 
improvement measure, in June 2023, the Cabinet of Ministers of Ukraine changed the group’s legal status to 
a joint-stock company with 100% public capital in order to attract international partners. Since then, the group 
has entered into various agreements with European and American operators to jointly produce weapons 
(portable rocket launchers with Rafael) and ammunition (small calibre with Northrop Grumman, large calibre 
with Rheinmetall, etc.).	
	
	
	
 In November 2024, UkrOboronProm began local assembly under licence from the Czech Colt CZ Group of the 
CZ BREN 2 assault rifle for the Ukrainian Armed Forces. The group will also produce ammunition for the rifle 
(5.56mm and 7.62mm calibres) under licence.
	
 In February 2025, the group signed an agreement with Canadian armoured vehicle manufacturer Roshel 
to produce armoured personnel carriers in Ukraine. Roshel will provide UkrOboronProm with the technical 
documentation and components needed to manufacture the vehicles. The Ukrainian Ground Forces already 
use the Canadian group’s Senator 4x4 vehicles (approximately 1,700 units).
	
 In October 2025, the Ukrainian Ground Forces selected Rheinmetall’s Lynx KF 41 as their future main tracked 
armoured infantry fighting vehicle. Rheinmetall Ukrainian Defence Industry, a 51%-49% joint venture between 
Rheinmetall and UkrOboronProm, will oversee the assembly of the vehicles in Ukraine.
Export markets
	
 Historically focused on international markets, the group generated 60% of its revenues from exports in 2020, 
particularly in aeronautics (components and MRO), anti-tank missiles and naval equipment (gas turbines). 
Since Russia’s invasion of Ukraine in February 2022, UkrOboronProm has refocused on its domestic market to 
supply the Ukrainian Armed Forces and has halted its arms exports.
	
 In the land sector, UkrOboronProm has sold T-80UD main battle tanks to Pakistan, BTR-4 Bucephalus 
armoured infantry fighting vehicles to Nigeria, Iraq and Indonesia, as well as BTR-3 armoured personnel 
carriers to 10 countries.
Technology and Innovation
	
 In March 2025, the Ukrainian presidency announced that the range of the group’s R-360 Neptune anti-ship 
missile was extended to 1,000 km and that it is capable of striking land targets. This missile contributed to the 
sinking of the Russian cruiser Moskva in the Black Sea in April 2022.
